## RESOLVED: the momentum-leak witness guarded one spelling of the violation; a quantity-level guard now covers all three demonstrated routes

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-28T23:55:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-29T02:20:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-063
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_ghat_gate.py::test_momentum_invariant_to_ghat_value`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/unified_driver.py::UnifiedColumnDriver._apply_vertical_diffusion`; `docs/model_contract.md`

### Issue
Filed by this loop's own review of 1DMIX-061. That issue's witness asserted on the `ghat=` keyword passed into `solve_diffusion_implicit` for `u_vel`/`v_vel`, which is the right shape for that route but guards one spelling of the violation rather than the invariant. The reviewer had built two other implementations of the same bug — folding `ghat` into the `visc_az` array handed to the momentum solve, and a post-solve counter-gradient correction — and measured that each passes all 68 tests.

### Resolution and justification
The issue asked for a keep-or-close decision first, and Arch recorded one: add the guard rather than document the bound. The invariant is MITgcm-mandated rather than stylistic, real `pkg/kpp` having no momentum application of `ghat` anywhere; a counter-gradient term on momentum injects a spurious non-physical body force; `_apply_vertical_diffusion` has now been edited by three consecutive issues, so a future edit there is not hypothetical; and both falsifying mutants already existed and were measured, making the guard cheap. Judging the existing coverage sufficient would have meant knowingly leaving two demonstrated leak routes undetected to save one bounded test.

`test_momentum_invariant_to_ghat_value` varies **only** `ghat` under `apply_ghat=True` and asserts the momentum fields are bit-identical while the tracer fields genuinely change. Exact equality rather than a tolerance is deliberate: `ghat` does not enter the momentum path at all, so any tolerance would weaken the guarantee without physical justification, and a sub-tolerance leak is precisely what a threshold test would miss. Including the tracer-side change in the same test prevents it passing vacuously on a configuration where `ghat` affects nothing. The property is indifferent to *how* a leak is implemented, which is what makes it route-independent within the driver body.

All three demonstrated routes were built in disposable scratch copies and run: each fails the new guard with an invariant-naming message, and routes two and three still pass the older call-level witness, which confirms they were the previously unguarded ones. Arch independently rebuilt the `visc_az`-folding route — the hard case that passed everything before — and reproduced both halves: the new guard fails under it, the call-level witness still passes. No production code changed; `_apply_vertical_diffusion`, the `KPP_GHAT` gate and the `shared_column_solver.py` flux formula are byte-identical to the pinned candidate.

### Verification and remaining bounds
`final_verification.py run` under `arch`: `EXECUTED PASS`, **122 passed / 3 skipped** in 496.54s — 122 rather than 121, the delta being this guard. `verify.py --suite focused --fresh`: `EXECUTED PASS` under both the implementer's and the reviewer's own identities. Structural suite `EXECUTED PASS`.

Bounds, and they are the interesting part. Asked to build a fourth route nobody else had built, the reviewer contaminated `MixingOutput.visc_az` inside `KPPAdapter.compute_mixing`, upstream of the driver body — moving `visc_az` from `0.0` to `5.837e-3` under nonzero `ghat` — and it escapes **both** momentum witnesses and the **entire 69-test focused suite**. The cause is structural rather than a tolerance problem: both witnesses hand-build `MixingOutput` and call `_apply_vertical_diffusion` directly, so neither exercises the real adapter-to-driver pipeline. That is a further coverage boundary rather than unmet acceptance here, and is filed as **1DMIX-064** with his mutant as its reproducer. The general form is worth stating: a hand-built witness guards the function it calls, not the pipeline that calls it. This thread has now demonstrated that three times in succession — 1DMIX-061, 1DMIX-063 and 1DMIX-064 — each found by asking the reviewer for a route the previous guard's author had not imagined.

### Traceability
Not committed; per project commit policy commits happen only on explicit owner authorization, and all changes remain in the working tree. One iteration, no correction rounds: `bob` `4d0cdd76-b9f6-4567-9529-acf0d6cd0b8f` implemented and demonstrated all three mutants in 384s, `richard` `3d70ac1b-cf32-4337-a28d-fc60883eb7e6` reviewed in 490s and returned `APPROVE` with no must-fix. Candidate `14d9d99f654492a5…`; sealed documentation report `c7af5d52af168408…`. Related: 1DMIX-061 (whose witness this complements and whose review filed this), 1DMIX-058 (where the thread began), 1DMIX-064 (the adapter-boundary gap this review opened).

## RESOLVED: `possible_kpp_bugs_in_mitgcm.md`'s Issue-1 excerpt quoted an algorithm the port had already replaced

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-28T17:05:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-29T01:40:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-062
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/reports/possible_kpp_bugs_in_mitgcm.md`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_scheme_specific.py::compute_bl_mixing`; `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F:1493`

### Issue
The report quotes port source as evidence for what the port does, and the quotation had stopped being what the port does. Line 67 showed a single-expression `wm_one = np.sign(wm_one[0]) * max(...)`, while current source computes `wm_one_mag`, takes an explicit `if wm_one[0] == 0.0` branch and uses `np.copysign`. That is not line drift but a different algorithm — and specifically one that handles the zero case the quoted one-liner got wrong. The direction of the error was the dangerous one: a reader checking the port's Fortran-edge-case fidelity from that snippet would conclude the port **still diverges** at `wm_one[0] == 0`, when the current code had already fixed precisely that. Line 74 was separately stale by a 73-line drift.

### Resolution and justification
The landing commit was traced across the `b68b847` repository reorganization, which defeats a naive `git log -S` because it is a file move, to **`cd4b2bc`** (2026-09-17 09:19:50 -0700, "1D update"). Arch verified that independently rather than accepting it: `cd4b2bc^` contains the exact quoted one-liner `np.sign(wm_one[0]) * max(config.phepsi, abs(wm_one[0]))` and `cd4b2bc` replaces it with the `copysign` form.

The correspondence was checked in real MITgcm source rather than the port's own comments, and Arch confirmed that too: `/Users/ifenty/git_repo_others/MITgcm/pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F:1493` reads `wm(i) = sign(eins,wm(i))*MAX(phepsi,ABS(wm(i)))`. Fortran `SIGN(1,0)` returns `+1` where `np.sign(0)` returns `0`, so the current `copysign` form matches MITgcm and the quoted one-liner did not. The excerpt was retained under a supersession marker in the convention the report already used for its `kpp_parameters.py` excerpt, with the current code, the landing commit and its diff hunk placed beside it. Line 74 was corrected in place (433-440 → 506-513, byte-identical), two stale bare file-path references were fixed, and every remaining code citation was audited.

### Verification and remaining bounds
`final_verification.py run` under `arch`: `EXECUTED PASS`, 121 passed / 3 skipped in 645.09s. `verify.py --suite focused --fresh`: `EXECUTED PASS` under both the implementer's and the reviewer's own identities. No production code changed — `kpp_scheme_specific.py` is byte-identical to the pinned candidate across all three implementer rounds — and `source_signature(scientific=True)` never drifted from its iteration-start value, so unlike 1DMIX-052 no workflow amendment was needed.

The review earned this iteration's cost twice, and both findings were the same defect the issue exists to repair: a claim asserted as verified that was not. Round 0 caught the repair itself stating "This is a direct date comparison from `git log`, not an inference" about the report's own `2026-08-20` header date — when git shows only the 2026-09-17 fix date, and git tracking of the report file begins `2026-09-18` via a pure create-mode add (confirmed `A`, not `R`, with `git show b68b847 -M5% --name-status`), one day **after** the fix, so it cannot corroborate an earlier authorship date at all. Round 1 then caught the retracted claim still standing in the Revision History bullet after the body had been corrected. The implementer swept the whole file for `measured`, `28 days`, `predates`, `git log` and `direct comparison`, reporting the complete hit list including hits already correct, and the reviewer audited the sweep rather than the fix.

Bounds. The report's exact authorship date relative to the fix is now recorded as **undetermined by git**, with what would settle it stated, rather than asserted — the `0820T0946` input-filename timestamp is circumstantial corroboration only. Whether any published validation figure was measured under the divergent behaviour therefore remains undetermined for figures whose own measurement dates are not independently recorded. Structurally, this file lies entirely outside `doc_contract`'s inventoried documentation, so its disposition plan carries **zero** targets and the routine staleness sweep gives it no coverage — the third demonstration of **TEAM-DOC-SWEEP-001**, after 1DMIX-057 recorded it and 1DMIX-052 confirmed it for the directory's primary reports.

### Traceability
Not committed; per project commit policy commits happen only on explicit owner authorization, and all changes remain in the working tree. One iteration, two implementer correction rounds and four reviewer rounds: `bob` `759067c8-8328-45ee-896b-f4064205c16d`, `richard` `df7ed076-6769-4a40-ae27-454ce92bd306`. The reviewer's rounds 0 and 1 are recorded `incomplete` for carrying open must-fix findings, and round 2 for citing a superseded sealed report, which round 3 corrected with no change to the `APPROVE` verdict. Candidate `6a3d50ea5611ab84…`, byte-identical across every round because the edited report lies outside the configured source paths; sealed documentation report `e56f80cabd068a6d6e0adfdbeb4d08eb633283e5ddb3a9698e2c40835653f8a6`. Related: 1DMIX-057 (which surfaced these excerpts and recorded the doc-sweep gap), TEAM-DOC-SWEEP-001.

## RESOLVED: the momentum boundary of KPP's `ghat` term was correct, MITgcm-mandated and guarded by nothing

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-28T16:30:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-29T00:20:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-061
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_ghat_gate.py::test_momentum_solve_never_receives_ghat`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/unified_driver.py::UnifiedColumnDriver._apply_vertical_diffusion`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/shared_column_solver.py::solve_diffusion_implicit`; `docs/model_contract.md`; `pkg/kpp/kpp_transport_t.F`

### Issue
Found by the independent review of 1DMIG-058 as a coverage gap on an invariant that is **correct**. In real MITgcm the non-local coefficient `ghat` enters only through `kpp_transport_t.F`, `kpp_transport_s.F` and `kpp_transport_ptr.F`; KPP's counter-gradient term is a tracer-transport term with no momentum analogue in `pkg/kpp`. The port honours this structurally — `theta` and `salt` share one `ghat_to_apply` while the `u_vel`/`v_vel` calls to `solve_diffusion_implicit` omit the keyword entirely — but that reviewer's momentum-leak mutant passed all 16 tests plausibly able to notice. The gap mattered because `_apply_vertical_diffusion` is exactly where 1DMIX-058 had just moved the `KPP_GHAT` gate, making it the file most likely to be edited next by someone reasoning about where `ghat` belongs.

### Resolution and justification
Added `test_momentum_solve_never_receives_ghat`. It spies on the keyword arguments actually passed into `solve_diffusion_implicit` and fails when `u_vel` or `v_vel` receives a non-local coefficient. The call-level shape is deliberate and is recorded in the test's own docstring: a velocity comparison would need a tolerance, would not name the invariant when it failed, and could be satisfied by a leak small enough to hide beneath that tolerance.

The demonstration was executed, not asserted, and executed twice independently. The implementer built the momentum-leak mutant in a scratch copy, ran the witness against it and reported a failure naming the invariant; Arch then built its own separate mutant in its own scratch tree and reproduced it, the message reading `momentum solve for u_vel was given a non-local coefficient (…); … ghat must never reach the u_vel/v_vel diffusion solve` rather than any numeric mismatch. Both scratch trees were deleted.

No production code was touched. The `ghat` application site, the `KPP_GHAT`/`apply_ghat` gate 1DMIX-058 established, and the flux formula in `shared_column_solver.py` are all unchanged. The constraint is now stated in `docs/model_contract.md`'s KPP section and the witness is routed from `docs/code_map.md`'s KPP core row, so it survives independently of the test — the issue existed precisely because the invariant was load-bearing and written down nowhere.

### Verification and remaining bounds
`final_verification.py run` under `arch`: `EXECUTED PASS`, 121 passed / 3 skipped in 648.47s — 121 rather than the prior 120, the delta being this witness. `verify.py --suite focused --fresh`: `EXECUTED PASS` under both the implementer's and the reviewer's own runtime identities. Structural suite PASS.

The independent review earned its cost by answering what neither demonstration had asked. Instead of re-confirming the settled mutant, the reviewer built two **different** implementations of the same bug — folding the `ghat` contribution into the `visc_az` array handed to the momentum solve, and applying a post-hoc counter-gradient correction to `u_vel`/`v_vel` after the solve returns — and ran the full suite against each: **68 passed, 3 skipped under both, unchanged**. So the witness guards the leak shape it names and not the invariant in general. That residual gap is filed as **1DMIX-063** with both mutants as its reproducers, rather than papered over.

He also established that the handcrafted witness does not diverge from the real pipeline, grep-verifying that `_apply_vertical_diffusion`'s body contains zero `self.`-references so `mixing_adapter=None`/`config_manager=None` cannot change its behaviour, and that the test's `kinematic_fluxes` keys match `_compute_kinematic_surface_fluxes`'s real output. What the synthetic construction cannot observe: whether `KPPAdapter.compute_mixing` wires `ghat`/`apply_ghat` correctly (covered by the sibling tests), and the real multi-step loop.

The correspondence claim was checked against real MITgcm source at `/Users/ifenty/git_repo_others/MITgcm`, not against the port's comments: `KPPghat` is consumed only in the three transport files, `longstep` and `offline` merely relay it to those same consumers, there are zero references in `pkg/mom_common`, `mom_fluxform` or `mom_vecinv`, and no momentum-transport file exists. The statement now written into `model_contract.md` and `code_map.md` is confirmed true and not overstated.

### Traceability
Not committed; per project commit policy commits happen only on explicit owner authorization, and all changes remain in the working tree. One iteration, no correction rounds: `bob` `ea16bddd-7ad1-4736-a345-69ea023bfff4` implemented and demonstrated in 625s, `richard` `511d36d2-0838-4f8f-9b91-9201ebbe2045` reviewed in 605s and returned `APPROVE` with no must-fix. Candidate `b650c03f60812c4279ca1eaf0ec38b10aa035e36eee3689e5816bd385b88898b`; sealed documentation report `214468eaf63035105c2350df9d7f7e703713c9146da8a2cd34ca4481836f35fd`. Both briefs restated the standing incremental-write rule explicitly after it cost a 59-tool-call reviewer turn on 1DMIX-052, and no turn was lost here. Related: 1DMIX-058 (whose review found this gap), 1DMIX-063 (the residual leak-shape gap this review opened).

## RESOLVED: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/`'s own reports read as issue-tracking archaeology rather than a standalone scientific account

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T20:20:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-28T22:35:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-052
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/README.md`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/KPP_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/GGL90_port_validation/GGL90_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`

### Issue
The directory's primary reports were accurate but unreadable for their stated audience. `VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` cited a bare `1DMIX-NNN` issue number 56 times and `README.md` 49 times, and both narrated results as before/after history ("RESOLVED (1DMIX-050)", "used to carry the pre-1DMIX-035 defect", strikethrough) rather than simply stating the current measured number. The owner wanted a report a newcomer with no interest in this project's development history could read start to finish.

### Resolution and justification
The rewrite itself landed under 1DMIX-055 and was not redone here: the combined report became two standalone per-scheme reports, `KPP_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` (614 lines) and `GGL90_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` (452 lines), with `VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` reduced to a 16-line pointer and `README.md` retaining the three-way method description, directory guide and a compact per-experiment status table. This iteration established that the acceptance holds on current bytes and obtained the content review that the structural criteria cannot supply.

The structural criteria were measured three times independently — by Arch, then the implementer, then the reviewer. `grep -c '1DMIX-'` returns 0 in all four documents, and a word-boundary check for `RESOLVED`/`~~`/"used to be"/"now fixed" returns no match in either per-scheme report. The implementer caught a false positive in the naive form of that check ("unresolved" matching a bare `RESOLVED` grep at KPP line 377), so the criterion is confirmed on the corrected regex rather than the loose one.

Content, not structure, is where the iteration earned its cost. The reviewer independently re-derived nine experiment sections from raw captures rather than rerunning the pytest wrappers — KPP `1D_ocean_ice_column`-10 and `global_oce_latlon`, GGL90 `isomip`, `global_ocean.90x40x15` and `vermix` — and every figure matched the reports, including `ghat` `median_abs` 0.001499852465312479 and `max_abs` 115.9702535835539, and `corr=0.998`/243,692 cross-checked against 1DMIX-025's own closed entry. He found one genuine defect, of exactly the kind relocation hides: `GGL90_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` line 159 and `README.md` line 88 both called `vermix` agreement "floating-point roundoff" while the table directly beneath reports max relative errors of 0.073%, 0.219%, 0.243% and 0.307% — eight to thirteen orders of magnitude above every other use of that phrase in these documents, which attach to 1e-11 through 1e-20 values. Both lines now state agreement within the project's 1% clean bar with the measured range, in the reviewer's own words, and he confirmed in a second round that the replacement text is itself true against the table it points at.

### Verification and remaining bounds
The issue was prepared as `kind: documentation` and closed as `scientific_change` under an explicit `workflow_amendment`. `source_signature(scientific=True)` moved from `f4763d088b61be6b355d6db03fbbc768fdeb7d1e116477e4528693c71f492659` at iteration start to `ecc793c0dd1835a8cd3654c6984eb94e7b5ed4e31bc7fcf053a2b5459b1f7fc5`, and the gate requires that drift be closed at the higher bar. The drift is not attributable to this issue: both files touched lie outside the configured source, test and configuration paths, no file in the 233-path scientific inventory has an mtime after the iteration start, the captured candidate is byte-identical at `852e2119…` across the correction round, and the signature is stable on repeated measurement. Its cause was not isolated and is not claimed here — this is the trap **LL-003** already records, where drift forces reclassification for an issue held open across other work even when its own candidate is unchanged. The amendment was honoured rather than argued around: a fresh `final_verification.py run` under `arch` qualified the current candidate against the configured scientific suite. Alongside it, `verify.py --suite focused --fresh`: `EXECUTED PASS` under both the implementer's and the reviewer's own runtime identities, and `pytest` on both named source test files (`test_ggl90_mitgcm_validation.py`, `test_kpp_mitgcm_validation_extended.py`): 46 passed, 0 failed.

1DMIX-055's one explicit open bound is resolved, and resolved cheaply. That closeout had recorded the `lab_sea` 6-month figures as retained rather than re-measured, and 1DMIX-057's `keep_mitgcm_bugs` default flip landed afterwards, so they were potentially stale for a reason nobody had checked. Rather than replay the capture, the reviewer used durable pre-existing evidence — `kpp_parameters.py`'s own flag docstring plus `devel-loop/loop_state/1dmix057-wscale-capture-threeway.txt` — to establish that `lab_sea_6mo_100step` `hbl` is identical to displayed precision under both flag settings (median 2.892e-3 m, max 40.72 m, matching the report). The default therefore does not move these figures, and the staleness caveat at `KPP_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` lines 286-290, which attributes the older full-run figures' staleness to tau-averaging and truncation-instrumentation fixes, is not omitting a larger second reason.

Bounds. No one has ever replayed the full 4368-timestep/655,200-column-timestep `lab_sea_6mo` capture under either flag setting; the report is already honest that its historical full-run figures have not been re-verified, and this review narrows why they are stale without closing that gap. The reviewer checked the primary question plus nine experiment sections rather than every quantitative claim line by line. Separately, and structurally: none of these four reports is inside `doc_contract`'s tracked documentation inventory, so the routine staleness sweep gives them no coverage at all — the same structural gap 1DMIX-057 recorded against **TEAM-DOC-SWEEP-001**, now confirmed to extend to the directory's primary reports.

### Traceability
Not committed; per project commit policy commits happen only on explicit owner authorization, and all changes remain in the working tree — two single-line documentation corrections. Two iterations: iteration 1 produced the rewrite work that closed partial, iteration 2 confirmed it on current bytes and obtained the content review the issue had never had. Candidate `852e21193c67934a2d6f0a25dda1455e1448ef1044e83a459a7a0f0cdf6345b4`, unchanged across the correction round because neither edited file lies inside the configured source paths; sealed documentation report `9029b239873eba074cedc52a59a429fa0d3ee7d622acd5e9727f9ce8a91058a8`. The reviewer's round-0 turn was killed by Arch's own 1700-second dispatch cap after 59 tool calls, leaving a 0-byte report; it was recovered by resuming the same identity with a write-first followup that reused his persisted measurements rather than redoing four capture replays — an instance of **TEAM-DISPATCH-TIMEOUT-001** and of the standing incremental-write rule, which Arch failed to restate in the brief. Related: 1DMIX-055 (the rewrite whose acceptance this confirms), 1DMIX-045 (the combined report it replaced), 1DMIX-057 (whose default flip raised the staleness question resolved here), TEAM-DOC-SWEEP-001.

## RESOLVED: the KPP port defaulted to a corrected `wscale` extrapolation that real MITgcm does not use, so the shipped default diverged from the source it claims to replicate

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-28T05:40:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-28T17:10:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-057
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_parameters.py`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_routines.py::wscale`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests/test_kpp_mitgcm_validation_extended.py`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/reports/possible_kpp_bugs_in_mitgcm.md`; `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F:980`

### Issue
Established by 1DMIX-056: real unmodified MITgcm leaves the `wscale` lookup-table extrapolation unclamped at `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F:980`, and the Sidorenko correction at `:990` is present in that source but commented out. The Python port clamped it, selected by `keep_mitgcm_bugs`, which defaulted to `False`. So the default every user received without opting in selected the *corrected* behaviour rather than the behaviour MITgcm actually has, while this project's documentation stated exact correspondence. Genuinely two-sided: matching MITgcm means reproducing a numerical artifact its own authors wrote a fix for and left disabled.

### Resolution and justification
`keep_mitgcm_bugs` now defaults to `True`. The decision rested on real-capture measurement, not preference. On the `global_oce_latlon` capture the flip cuts `hbl` `max_abs` from 3.390e+01 m to 3.092e+00 m and cells above 1% relative error from 146/11575 to 10/11575; `visc_az` `max_abs` from 3.316e-01 to 9.569e-02; `diff_kz_s/t` from 9.593e-01 to 1.103e-01.

Both full-suite runs, one under each flag setting, gave an identical 113 passed / 3 skipped. No registered test's pass/fail depended on the default — the improvement is visible only at field level, which is why the field-level A/B was the decisive measurement rather than the suite. `wscale` branch entry was instrumented across all four real captures, at 1.2% to 12.9% of evaluation points each.

The flag name is retained deliberately. `True` now means "reproduce MITgcm", which reads oddly against a name promising to keep bugs; the reviewer judged it literally accurate for what `True` does and defensible because the default is explained at every point of contact — the field docstring, `docs/model_contract.md`'s Decision section, and the report's own superseded block — and recommended no rename.

### Verification and remaining bounds
The two `global_oce_latlon` witnesses carry bounds re-derived from the post-flip measurement and strictly tighter than before; none was widened. The first review recomputed every bound from raw capture NetCDF with its own replay script, confirming each is satisfied with real margin — worst case a 0.15 bound against 0.1103 measured, about 36% headroom, none flush against its measurement — confirmed by independent grep and by reading `main/eos.py` that `wscale` is the single site the flag gates, confirmed no other default moved, confirmed 1DMIX-056's witnesses pin the flag per call and are therefore unaffected, and reran at N=40, eight times the test window, where the improvement direction holds and strengthens. The final reviewer independently re-derived the whole A/B himself, monkeypatching the flag both ways and recomputing from the raw capture rather than reusing any prior figure: `True` gives `hbl` `max_abs` 3.0916 m and 10/11575 cells, `False` gives 33.904 m and 146/11575, matching every quoted number to the precision published and confirming the tightened bounds would genuinely fail under the old default.

A prior `Rib`/`Ricr` attribution in the `hbl` test docstring was corrected, for the same reason 1DMIX-056's was wrong: the mechanism is the `wscale` extrapolation, not the Richardson-number criterion.

Three annotations in `possible_kpp_bugs_in_mitgcm.md` were authored by Arch directly, because the implementer's correction allowance was exhausted and the scope deadline had passed — so they entered the record with no reviewer between author and record. That gap is now closed: all three were independently confirmed factually true of current bytes and unambiguously marked historical. Asked additionally to read the section as a first-time reader rather than as a diff, the reviewer found a fourth statement nobody had annotated — line 189's `**Python Port Status**: 🟢 Bug fixed by default`, false of current bytes, carrying no marker, and the *first* thing a reader of that section encountered. It now states that the bug is reproduced by default and points to the superseded block, whose cross-reference both the implementer and the reviewer confirmed resolves. A stale line-number citation at line 259 was corrected from `170-177` to `175-182`, measured independently by the reviewer rather than taken from the report.

Bounds. Two further Issue-1 excerpts in that file remain stale and are filed as **1DMIX-062**: one is a pure 73-line drift, and one is a real content mismatch where the quoted `np.sign(wm_one[0]) * max(...)` no longer matches current source, which uses `np.copysign` and an explicit zero branch. The reviewer bounded its severity: the report's overarching "exactly replicates" prose remains true and only the supporting evidence is stale, so the direction of the error is that a reader would believe the port *still* diverges at `wm_one[0] == 0` when it has since been fixed. Neither crosses the standard line 189 failed, since neither claim was made stale by this issue and both are topically unrelated to `keep_mitgcm_bugs`. Also unexamined: the GGL90-side implications of the same clamp. Separately, this file sits outside `doc_contract`'s tracked inventory entirely, which is the structural reason the staleness escaped the normal sweep — recorded against `TEAM-DOC-SWEEP-001`.

## RESOLVED: `KPP_GHAT` gated computation instead of application, zeroing the non-local coefficient the port should have computed unconditionally

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-28T06:55:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-28T16:35:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-058
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_scheme_specific.py::compute_bl_mixing`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/unified_driver.py::UnifiedColumnDriver._apply_vertical_diffusion`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/mixing_adapter.py::MixingOutput`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_ghat_gate.py`; `pkg/kpp/kpp_transport_t.F`

### Issue
1DMIX-057's field-level A/B measurement exposed a total `ghat` disagreement on the `global_oce_latlon` capture: 394 of 394 active cells above 1% relative error, `max_abs` 1.160e+02, bit-identical under both settings of `keep_mitgcm_bugs` and therefore untouched by the `wscale` clamp that explained that capture's `hbl`/`visc_az`/`diff_kz` behaviour. A separate, pre-existing defect that the 1DMIX-057 measurement revealed rather than caused. The existing `ghat` test passed anyway, so its tolerance could not detect a complete failure of the quantity it named.

### Resolution and justification
A real port defect, root-caused in MITgcm source rather than inferred. `KPP_GHAT` appears in real MITgcm only in `kpp_transport_t.F`, `kpp_transport_s.F` and `kpp_transport_ptr.F`, and never in `kpp_routines.F`. It therefore gates whether the non-local coefficient is **applied** to a tracer's diffusive flux, and never whether `blmix` **computes** it. The port had conflated the two, zeroing `ghat` inside the computation whenever `use_ghat` was false.

`compute_bl_mixing` now computes the coefficient unconditionally, matching `blmix`. The gate moved to the real application site: `MixingOutput` gained `apply_ghat` as the carrier for the decision from scheme to solver, and `UnifiedColumnDriver._apply_vertical_diffusion` honours it. The flux formula in `main/shared_column_solver.py` is byte-unchanged — the gate sits outside the formula, not inside it — confirmed by hashing that file against the pinned baseline blob rather than against HEAD, since the working tree carries unrelated modifications that make a HEAD diff meaningless.

On the `global_oce_latlon` capture, the only registered capture carrying `use_ghat=0`, `ghat` `median_abs` fell from 8.64 to 1.4999e-3. The other three captures all carry `use_ghat=1` and are byte-unchanged, which is what the fix predicts: it can only change behaviour when the flag is false. The capture's `ghat` bounds were tightened from 15.0/150.0 to 0.01/140.0, re-derived from the post-fix measurement.

### Verification and remaining bounds
`Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_ghat_gate.py` is the registered witness: 3 tests covering 5 invariants. Independent review built five in-process mutants — compute-zero-if-`use_ghat`-False (the original bug), compute-zero-if-True (inverted), adapter-ignores-flag, always-apply, never-apply — and established which test catches which: the compute mutants are caught by `test_ghat_computed_unconditionally` and `test_kpp_adapter_apply_ghat_flag`; adapter-ignores-flag only by `test_kpp_adapter_apply_ghat_flag`; always- and never-apply only by `test_apply_ghat_gate_changes_tracer_evolution`. Every named invariant is guarded by the test meant to guard it, in both directions.

The reviewer re-measured the headline figure from scratch rather than rerunning the test, reproducing `median_abs=0.001499852465312479`, `max_abs=115.9702535835539`, `n_active=394`, `frac_gt1pct=2.538%` — an exact match to the sealed report. He also established that 1DMIX-057's and 1DMIX-059's work, both of which landed in the tree after that measurement was taken, did not move it: the full 25-test extended validation suite passes fresh across all four captures. `audit.py` PASS; `verify.py --suite focused --fresh` EXECUTED PASS under both the implementer's and the reviewer's own runtime identities.

Bounds. The reviewer confirmed by direct source read that `ghat` never reaches momentum — `theta` and `salt` share one `ghat_to_apply` while the `u_vel`/`v_vel` solver calls omit the keyword entirely — which is correct, since `ghat` is a tracer-transport term with no momentum analogue in `pkg/kpp`. But a momentum-leak mutant is caught by **nothing** in the suite (16 tests pass under it). That is a coverage gap on a correct invariant, filed as **1DMIX-061**, not a defect here. The other three captures' "byte-unchanged" claim rests on the structural argument plus the passing suite, not on a fresh numeric remeasurement of those three the way `global_oce_latlon` was remeasured.

### Traceability
Not committed; per project commit policy commits happen only on explicit owner authorization, and all changes remain in the working tree. Two iterations: iteration 1 implemented and validated the fix but closed partial when the implementer's correction rounds were exhausted after Arch wrongly told him to skip a reseal; iteration 2 verified the fix in current bytes with no edits and obtained the independent review the issue had never had bound to a current candidate. Candidate `eb9548c3783d12e4d4c8850a69252ae6e9680139c97e8127258192a29c5ae65e`; sealed documentation report `7b32e34d252fbab0a840f02535a54b9a69fd7aebfcb0bc7b4de1b00774c4d4ab`, confirmed by the reviewer on every checked claim. Related: 1DMIX-056 and 1DMIX-057 established the `wscale` mechanism this defect was distinguished from; 1DMIX-061 carries the momentum-coverage gap this review surfaced.

## RESOLVED: GGL90 had no standalone-Fortran-routine harness — step (2) of the three-way method was unverified for GGL90

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T16:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T16:05:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-024
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/ggl90_standalone_driver/`; `pkg/ggl90/ggl90_calc.F`, `pkg/ggl90/ggl90_mixinglength.F`.

### Issue
KPP had a standalone-subroutine harness (`kpp_standalone_driver/`) giving independent confirmation that the real, isolated Fortran routine agrees with (1) the full model and (3) the Python port, including on inputs no full-model run ever produced (the 6 idealized scenarios). GGL90 had no equivalent: its Python-port validation rested on the full model and the Python port only, never independently confirming that a standalone call to the real `GGL90_CALC` agreed with either.

### Resolution and justification
Built `ggl90_standalone_driver/` (`ggl90_standalone_main.F` + `build_and_run.sh`), mirroring `kpp_standalone_driver/`'s technique but adapted to `GGL90_CALC`'s very different interface: unlike `KPPMIX` (pure-argument), `GGL90_CALC` reads/writes ~15 named `DYNVARS.h`/`FFIELDS.h`/`GRID.h`/`GGL90.h`/`PARAMS.h` fields via COMMON, populated by `#include`-ing the real, unmodified headers and vermix's own real `SIZE.h`. The one missing input, `sigmaR` (GGL90_CALC's one explicit array argument), was added as a new `INPUT_SIGMAR` capture line in `ggl90_calc.F` (confirmed purely additive via direct pre/post netCDF field-by-field diff). For the 6 idealized scenarios (no full-model MDJWF run to bit-match against), `sigmaR` was derived by algebraically inverting each scenario's own already-computed `n_square`, isolating the comparison to `GGL90_CALC`'s downstream physics only.

**Vermix**: standalone driver vs. full-model capture — `visc_az` max_rel=6.5e-16, `diff_kz` max_rel=6.6e-16, `mixing_length` max_rel=9.9e-16, `tke_after` max_rel=1.4e-15, 0/520 mismatches (>1% rel) on all four fields.

**6 idealized scenarios**: the harness worked correctly and surfaced a real, previously-unverified discrepancy between the Python port and real MITgcm for `mxl_max_flag=0` (the project's own default) — the mixing-length depth-limiter ceiling used cell-center span instead of MITgcm's interface-to-interface span. Root-caused, fixed, and closed separately as 1DMIX-030 (not papered over — the acceptance criterion's "concrete, sourced explanation for any residual" branch).

Independently reviewed in this closure's redispatch (the original build/verification work predated this session's `subagents` bookkeeping fix): Bob (agent_id `2db46eed-3489-49f8-92c3-4b1a333025f7`) confirmed the harness exists, correctly includes the real unmodified MITgcm headers, the `INPUT_SIGMAR` addition is genuinely purely-additive, and the documented vermix result is directly reproducible from artifacts still on disk. Richard (agent_id `c781f983-adc5-450c-9b9e-98a82e2b3e8a`) independently reconfirmed the same from scratch, including his own diff of the driver's `#include` headers against the real MITgcm checkout. Both verdict **APPROVE**, no must-fix.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest suite: 56 passed, 3 skipped, unchanged throughout. Structural suite (`verify.py --suite structural --owner arch --fresh`): EXECUTED PASS. `final_verification.py run --review ... --owner arch`: EXECUTED PASS, real receipt (`devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/42178a770c08a3f02327eecfc40607c820224f0236ffd4a4142a04b84dcde7cc.json`). No remaining bounds specific to this issue.

### Traceability

## RESOLVED: GGL90 `mxl_max_flag=0`/`1` depth-limiter ceiling used cell-center span instead of MITgcm's interface-to-interface span

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-20T02:50:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T15:30:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-030
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_scheme_specific.py::GGL90MixingLength._limit_method_0`/`._limit_method_1`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_core_driver.py::GGL90Driver.compute_mixing`; `pkg/ggl90/ggl90_mixinglength.F:163-193`.

### Issue
Found via the standalone-`GGL90_CALC`-driver scenario check: the mixing-length depth limiter (`mxl_max_flag=0`/`1`, the project's own default configuration used by every one of the 6 idealized scenarios) used `depth_to_surface`/`depth_to_bottom` computed from cell-center-to-cell-center spans (`z[0]-z`/`z-z[-1]`), where real MITgcm uses interface-to-interface spans (`Ro_surf-R_low`/`Ro_surf-rF(k)`/`rF(k)-R_low`) — short by half the top cell plus half the bottom cell. Confirmed exactly at every mismatched point (e.g. `arctic_convection`: 13/115,000 `mixing_length` points, diff exactly 40.790 m = that scenario's own half-cell offset), propagating into `visc_az`/`diff_kz` (max abs diff 1.824 m²/s).

### Resolution and justification
Root-caused against `pkg/ggl90/ggl90_mixinglength.F:163-193`, `model/src/ini_depths.F:91-102/155-166`, `model/src/ini_vertical_grid.F:162` directly. Fix is entirely in `ggl90_core_driver.py::GGL90Driver.compute_mixing`'s construction of `depth_to_surface`/`depth_to_bottom` — no change needed in `_limit_method_0`/`_limit_method_1` themselves, since both methods' own formulas already exactly match MITgcm's once fed interface-derived inputs. Replaced the cell-center construction with an interface-depth construction built from `dz` (`grid.cell_thickness`, already available at every call site) — algebraically derived and confirmed to reduce to the constant `Ro_surf-R_low` for every `k` (`mxl_max_flag=0`) and to `min(Ro_surf-rF(k),rF(k)-R_low)` per-level (`mxl_max_flag=1`), matching the Fortran-index correspondence exactly.

Re-verified via a fresh export+build+run+compare cycle through the standalone `GGL90_CALC` driver across all 6 idealized scenarios plus `vermix`: `visc_az`/`diff_kz`/`mixing_length` 0 mismatches (>1% rel) on all 6 scenarios, max_rel 4.0e-15 to 4.7e-15 — matching 1DMIX-024's own bit-level-agreement bar. Directly confirmed at the unit level too via a synthetic asymmetric-cell grid (2 m top cell, 40 m bottom cell): fixed ceiling 72 m (matches `Ro_surf-R_low`) vs. the old buggy 51 m. `vermix` (`mxl_max_flag=3`, which never reads `Ro_surf`/`R_low`) confirmed unaffected. `tke_after` mismatches were found unchanged in magnitude/count by this fix (a genuinely separate mechanism) — split to 1DMIX-031, which also disproved this issue's own original solve_tridiagonal-ordering hypothesis.

Independently reviewed by Richard across 4 total rounds (3 in the original review, 1 in this closure's redispatch — the original review's dispatch events were never recorded in `loop_history`'s `subagents` field, blocking formal closure until now): re-derived the fix from primary Fortran sources rather than trusting the narrative, independently reproduced the standalone-driver comparison, built a new independent unit check on a different asymmetric grid, and in this closure's redispatch (agent_id `4646b49e-eb03-4e15-a2b3-2d06ec9a6b21`) independently reconfirmed everything from scratch including a fresh hand-rederivation of both `mxl_max_flag=0`/`1` formulas. Bob (agent_id `483c3565-6b37-4c83-9f71-df9cf1ce7d49`) also independently reconfirmed the fix in this closure's redispatch. All rounds verdict **APPROVE**, no must-fix.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest suite: 56 passed, 3 skipped, unchanged throughout. Structural suite (`verify.py --suite structural --owner arch --fresh`): EXECUTED PASS. `final_verification.py run --review ... --owner arch`: EXECUTED PASS, real receipt (`devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/1e0c283a1bfd7229f53c1ed35cdc5cd42ee33c8cb4ce80dc27725d4bf532755b.json`). No remaining bounds specific to this issue (the separately-tracked `tke_after` residual is 1DMIX-031's own scope).

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree).

## RESOLVED: `kpp_calc.F`'s `OUTPUT_MIXING` dump had the SAME early-truncation defect as 1DMIX-033's `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` bug — zero-filled real, nonzero `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t`/`ghat` for 83.5% of `global_oce_latlon`'s wet columns

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-20T09:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T13:55:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-035
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F` (the `OUTPUT_MIXING` loop, previously left unchanged by 1DMIX-033's fix); `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F::Ri_iwmix` (confirms background viscosity is unconditional).

### Issue
Re-examining `global_oce_latlon`'s already-completed full-year (720-timestep) KPP capture with a different question ("how often does MITgcm's own `visc_az` read exactly zero across an entire column when the real Fortran physics provably cannot produce that") found **83.5% of all 1,666,800 wet column-timesteps had `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t`/`ghat` exactly zero at every one of the 15 captured levels** — not a scientific disagreement, but capture data loss, the same failure mode fixed for `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` in 1DMIX-033 but left untouched for `OUTPUT_MIXING` on the (disproven) assumption that its own early-exit only ever fires when mixing genuinely is zero below that point.

### Resolution and justification
`pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F::Ri_iwmix` (lines 1206-1208) adds background viscosity unconditionally at every real interior level; `kpp_calc.F`'s `OUTPUT_MIXING` loop checked whether all 4 mixing fields were exactly zero at a level and, if so, wrote `OUTPUT_TRUNCATED` and skipped every level below — losing real, provably-nonzero data whenever that coincidence occurred at an early level. A point-level spot-check (t=0,x=0,y=2, 13 real wet levels) confirmed the exact mechanism: MITgcm's own dump was zero at every one of its 4 checked fields at level 1, triggering truncation for the entire rest of that column. Fixed by removing the value-based early-exit entirely, writing all 4 fields unconditionally for every level 1..Nr (pure control-flow change, no value computation touched) — the same fix pattern as 1DMIX-033. Regression-checked first on a cheap 10-timestep `1D_ocean_ice_column` rebuild (230 `OUTPUT_MIXING` lines exactly, 0 `OUTPUT_TRUNCATED`, correct background floor visible) before rebuilding and rerunning `global_oce_latlon`'s full 720-timestep capture with the fix.

**Cross-experiment spot-check confirmed this was pervasive, not `global_oce_latlon`-specific**: the same defect affected `1D_ocean_ice_column` (51.8% of wet columns), `lab_sea` (55.4%), `vermix` (30.0%), and `seaice_obcs` (62.4%)'s own existing captures. GGL90's analogous dump (`ggl90_calc.F`) was checked and confirmed clean (no early-exit logic at all) — the 1DMIX-028/029/030/031 GGL90 comparisons were unaffected.

**The fix's actual scientific payoff, obtained after the corrected full-year rerun+reparse+replay**: 0 of 1,666,800 wet columns (0.00%) now have `visc_az` all-zero at every level, down from 83.5% — the defect is completely eliminated. Agreement between the Python port and real MITgcm on the now-complete ground truth is excellent: `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t` across all wet levels — median abs diff 0 (exact), p95 4-7e-8, p99 8-14e-5, max 2-5.6 (rare tail). This directly supersedes 1DMIX-027's own provisional statistics (computed against the truncated ground truth) with real, complete, trustworthy numbers, confirming — on solid ground for the first time — that the Python port's KPP mixing coefficients agree with real MITgcm to near machine precision for the vast majority of `global_oce_latlon`'s full year.

Independently reviewed: Bob (agent_id `c1587ea7-944c-4dc6-ac3f-35f935f4c2a0`) confirmed the git diff is pure control-flow and independently reproduced the corrected capture's aggregate statistics from raw netCDF/log data (also flagged, then correctly withdrew, a false pytest-count discrepancy caused by comparing two suites with different scope — resolved, no real error). Richard (agent_id `804176ac-f8ef-4a38-ab6c-c0a1316a9f17`) independently verified the diff, spot-checked a different boundary point (t=719, last timestep, x=89/y=39 spatial corner) directly from the raw 13.7GB `output.txt` as an oracle independent of the netCDF/parser pipeline, and reran the exact pytest command. Richard found one real, valid must-fix: the sealed documentation report's `map_delta.reason` falsely claimed `docs/code_map.md` names the Fortran capture files it does not — corrected to accurately state the map's own header note scopes non-Python content to a bounded source search rather than a table row. Resealed, reconfirmed, verdict APPROVE.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest suite: 56 passed, 3 skipped, unchanged throughout (independently reconfirmed by both Bob and Richard). Structural suite (`verify.py --suite structural --owner arch --fresh`): EXECUTED PASS. `final_verification.py run --review ... --owner arch`: EXECUTED PASS, real receipt (`devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/dfd29d3cf95956f06fb2e7b21d58e116ea0bbe8ec4021444f31b49aac5d5fd29.json`). **Remaining bound**: whether `1D_ocean_ice_column`/`lab_sea`/`vermix`/`seaice_obcs`'s own prior `visc_az` comparisons need the same corrected-capture refresh (each experiment's own most-recent capture is stale relative to this fix) is flagged but not yet done — a candidate for a future issue.

### Traceability

## RESOLVED: KPP `hbl` diverged substantially on `vermix` (mean ~9 m over 20 timesteps) — missing `KPP_ESTIMATE_UREF` reference-velocity estimate, fixed and independently reviewed

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-20T05:44:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T12:35:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-032
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_core_driver.py::KPPDriver._compute_shear`, `KPPDriver._estimate_reference_velocity`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_parameters.py::KPPParameters.estimate_uref`; test: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_estimate_uref.py`.

### Issue
`vermix`'s real MITgcm KPP run (20 timesteps, single column, 26 levels, stock config with non-default `Ricr=0.45`/`minKPPhbl=10.`) showed `hbl` mean|diff| 9.12 m (max 13.8 m) against the Python port — far larger than any other KPP experiment's disagreement (all previously <1 m median). `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s` matched exactly (0% median relative error), localizing the divergence specifically to the `hbl` diagnosis, not the mixing-coefficient computation.

### Resolution and justification
Ruled out EOS choice (`vermix` uniquely uses MDJWF) empirically — swapping to JMD95Z changed the result by <0.01 m. Root-caused via decisive isolation: feeding MITgcm's own captured `dVsq`/`buoy_freq_sq`/`Ritop` directly into the Python port's `diagnose_bl_depth` (bypassing the port's own upstream computation) reproduced MITgcm's `hbl` exactly at all 20 timesteps — proving the bulk-Richardson search, `ws`/`wm` lookup, and `minKPPhbl` post-processing were all correct, and the divergence was entirely upstream in how `dVsq`/`Ritop` were computed from raw state. Traced to `kpp_forcing_surf.F:309` (`#ifdef KPP_ESTIMATE_UREF`): `vermix`'s own `KPP_OPTIONS.h` uniquely defines this flag among all tested experiments, selecting a resolution-independent reference-velocity estimate (log-profile or depth-weighted-average over a mixed-layer-depth-dependent reference level) instead of the simple `Uref=U(1)` the Python port always used. Fixed by adding `KPPDriver._estimate_reference_velocity` (a line-by-line translation of `kpp_forcing_surf.F:309-419`), wired into `_compute_shear` behind the pre-existing but previously-unwired `KPPParameters.estimate_uref` flag (default `False`, matching MITgcm's own `#undef` default — zero behavior change for every other experiment). Verified end-to-end: `hbl` mean|diff| dropped from 9.13 m to 5.6e-5 m (~160,000x reduction); `dVsq` itself now matches MITgcm to floating-point roundoff (max_rel 1.47e-15, was up to 3 orders of magnitude off). 4 new tests added, each independently re-deriving expected values via hand arithmetic, not by calling the driver.

Independently reviewed twice this session. First round (2026-09-20T06:59Z-08:03Z): Bob + two independent Richards, all APPROVE — but that review's dispatch events were never recorded in `loop_history.jsonl`'s `subagents` field, so this project's own `known_completion_iteration()` mechanism could not historically reuse them for formal closure (a real bookkeeping gap, not a scientific one). Second round (2026-09-20T11:50Z-12:35Z, this closure): redispatched Bob (agent_id `888680b6-5b30-403c-90a5-6eced3b56d08`) to reconfirm the fix is present, unchanged, and independently hand-rederive one test case; dispatched two fresh independent Richards (`650ad2b7-e91c-4d4f-865f-ceeff9e2b87f`, `34771be1-7fc0-4a02-a7c4-2f76f0dd9908`) with different review angles — Richard 1 did a term-by-term Fortran-to-Python translation check plus an algebraic proof the C-grid 4-point average collapses exactly for a laterally-uniform single-column setup; Richard 2 confirmed `vermix` actually compiles with `KPP_ESTIMATE_UREF` defined (not just that the Python code exists) and traced the flag concretely activating on `vermix`'s real capture. Both independently hand-rederived the depth-weighted-average branch (deliberately the other of the two branches from Bob's log-profile check). Both verdict **APPROVE**, no must-fix.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest suite: 56 passed, 3 skipped throughout (unchanged from the documented baseline; the 4 new tests are additive). Structural suite (`verify.py --suite structural --owner arch --fresh`): EXECUTED PASS. `final_verification.py run --review ... --owner arch`: EXECUTED PASS, real receipt (`devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/1969ae6dd3a35fe73e93568b2a00b211cd28c4407a489d9f795e23b481be3876.json`). Two rounds of pure bookkeeping corrections were needed in the second review round (both Richards initially used the candidate file's own internal `signature` field instead of `project.py::source_signature()` for `candidate_signature`; both used `documentation_review.status="confirmed_current"` instead of the required literal `"confirmed"`) — neither affected their technical findings, both resolved via targeted followups. **Remaining bound**: the tiny residual (~1e-4 m in `hbl`) consistent with `buoy_freq_sq`'s already-known, separately-tracked ~0.34% residual (1DMIX-022) is not part of this issue's own scope.

This closure directly informs the same redispatch pattern needed for 1DMIX-024/1DMIX-030/1DMIX-033 (all share the identical missing-subagents-bookkeeping gap from their own original review rounds).

## RESOLVED: `kpp_calc.F`'s `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` dump was truncated by the `OUTPUT_MIXING`-only zero check — silently zero-filled real shear_sq/buoy_freq_sq/Ritop whenever KPP's mixing enhancement vanished early

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-20T05:55:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T15:05:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-033
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/parse_mitgcm_split.py` (confirmed order-independent, unaffected).

### Issue
Found while root-causing 1DMIX-032's `vermix`+KPP `hbl` divergence. The instrumented `kpp_calc.F`'s per-level output loop checked whether `KPPviscAz`/`KPPdiffKzS`/`KPPdiffKzT`/`KPPghat` (the `OUTPUT_MIXING` fields) were all exactly zero at a level, and if so, wrote a single `OUTPUT_TRUNCATED` marker and skipped the `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` WRITE (`shear_sq`, `buoy_freq_sq`, `Ri`, `dVsq`, `Ritop`) for that level and every level below — correct for `OUTPUT_MIXING` itself, but wrong for `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS`, which are raw physical quantities that exist at every level regardless of KPP's own mixing-enhancement decision. Confirmed directly on `vermix`'s real capture: `TIMESTEP=3` had zero real `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` lines despite the Python port independently computing real, physically-reasonable nonzero values.

### Resolution and justification
Split the single output loop into two independent loops: `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` now always written unconditionally for every level 1..Nr; `OUTPUT_MIXING` originally kept its own separate early-exit (later itself removed by the separately-closed 1DMIX-035, making both loops unconditional in the file's current state — a superset of this issue's own acceptance criterion, not a regression). Purely additive: every previously-written `OUTPUT_MIXING`/`OUTPUT_HBL`/`OUTPUT_TRUNCATED` line stayed byte-identical. Verified on `vermix`'s real capture: `TIMESTEP=3` went from 0 real `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` lines to 26, matching the Python port's own independent computation to 6 significant figures. `parse_mitgcm_split.py` confirmed by direct read to key purely by line-tag into dicts by `(bi,bj,i,j,k)` — line order/interleaving has zero effect on parsing.

Independently reviewed twice (first round undocumented in `subagents` bookkeeping, redispatched for proper closure): Bob (agent_id `d5ef2098-234b-4b2d-8116-1d228562fa50`) reconfirmed the fix is present and purely additive, and independently confirmed `parse_mitgcm_split.py`'s order-independence by direct read. Richard (agent_id `0f13f87e-ef27-47f4-af39-1e7380150d3c`) independently reconfirmed the same from scratch, read the parser's dispatch loop directly (lines 163-273), reran the full pytest suite, and went beyond the brief with two extra checks: confirmed no live consumer still depends on the removed `OUTPUT_TRUNCATED` tag, and cross-checked a different instrumented site (`vermix`) showing the identical additive pattern. Both verdict **APPROVE**, no must-fix — clean on the first attempt, no correction rounds needed.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest suite: 56 passed, 3 skipped, unchanged throughout (independently reconfirmed by both Bob and Richard). Structural suite (`verify.py --suite structural --owner arch --fresh`): EXECUTED PASS. `final_verification.py run --review ... --owner arch`: EXECUTED PASS, real receipt (`devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/d4d890e9ebf1fb9ef9cd8e14ec132b9f7fab5728c5fed4e3fffe7662cd1fd684.json`). No remaining bounds specific to this issue.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree).

## RESOLVED: `tools/esx/verify.py`'s evidence-reuse fingerprint hashes raw `PATH`, causing spurious "verification evidence is stale" failures across process boundaries — blocked formal closure of 1DMIX-024/1DMIX-030/1DMIX-032/1DMIX-033

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-20T10:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T11:33:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-036
**Anchors**: `tools/esx/project.py::environment` (the `variables` dict built from `cfg['environment_variables']`); `esx/project.json` (`environment_variables` list); `tools/esx/verify.py::fingerprint`/`::load_evidence` (the consumer that fails "stale" on any digest mismatch).

### Issue
Across this session, four independently scientifically-completed and independently-reviewed issues (1DMIX-024, 1DMIX-030, 1DMIX-032, 1DMIX-033) each failed to reach formal `outcome=completed` for the same reason: `verify.load_evidence()` rejected a reviewer's (Bob's/Richard's) `independent_check` evidence as "stale" when later checked from a different OS process (Arch's own shell) than the one that produced it, even though the actual source tree never changed between dispatches (confirmed each time via `project.source_signature('.')`).

### Resolution and justification
Root-caused directly: `tools/esx/project.py::environment()` builds `variables = {k: os.environ.get(k) for k in cfg['environment_variables']}`, and `esx/project.json` configured `environment_variables = ["PATH", "CONDA_PREFIX", "CONDA_DEFAULT_ENV", "PYTHONPATH", "OMP_NUM_THREADS", "LANG", "LC_ALL"]`, folded by `verify.py::fingerprint()` into `load_evidence()`'s exact-match staleness digest. Direct reproduction: a synthetic "different session" `PATH` string (preserving every functionally relevant directory but changing one incidental trailing segment, e.g. a plugin-cache-version bump, and reordering entries) changed the `environment()` digest while every *other* field (python/executable/platform, remaining variables, toolchain-probe stdout capturing real `numpy`/`scipy`/`xarray`/`netCDF4` versions, external-input hashes) stayed byte-identical — proving `PATH` was the single field exposed to incidental, non-reproducibility-relevant session/process variation while every other field already robustly captured what genuinely matters. Fix: removed `"PATH"` from `esx/project.json`'s `environment_variables` list (one-line configuration change, `git diff` confirms nothing else touched). Classified `kind=scientific_change` with `guard_relaxation` risk (2 required reviewers, since `esx/project.json` is part of this project's scientific signature and this loosens what counts as matching evidence). **Bob** (agent_id `05e57d57-876a-4759-bff5-2c9326a98e1d`) independently reproduced the same root cause with his own script and implemented exactly this fix. **Two independent Richards** (agent_ids `35205261-7aac-453d-98c6-a89119ecb54c` and `de62d2a7-ddfb-495d-9dbb-3ff8eaa65e22`), each given a different review question and instructed not to read the other's transcript, both independently reproduced the defect with distinct techniques (subprocess-dispatch synthetic PATH vs. in-process `os.environ` mutation), confirmed zero reproducibility signal was lost, and both verdict **APPROVE**, no must-fix. Richard 2 additionally found, and explicitly did not block on, a well-evidenced follow-up: `OMP_NUM_THREADS`/`PYTHONPATH` empirically share PATH's exact defect class — flagged for a future, separate issue once real production impact is observed (not yet split out; evidence preserved in his dispatch report).

### Verification and remaining bounds
Attempting the final closure step first surfaced a second, distinct, previously-unexercised tooling bug in `final_verification.py` itself (a dual-module-identity lease bug — see 1DMIX-037, resolved separately, immediately below/above in this file). Once that was fixed, revisited this issue (iteration 2): recaptured a fresh candidate reflecting both the PATH fix and the unrelated 1DMIX-037 fix, resealed documentation covering both changed targets (reusing Bob's original `esx/project.json` disposition text, adding a new disposition for `final_verification.py::<module>`), and ran two rounds of lightweight reconfirmation with both Richards (confirming their technical approval still held against the new candidate signature, plus one footer-bookkeeping correction each — an `iteration_timestamp` field and a `documentation_review.report` reference, neither touching their technical judgment). `workflow_records.py readiness --stage final`: `READY`. **`final_verification.py run --review ... --owner arch`: `EXECUTED PASS`** — the first successful run of this code path in this project's entire history, producing a real receipt (`devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/439c6a0c74507b5a23762913125f5ffdf7fece2b75dde61eb5554068cc6f05b0.json`) and scientific evidence (`devel-loop/loop_state/verification/a263727fb7ce13f74e39d4ec200b4a3d6e9027e16b254cadd01b80e4960703eb.json`). Structural suite (`verify.py --suite structural --owner arch --fresh`): `EXECUTED PASS`. Full pytest suite unchanged throughout (56 passed, 3 skipped). **Remaining bound**: `OMP_NUM_THREADS`/`PYTHONPATH`'s confirmed-possible (not yet confirmed-in-production) spurious sensitivity, per Richard 2's finding, is deferred to a future issue.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree). This closure directly enables revisiting 1DMIX-024/1DMIX-030/1DMIX-032/1DMIX-033 using the now-fixed gate.

## RESOLVED: `tools/esx/final_verification.py`'s lease-based scientific-verification authorization always fails when invoked via its own documented CLI — a dual-module-identity bug, never previously exercised

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-20T10:41:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T10:56:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-037
**Anchors**: `tools/esx/final_verification.py::authorize`/`::run` (the `_LEASES` module-level dict); `tools/esx/verify.py::run` (the lazy `import final_verification` call site).

### Issue
`python3 tools/esx/final_verification.py run --review <packet> --owner <role>` — the exact invocation documented in `devel-loop/verification.md` for qualifying a reviewed `scientific_change` candidate — failed unconditionally with `"active issue scientific verification requires final_verification.py run after reviews and documentation are ready"` whenever there was an active issue (i.e. always, in normal operation), regardless of how correct or ready the underlying review packet was. Discovered while attempting to formally close 1DMIX-036 (fix approved by Bob and two independent Richards, `workflow_records.py readiness --stage final` reporting `READY`, `final_verification.py check` succeeding) — the `run` subcommand still failed.

### Resolution and justification
Root-caused directly (not guessed): `final_verification.py` module-level `_LEASES = {}`; its own `run()` does `token = object(); _LEASES[token] = (review, owner, identity)` then calls `verify.run(root, 'scientific', owner, fresh=True, _lease=token)`. `verify.py::run()` does a *lazy* `import final_verification` (inside the function body, not at module scope) and calls `final_verification.authorize(root, _lease)`, which checks `require(lease in _LEASES, ...)`. When `final_verification.py` is executed directly as a script, Python names the running module `__main__` and does **not** register it under `sys.modules['final_verification']` — so `verify.py`'s lazy `import final_verification` triggers a **fresh, second import** of the same file, creating an independent module object with its own separate, empty `_LEASES` dict. Confirmed with an isolated two-file minimal reproduction outside this project's code: a script defining `_LEASES = {}` and, when run as `__main__`, doing `import <itself-by-filename>` and comparing `is` on the two `_LEASES` objects — `False` before the fix. `devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/` contained zero prior records before this session's own failed attempt, and `closed_issues.md` had never contained a `scientific_change`-kind entry — confirming this exact code path had never been successfully exercised in this project's history before now; the bug was real but latent, not a regression. Pure verification-tooling defect, zero scientific/source impact (`tools/esx` is not part of `source_paths`/`test_paths`/`configuration_paths`, so this fix does not change `source_signature(scientific=True)`/`numerical_signature_at_start`), classified `kind=harness_change`, no risk tag, implemented directly by Arch given the fix's deterministic, already-isolated-and-confirmed nature (a textbook Python module-identity mechanics bug, not a scientific judgment call). Fix: added `sys.modules.setdefault('final_verification', sys.modules['__main__'])` immediately inside the `if __name__ == '__main__':` guard, before `main()` runs (`git diff` shows only this 4-line addition — a comment plus the one statement — nothing else touched).

### Verification and remaining bounds
Verified three independent ways, all convergent: (1) the isolated minimal reproduction above, confirmed `is`-identical (`True`) after the fix; (2) direct real-file end-to-end test: reran the exact previously-failing command against 1DMIX-036's own real review packet while 1DMIX-037 was the active issue — the error message *changed* from the lease-authorization failure to `"review packet must preserve issue and iteration timestamp"`, the next, legitimate, *expected* validation failure (since 1DMIX-037, not 1DMIX-036, was the active issue) — direct proof execution now proceeds past the exact previously-broken check; (3) direct module-identity confirmation on the real file (via `runpy.run_path(..., run_name="__main__")`): confirmed `sys.modules['final_verification']._LEASES is (fresh import final_verification)._LEASES` → `True`. Full pytest suite fresh: 56 passed, 3 skipped (unchanged). `loop_gate.py --doctor`: PASS. `verify.py --suite structural --owner arch --fresh`: `EXECUTED PASS`. **Remaining bound**: a full happy-path run of `final_verification.py run` against a real *active* `scientific_change` issue (proving the complete success path, not just "past the lease bug") is deferred to 1DMIX-036's own revisit, since this issue's own kind is `harness_change` and has no active scientific-change context of its own to test against.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree). Discovered and fixed within the same iteration as part of 1DMIX-036's closure attempt; blocks 1DMIX-036 (already implemented, doubly Richard-approved) and, transitively, 1DMIX-024/1DMIX-030/1DMIX-032/1DMIX-033 — all to be revisited using this now-fixed gate.

## RESOLVED: `1D_ocean_ice_column`'s stale 11,000-timestep capture reprocessed and validated — 1DMIX-012's bug did affect it (diagnostic columns only, not the primary mixing comparison), and the fixed, full three-way comparison is excellent

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T17:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T00:35:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-029
**Anchors**: `1D_ocean_ice_column/output_validation_11k/` (external MITgcm checkout); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/parse_mitgcm_split.py`, `scripts/run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`

### Issue
A real 11,000-timestep `1D_ocean_ice_column` capture (`output_validation_11k/`, `output.txt`/`mitgcm_kpp_inputs.nc`/`mitgcm_kpp_outputs.nc`) predates every fix made earlier this session (dated 2026-08-19, a full month before 1DMIX-012's bare-exponent parser fix and everything since) and had apparently never been reprocessed through the current pipeline — the longest single-column duration available in this project, sitting unused.

### Resolution and justification
Preserved the stale `.nc` pair (`/tmp/stale_mitgcm_kpp_*.nc`), reparsed the raw `output.txt` with the current `parse_mitgcm_split.py` (no MITgcm rebuild needed — the text capture already existed), and diffed old vs. new. **1DMIX-012's bare-exponent bug did affect this capture**: 139 of 253,000 `shear_sq`/`buoy_freq_sq` (OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS) cells differ, confirmed at the exact mechanism (e.g. `t=1,x=0,y=0,z=17`: stale `shear_sq=0.0`/`buoy_freq_sq=0.0`, fresh `shear_sq=1.05e-105`/`buoy_freq_sq=2.01e-3` — the tiny `shear_sq` value needs a 3-digit exponent, hit the bare-exponent bug, and silently zeroed both it and its sibling field on the same line, exactly 1DMIX-012's pattern). Critically, **`hbl`/`visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t`/`ghat` — the primary three-way comparison quantities — are byte-identical between stale and fresh**: these come from separate `OUTPUT_MIXING`/`OUTPUT_HBL` tags/WRITE statements in `kpp_calc.F`, a different code path never exposed to this bug for this capture. So the bug was real here, but confined to diagnostic-only columns (relevant to future `dVsq`/`Ritop`-style standalone-driver export work, not to the primary KPP-mixing-coefficient validation).

Ran the actual three-way comparison (Python port vs. the now-current MITgcm capture) for the first time over the full 11,000 timesteps: `hbl` median diff 0.0001 m, p95 0.022 m, p99 0.080 m, only 0.38%/0.25% of timesteps exceed 1 m/5 m diff (worst case 21.3 m at `t=2311`, a real, sharp thermocline transition — theta jumps from -1.95°C to -0.59°C across 2 levels in this sea-ice-coupled column, exactly the sharp-gradient regime the already-characterized 1DMIX-019 Rib/Ricr threshold-sensitivity mechanism predicts should be most sensitive; not re-investigated from scratch given the matching signature). `visc_az`: median diff exactly 0.0, **zero** active cells exceed the 1e-2 threshold (better than every other experiment tested this project, including `lab_sea`'s 0.024%).

### Verification and remaining bounds
This is the best three-way agreement of any experiment validated so far, over the longest single-column duration available. `data.pkg` confirmed `useKPP=.TRUE.` only (no `useGGL90`, no `data.ggl90` present) — GGL90 has never been tested on this experiment's sea-ice-coupled configuration; tracked separately under 1DMIX-028 (systematic KPP↔GGL90 cross-testing) rather than duplicated here. Full pytest suite: `50 passed, 3 skipped` (unaffected — this was pure data reprocessing, no code changed).

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). No source files changed (reprocessing only); regenerated (gitignored, external checkout, not tracked) `1D_ocean_ice_column/output_validation_11k/mitgcm_kpp_inputs.nc`, `mitgcm_kpp_outputs.nc`, and new `python_kpp_outputs.nc`. Stale pre-fix `.nc` pair preserved at `/tmp/stale_mitgcm_kpp_{inputs,outputs}.nc` for this comparison's evidence trail (not a permanent project location).
