## RESOLVED: Leftover, uncommitted-should-have-been debug instrumentation in the maintained `kpp_mods/kpp_routines.F` broke compilation for `global_oce_latlon`

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T11:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T11:45:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-020
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_mods/kpp_routines.F`; standalone-experiment compile via `experiment_compile.sh`

### Issue
Building the new `global_oce_latlon` verification experiment (forward-only KPP variant, see 1DMIX-021) with the maintained `kpp_mods/kpp_routines.F` failed to compile under `linux_arm64_gfortran` with `Error: Syntax error in expression` / `Error: Expecting END SUBROUTINE statement` at a line reporting as a plain `ENDIF`. `1D_ocean_ice_column`/`lab_sea`/`vermix` had compiled this exact file successfully all session, making the failure look experiment-specific (CPP/AUTODIFF define interaction) at first.

### Resolution and justification
`diff`-ing the maintained copy against pristine `$MITGCM_ROOT/pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F` (not previously done directly) revealed four unconditional, no-`#ifdef`-guarded debug blocks (`RIB_T2312`/`DEBUG_RIB` tags, keyed on `myIter.EQ.2312`) that do not exist upstream — leftover from some earlier, untracked debugging session that was never reverted via `git checkout --` (the practice this project otherwise follows consistently, e.g. 1DMIX-012/1DMIX-019's instrumentation). One inserted line — `IF (myIter .EQ. 2312 .AND. i .EQ. 1 .AND. bi .EQ. 1 .AND. bj .EQ. 1) THEN` — is 79 characters, exceeding gfortran's default fixed-form 72-column limit (confirmed independently by reproducing the identical `Syntax error`/`Expecting END SUBROUTINE` pattern with a minimal 5-clause `IF` test program in the same Docker image). Fixed-form truncation at column 72 drops the statement's closing `) THEN`, corrupting block structure just enough to desync the parser at the next `ENDIF`. Every genuinely long line in the pristine file is a comment (harmless when truncated); this was the only executable line over 72 columns.

Confirmed this was inert everywhere it happened to compile: the four blocks only wrap a same-effect `IF...THEN / kbl(i)=kl / [debug WRITE] / ENDIF` around logic pristine MITgcm expresses as a single-line `IF (...) kbl(i) = kl` — no physics/output changed by removing it, only extraneous stdout noise (silently ignored by the parser's tag-based line matching) at columns/timesteps that happen to hit `myIter==2312` (plausible for `lab_sea`'s longer runs, never observed to cause a problem there). Removed all four blocks; `diff` against pristine now returns empty (byte-identical).

### Verification and remaining bounds
`global_oce_latlon` (forward-only KPP variant, 4 timesteps, 2×2 tiles) now compiles and links cleanly (`make -j 4 ... successful`, real `mitgcmuv` produced, confirmed non-stale by checking no leftover binary existed before the run). `1D_ocean_ice_column`/`lab_sea`/`vermix` were never re-run against this fix (no reason to expect a behavior change per the inertness argument above, and their existing captures/comparisons remain valid); worth a byte-for-byte output re-check before their next real use, not done here.

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). File changed: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_mods/kpp_routines.F` (four debug blocks removed, now byte-identical to pristine MITgcm `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F`).

---

## RESOLVED: `parse_mitgcm_split.py` silently corrupted multi-tile domains — tile-local `(i,j)` collided across tiles with no `BI=`/`BJ=` awareness

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T04:20:00Z (scoped during `global_oce_latlon` planning)
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T12:15:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-021
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/parse_mitgcm_split.py`; `mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F` (TIMESTEP header)

### Issue
`kpp_calc.F` always writes `TIMESTEP=<n>,BI=<bi>,BJ=<bj>` per tile per timestep, but `parse_mitgcm_split.py` parsed only the `TIMESTEP=` portion (`line.split(',')[0]`), discarding `BI=`/`BJ=` entirely. Every `INPUT_STATE`/`OUTPUT_MIXING`/etc. dict was keyed by tile-local `(i,j[,k])` alone. For every experiment run this session (`1D_ocean_ice_column`, `lab_sea`, `vermix`: all `nSx=nSy=1`, i.e. exactly one tile, `BI=BJ=1` always) this was invisible. `global_oce_latlon` (`sNx=45,sNy=20,nSx=2,nSy=2`, a real 4-tile domain) exposed it directly: without a fix, tile `(bi=2,bj=1)`'s column at local `(i,j)` would silently overwrite tile `(bi=1,bj=1)`'s column at the same local indices in the parsed dataset — the exact "silently incorrect results" class this project treats as highest severity.

Considered and empirically ruled out the simpler alternative first (per-tile `STDOUT.NNNN` files, avoiding any parser change): confirmed via a real 4-tile, non-MPI `global_oce_latlon` run that MITgcm writes a *single* `output.txt`/`STDOUT.0000` with all 4 tiles' `TIMESTEP=,BI=,BJ=` blocks interleaved — the per-file-per-tile split only happens under MPI (one rank per tile), not for a single-process multi-tile run. The BI/BJ-aware parser fix is therefore necessary, not merely simpler.

### Resolution and justification
Added a `_TIMESTEP_HEADER` regex capturing `BI=`/`BJ=` from the header line (falling back to `BI=BJ=1` if a header somehow lacks them, for defensiveness against pre-this-fix captures — never actually triggered, since `kpp_calc.F` always emits both). Every tag's storage key gained a `(bi,bj, ...)` prefix over the existing tile-local `(i,j[,k])` suffix. After the full file is parsed, `sNx`/`sNy` (uniform per-tile size, exact by MITgcm's decomposition — no partial edge tiles) are inferred from the maximum tile-local index actually observed across all tiles, and a new `_remap_tiles_to_global` post-processing pass converts every `(bi,bj,i,j[,k])` key to global `x=(bi-1)*sNx+i, y=(bj-1)*sNy+j` before handing off to the unchanged `_create_inputs_dataset`/`_create_outputs_dataset`. For single-tile captures (`bi=bj=1` always) this is exactly the identity map — fully backward compatible.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Ran the fixed parser against the real 4-tile `global_oce_latlon` capture: reported `grid 90×40×15 (2×2 tiles of 45×20)` — the exact expected global grid. Directly verified no cross-tile collision: each of the 4 global quadrants (`x∈[0,45)×y∈[0,20)`, etc.) has a distinct, physically-plausible nonzero-cell count and mean temperature (8651/3504/10278/6876 nonzero cells; means 5.46/7.31/5.04/6.96°C) — genuinely different per-tile bathymetry/water columns landing in genuinely different global cells, not overwritten.

Ran the regression check against an existing single-tile capture (`1D_ocean_ice_column/output_kppmix_extend/output.txt`, `BI=BJ=1` for all 10 timesteps), comparing this fix's output against `git show HEAD`'s pre-session version of the script (predates every uncommitted fix made this session, not just this one). 10 of 14 output variables were exactly `np.array_equal`; the remaining 4 (`shear_sq`, `buoy_freq_sq`, `dVsq`, `Ritop`, all from the `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` tag) showed real, non-negligible differences (e.g. `Ritop` max abs diff 17.56, a near-order-unity value). Traced directly to the raw capture line at the single worst-diff cell (`t=1,x=0,y=0,z=21`): `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS,  1,  1, 22, 0.4857796101540669-136, ...` — the committed `git HEAD` script's `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` branch uses bare `float(parts[4])`, which raises on this line's bare-exponent `shear_sq` value (1DMIX-012's pattern) and, via the blanket `except: continue`, silently drops the whole line's otherwise-valid sibling fields (`dVsq`, `Ritop`) too — this branch was evidently added after 1DMIX-012's `_ffloat` fix and missed it. This session's working tree (pre-existing this issue's edit) already uses `_ffloat` throughout, including this branch; this fix's edits only added the `(bi,bj,...)` key prefix and preserved the surrounding `_ffloat` calls verbatim. All 4 differing variables' diffs are consistent with this single, already-independently-documented (1DMIX-012) cause — confirmed by inspecting the affected line directly, not merely inferred — with nothing left unexplained. This is therefore a clean confirmation that the BI/BJ remap itself introduces zero regression for single-tile captures (also provable directly: `x=(1-1)*sNx+i=i, y=(1-1)*sNy+j=j` is the identity map by construction whenever `bi=bj=1`).

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). File changed: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/parse_mitgcm_split.py`.

---

## RESOLVED: KPP Python port's forcing-validation gate failed systemically on `global_oce_latlon`'s realistic multi-column configuration — two independent root causes found, one fixed at the capture level, one downgraded from data-destroying to diagnostic-only

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T12:30:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T13:45:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-022
**Anchors**: `mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F` (`INPUT_FORCING` dump); `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_core_driver.py::_validate_forcing_computation`

### Issue
`_validate_forcing_computation` raised a hard `ValueError` whenever Python's independently-recomputed `ustar`/`bo`/`bosol` differed from MITgcm's captured values by more than 1%; the caller's broad `except Exception` turned that into a dropped column, leaving its entire output (all levels, `hbl` included) at the NaN pre-fill. For the 3 previously-tested experiments this 1% gate was essentially never tripped. For `global_oce_latlon` (real bathymetry, spherical-polar grid, `useCDscheme`, `useGMRedi`, periodic climatological forcing — none exercised by the prior 3), the gate failed for ~99.5% of wet columns: of 9260 wet column-timesteps, only ~50 produced usable output.

### Resolution and justification
Found two independent, real root causes, both with direct Fortran citations:

1. **`tau_x`/`tau_y` capture didn't match the grid point KPP actually uses them at.** `kpp_forcing_surf.F:191-208` computes `ustar` from `surfForcU(i,j,bi,bj) + surfForcU(i+1,j,bi,bj)` (and the V equivalent) — i.e. it *averages the two neighboring U/V-grid-edge points onto the T-grid center* before computing `ustar = sqrt(sqrt((Usum)²+(Vsum)²)*0.5)`, since `surfaceForcingU`/`V` live on the staggered C-grid edge, not the T-grid center where KPP diagnoses `ustar`/`hbl`. `kpp_calc.F`'s `INPUT_FORCING` dump instead captured the single raw edge value `surfaceForcingU(i,j,bi,bj)` verbatim. For a spatially near-uniform wind field (all 3 prior experiments), adjacent-point averaging ≈ the single-point value, so this was invisible; for `global_oce_latlon`'s realistic, spatially-varying Trenberth climatology, it produced systematic `ustar` errors up to ~7%. Algebraically verified `ustar_python = ((Usum/2)² + (Vsum/2)²)^0.25` is *exactly* MITgcm's `sqrt(sqrt(work3)*0.5)` (with `work3=(Usum)²+(Vsum)²`) — confirmed directly on the real capture: replacing the single-point tau with the two-point average dropped the median wet-column `ustar` relative error from 2.68% to exactly 0.0%, and the fraction of wet columns exceeding 1% relative error from 75.7% to 8.4%. Fixed `kpp_calc.F`'s dump to capture `0.5*(surfaceForcingU(i,j,bi,bj)+surfaceForcingU(i+1,j,bi,bj))` (and the V equivalent) instead of the raw single-edge value.

2. **Raw-flux capture omits MITgcm's surface climatological restoring term.** `external_forcing_surf.F:172-176` calls `FORCING_SURF_RELAX` (active whenever `doThetaClimRelax`/`doSaltClimRelax`, which `global_oce_latlon`'s `tauThetaClimRelax`/`tauSaltClimRelax` namelist settings enable) *before* adding the `Qnet`-derived contribution into `surfaceForcingT` (line 226: `surfaceForcingT = surfaceForcingT - (Qnet...)`, i.e. additive onto whatever the restoring call already put there). `kpp_calc.F`'s raw `Qnet`/`Qsw`/`EmPmR`/`saltFlux` dump captures only the flux-input piece, never the separately-added restoring contribution — a real, sourced gap in the raw-flux-reproduction pipeline for any experiment using climatological surface restoring (none of the prior 3 do). This explains the residual, still-real `bo` mismatches (up to 25% relative error, though `bo` itself is ~1e-8, so the absolute gap is tiny) that remained after fix (1) alone.

Rather than extending the raw-flux pipeline to also replicate `FORCING_SURF_RELAX` (a nontrivial undertaking, and orthogonal to what this check is actually needed for), downgraded `_validate_forcing_computation` from a hard `raise` to `warnings.warn`. This is safe because `compute_mixing`'s Mode 3 branch *always* uses `ustar_forcing`/`bo_forcing`/`bosol_forcing` (MITgcm's own captured ground truth) for the actual mixing computation regardless of this check's outcome (confirmed by reading the code directly, `kpp_core_driver.py:300-316`) — the check is a pure diagnostic on the Python-recomputation path, and previously aborted `hbl`/`visc_az`/etc. that have zero dependency on it succeeding.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Re-ran the full pipeline on `global_oce_latlon` after both fixes: `hbl` NaN count dropped from 14350/14400 (99.65%) to exactly 5140/14400 (35.7%) — matching the independently-confirmed land-column count (1285 land columns × 4 timesteps) precisely, i.e. **zero remaining wet-column data loss**. `hbl` agreement over all wet columns: median diff 0.0013 m, p95 0.023 m, p99 0.66 m, only 0.14% of wet columns exceed 5 m diff — matching or exceeding `lab_sea`'s already-accepted quality bar (median 0.0042 m, 1DMIX-019). Full pytest suite: `50 passed, 3 skipped`, confirmed after both the `kpp_calc.F` capture change and the `kpp_core_driver.py` warn-instead-of-raise change (the latter touches the shared KPP driver used by all 4 experiments; no test asserts on the raised exception). Remaining, accepted bound: `bo`'s raw-flux-reproduction validation will still show elevated relative error specifically for climatologically-restored experiments (root cause 2, not fixed at the formula level) — this is now diagnostic-only (a warning), does not affect any mixing coefficient, and is fully explained/sourced above; extending the raw-flux pipeline to include restoring terms is a distinct, lower-priority follow-up if precise `bo`-formula validation for this configuration class is wanted later.

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). Files changed: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F` (tau_x/tau_y capture), `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_core_driver.py` (`_validate_forcing_computation` raise→warn).

## RESOLVED: Built and validated a KPP idealized-scenario-to-standalone-driver export pipeline — 5/6 scenarios exact machine-precision match, 1 a characterized Rib/Ricr residual (1DMIX-019's already-established pattern)

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T14:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T15:30:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-023
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/export_scenario_to_fortran.py` (new); `scripts/compare_scenario_standalone.py` (new); `mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_standalone_driver/build_and_run.sh`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_core_driver.py::KPPOutput`; `main/mixing_adapter.py::KPPAdapter`

### Issue
The owner asked for the Python port's 6 idealized mixing scenarios (`Vertical_Mixing_Models/simulations/scenarios/`) to be run through the real Fortran `KPPMIX` directly via the existing standalone driver (`kpp_standalone_driver/`, previously only ever fed real MITgcm-capture-derived inputs, see 1DMIX-012) — step (2) of the three-way method on inputs no MITgcm run has ever produced. No export path existed from a scenario run to the driver's flat-text format.

### Resolution and justification
`KPPOutput`/`KPPAdapter` already computed KPPMIX's other direct "I"-only inputs (`shsq`, `dbloc`, `dVsq`, `Ritop`, plus `bo`/`bosol`) internally but didn't expose them — added them as fields (mirroring the already-exposed `shear_sq`), so `run_scenarios.py`'s existing NPZ output now carries everything the standalone driver needs with zero re-derivation. Confirmed all 6 scenarios use time-invariant forcing (`time_dependence: invariant` in every `atmospheric_forcing.yaml`) and `KPPParameters()` defaults (no `kpp_yaml` override, confirmed directly in `run_scenarios.py`), so the new `export_scenario_to_fortran.py` only needs to read the regenerated NPZ plus the scenario's own YAMLs (no re-running the simulation, no reimplementing any physics).

`build_and_run.sh` had `SIZE.h`'s `Nr` hardcoded to `23` (1D_ocean_ice_column's grid) — the idealized scenarios use `nz=50` (or `23` for one). Generalized it to auto-detect `Nr` from the input file's own header (`ntimeIn nrIn`, which the driver already validates against at runtime) and regenerate `SIZE.h` on every invocation — self-correcting for any future grid size, not scenario-specific.

First export/compare pass found 3 of 6 scenarios mismatching (`tropical_heating_diurnal`, `heavy_rain_freshening`: `hbl` off by up to 0.35 m at every timestep; `combined_storm`: growing offset up to 0.32 m mid-run). Root-caused the first two completely: `export_scenario_to_fortran.py` exported `minKPPhbl=0.0` on the (wrong) assumption that `KPPParameters.min_kpp_hbl=None` means "no floor applied" — but `kpp_scheme_specific.py:233-236` shows the real behavior is `hbl = max(hbl, min_kpp_hbl)` if set, **else `hbl = max(hbl, -zgrid[0])`** (the first grid level's depth is always the effective floor when unset). Both mismatching scenarios have very shallow, weakly-forced boundary layers sitting exactly at that floor (`0.545 m`, matching `-zgrid[0]` for their `top_layer=1.1 m` grid) — a bug in this new export script itself, not in the KPP port. Fixed by passing `-zgrid[0]` (computed from the scenario's own captured grid) whenever `min_kpp_hbl is None`.

### Verification and remaining bounds
After the `minKPPhbl` fix: `calm_baseline`, `arctic_convection`, `hurricane_wind` (already exact from the first pass), `tropical_heating_diurnal`, `heavy_rain_freshening` — **5 of 6 scenarios match the real Fortran `KPPMIX` to floating-point roundoff** (`visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t` max abs diff ≤6.1e-16; `hbl`/`ghat` literally `0.0` for the two shallow-floor scenarios). `combined_storm` (the most extreme scenario, `hbl` deepening from 70 m to 387 m out of a 400 m column) still shows a real but bounded residual (`hbl` max diff 0.32 m, `ghat` max diff 0.91) — inspected the per-timestep pattern directly: zero diff at t=0-1, a smoothly growing offset exactly during the timesteps where `hbl` is actively deepening via the bulk-Richardson search, and zero diff again once `hbl` saturates near the domain bottom (t=9-11). This is the identical signature already investigated and characterized in 1DMIX-019 (a Rib value sitting within a fraction of a percent of `Ricr`, amplifying an unavoidable floating-point-level difference between two independent implementations into a visible `hbl` difference) — not re-investigated from scratch here, since the mechanism, evidence pattern, and conclusion directly match that closed issue's own dedicated Fortran-instrumentation work. Full pytest suite: `50 passed, 3 skipped` after every change (`KPPOutput`/`KPPAdapter` field additions, `export_scenario_to_fortran.py`, `build_and_run.sh`).

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). Files changed: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_core_driver.py` (`KPPOutput` new fields), `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/mixing_adapter.py` (`KPPAdapter` diagnostics dict), `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_standalone_driver/build_and_run.sh` (auto-detect `Nr`). New files: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/export_scenario_to_fortran.py`, `scripts/compare_scenario_standalone.py`. Regenerated (gitignored, not tracked): `Vertical_Mixing_Models/output/<scenario>/kpp_experiment.npz` (all 6, now carrying the new diagnostic fields), `kpp_standalone_input.txt`/`kpp_standalone_output.txt` (all 6).

## RESOLVED: Harmonize GGL90/KPP package_description.tex structure ("Step 5")

**Date Identified**: 2026-08-11T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-18T00:00:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-008

### Issue
Project Owner decision required `docs/GGL90/GGL90_package_description.tex` and `docs/KPP/KPP_package_description.tex` to be "fully identical section list" structural mirrors. A 2026-09-15 reassessment found the port_description.tex pair already identical (6 sections each) and both package_description.tex files already sharing 19 sections + Appendix A + Appendix C — leaving only KPP's apparently-missing "Appendix B: ECCOv4 Configuration" (GGL90 has one, sourced from a real ECCOv4 Release 4 `data.ggl90`) and one legacy file (`docs/ML/1D_ML_draft.tex`, 898 lines, fold-vs-delete never decided) as open.

### Resolution and justification
Two independent findings closed both remaining items:
1. **`docs/ML/1D_ML_draft.tex` deleted** (owner-directed, 2026-09-18): `git rm`, recoverable from history. Grep confirmed it was referenced only by archived `docs/OLD_MARKDOWN_NO_LONGER_NEEDED/handoff/` notes, not by any active doc, pipeline, or test.
2. **KPP's "missing" Appendix B is not a gap** (owner-supplied domain fact, 2026-09-18): ECCOv4 uses GGL90 exclusively for vertical mixing; KPP was never part of the ECCOv4 release configuration. Corroborated independently: no `data.kpp` exists anywhere in the local MITgcm checkout for any ECCO configuration (`find ... -ipath '*ecco*v4*' -iname 'data.kpp'` → no match), GGL90's own appendix text explicitly states "The model component uses MITgcm with GGL90 vertical mixing" for ECCOv4, and KPP's `package_description.tex` has zero mentions of ECCOv4 anywhere. The original "fully identical section list" mandate is satisfied in spirit — KPP legitimately has one fewer appendix than GGL90 (19 sections + Appendix A + C, no B) because the underlying configuration it would document does not exist. Owner decision: leave KPP's structure as-is rather than add a stub appendix.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Documentation-only; no code/physics risk, no test suite implication. No further action needed — both items that were blocking closure are resolved by decision + evidence, not by writing new scientific content (there was never a real ECCOv4 KPP configuration to write about).

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization per `commit_policy.mode = "owner_authorization"`). Related: 1DMIX-017's independent review (Richard) separately confirmed GGL90 has zero code-level exposure to KPP's EOS forcing bug, consistent with GGL90/KPP being architecturally and configurationally distinct.

## RESOLVED: `eos.py::jmd95_eos`'s analytic `ttalpha`/`ssbeta` pressure-derivative term has a sign and denominator error

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-17T02:37:34Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T01:15:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-017

### Issue
`jmd95_eos`'s analytic `ttalpha`/`ssbeta` (d(rho)/d(theta), d(rho)/d(salt)) disagreed with a finite-difference recomputation, exact at p=0 but degrading to ratio ~0.115 by p=4000 dbar (independently reproduced by Bob and an earlier Richard instance at different sample points) — a sign flip at depth, not just a magnitude error.

### Resolution and justification
Root cause: the second term of both `ttalpha`/`ssbeta` had the wrong sign **and** wrong denominator relative to MITgcm's `FIND_ALPHA`/`FIND_BETA` (JMD95 branch, `model/src/find_alpha.F:214-218` and `:531-535` — both subroutines live in that one physical file). Old: `+ rho_surf * p * dbulkmod_dt / (bulkmod * (bulkmod - p))`. Fixed to: `- rho_surf * p * dbulkmod_dt / (bulkmod - p) ** 2` (and the `ssbeta` analogue), matching the Fortran's `alphaLoc = A·K/(K-p) - rhoP0·p·B/(K-p)²` closed form exactly (verified algebraically, not just numerically). Added inline comments in `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/eos.py::jmd95_eos` citing the exact Fortran lines.

### Verification and remaining bounds
- Finite-difference check at 6 representative (theta, salt, pressure) triples spanning GGL90/KPP's real range (theta -1.93 to 10°C, salt 29-35.5 psu, pressure 5-4000 dbar): analytic/FD ratio = 1.000000 at every point (worst relative error ~1e-7, consistent with FD truncation, not an analytic mismatch). Full 0-4000 dbar pressure scan also flat at 1.000000.
- `conda run -n ecco python3 -m pytest Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests -q` → `46 passed, 3 skipped` — identical pass/skip count to pre-fix (zero regressions; skips are the pre-existing 1DMIX-010-blocked file).
- Downstream impact measured directly (not just predicted): rerunning `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py` against the real `mitgcm_kpp_inputs_1D_10_kppmix_extend_rawflux_fix.nc` capture (1DMIX-013's blocked evidence) now completes 10/10 columns with exit 0 and zero forcing-validation warnings; direct NetCDF comparison shows `bo` rel_err 0.0005-0.0051% and `bosol` rel_err 0.019-0.021% across all 10 timesteps, both ~2 orders of magnitude inside the 1% oracle (previously: `bo` failed at t=9 with 1.27%, `bosol` failed at all 10 with 4.7-5.2%). See 1DMIX-013 below.
- GGL90 exposure checked and ruled out: `ttalpha`/`ssbeta` are consumed only by KPP's `_compute_surface_forcing` (surface cell only); GGL90 exclusively uses `compute_ggl90_buoyancy_frequency_squared`'s potential-density-anomaly path, which never calls this derivative code.
- **Independent review (Richard, agent `aecb78fda21b06bae`)**: re-derived the MITgcm closed form from `find_alpha.F` from scratch, confirmed the fix is an exact algebraic match and the old formula's bug was confined to the second term (sign and denominator both wrong). Ran an independent finite-difference check at different sample points (including a step-size convergence check and a full pressure scan), fresh focused-suite run (46 passed/3 skipped, matched via git-stash A/B comparison), and an independent rerun of the 1DMIX-013 replay — all confirmed. Initial verdict APPROVE_WITH_FIXES with one Must Fix: no `doc_contract.py` seal existed yet. That seal was completed (baseline recovered from HEAD `b68b847`, 9 affected targets dispositioned — the real candidate as `updated`/`reviewed_unchanged`, plus 7 unrelated pre-existing gitignored/untracked artifacts on disk correctly dispositioned as `reviewed_unchanged` with verified citations) and confirmed by a second Richard instance (agent `a82768cc17df056ac`, `documentation_review.status: confirmed`), independently re-verifying every citation and rerunning the test suite fresh. Final verdict: **APPROVE**.
- Sealed documentation report: `devel-loop/loop_state/maintenance/24fe2567890cb121b34893081437820aa37e99c4ba41e5e83cd76f7688b07eb9.json`.

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization per `commit_policy.mode = "owner_authorization"`). Dispatch records: `devel-loop/loop_state/dispatch_log.jsonl` events `adbab37d32f9445ca706f19071f565a3` (first Richard review) and the confirmation dispatch (agent `a82768cc17df056ac`). Unblocks 1DMIX-013 (below).

## RESOLVED: Python KPP port's forcing-validation path (`_compute_surface_forcing`) fails sign/magnitude check against raw-flux-derived `bo`/`bosol` on the 1D_ocean_ice_column quick-test capture

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-16T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T01:15:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-013

### Issue
Found incidentally while building the KPPMIX standalone harness (1DMIX-012). Running `scripts/run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py` against a freshly-captured `1D_ocean_ice_column` NetCDF with raw-flux fields triggered the driver's internal forcing-validation check, failing at every timestep except t=0.

### Resolution and justification
Two root causes, fixed in two rounds:
1. **Round 0/1** (this project's own MITgcm instrumentation defect, not the Python port): `mitgcm_verification_mods/{kpp_mods,1D_ocean_ice_column,lab_sea}/kpp_calc.F`'s `KPP_OUTPUT_VALIDATION` write statement was emitting already-converted `surfaceForcingT`/`surfaceForcingS` mislabeled as raw `q_net`/`fw_flux`. Fixed to emit genuinely raw `FFIELDS.h` state; MITgcm rebuilt and rerun to produce a corrected capture (`*_kppmix_extend_rawflux_fix.nc`). Also fixed a missing `selectPenetratingSW` mapping in `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`. Both independently verified by Bob and Richard (round 0/1 reviews: APPROVE_WITH_FIXES, all Must Fix items resolved).
2. **Remaining residual (bo t=9, bosol all 10 timesteps)**: proven by both Bob and Richard (via monkeypatch) to be fully attributable to 1DMIX-017's EOS pressure-derivative bug — tracked and fixed separately (above).

### Verification and remaining bounds
With 1DMIX-017 fixed, re-ran `conda run -n ecco python3 scripts/run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py KPP_port_validation/inputs_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_kpp_inputs_1D_10_kppmix_extend_rawflux_fix.nc` against the real (non-monkeypatched) corrected capture: exit code 0, 10/10 columns processed, zero forcing-validation warnings. Direct NetCDF comparison: `bo` rel_err 0.0005-0.0051% (was: failed at t=9 with 1.27%), `bosol` rel_err 0.019-0.021% (was: failed at all 10 with 4.7-5.2%) — both well within the 1% oracle at all 10/10 timesteps, satisfying this issue's stated acceptance criterion exactly. Independently reproduced by Richard in the same review pass that closed 1DMIX-017.

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). Depended on 1DMIX-017 (above); depends transitively on the round-0/1 MITgcm-instrumentation fixes recorded in this issue's own history (not separately filed).

## RESOLVED: KPP Python port's `hbl` diagnosis is sensitive to razor-thin Rib/Ricr threshold crossings — characterized as an inherent floating-point sensitivity, not a port defect

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T07:10:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T09:15:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-019

### Issue
After fixing 1DMIX-018's harness bugs, a bounded residual remained in a full lab_sea rerun. Traced to its root: at one example column (lab_sea, t=1, x=19, y=4), `hbl` itself disagreed (Python 67.4 m vs. MITgcm 41.0 m) because the bulk Richardson number `Rib` at one level (`Rib[3]=0.299`) sits 0.3% below the critical threshold `Ricr=0.3` in the Python port's computation — a razor-thin miss that causes the boundary-layer search to lock onto a much deeper crossing instead.

### Resolution and justification
Verified the crossing-search algorithm is an exact match to MITgcm's real Fortran logic (`kpp_routines.F:655`, "first exceedance, lock in permanently," including never revisiting `kbl`). Tested and disproven four further hypotheses with hard evidence: level-dependent `viscArNr` (ruled out via the experiment's own `data` namelist), vertical Ri-smoothing (`num_v_smooth_Ri=0`, a configured no-op), and — checking whether this was a repeat of 1DMIX-012's newly-discovered float-parsing bug — grepped the full raw lab_sea capture (1,782,307 `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` lines) for the same bare-exponent pattern: zero matches, ruling that out too.

**Decisive confirming step**: added temporary debug instrumentation (reverted after use, never committed) directly inside `kpp_calc.F` dumping the real `shsq`/`dVsq`/`dbloc`/`Ritop` feeding `bldepth`'s bulk-Richardson search for this exact column, rebuilt lab_sea via Docker (short 4-timestep run), and compared against the Python port's own independently-recomputed values for the same column/timestep (captured earlier via direct instrumentation of `diagnose_bl_depth`). **The two independently-computed `Ritop` profiles match to 4 significant figures at every level** (e.g. level 6: MITgcm `9.733e-2` vs. Python `9.733e-2`; level 12: MITgcm `4.952` vs. Python `4.949`) — confirming the Python port's own Rib-determining computation is fundamentally correct, not corrupted or buggy. The remaining tiny (4th-5th significant figure) numerical differences — an unavoidable consequence of two independent floating-point implementations computing the same physics via different code paths (EOS calls, gradient formulas, etc.) — get massively amplified into a large `hbl` difference only because this specific level's `Rib` happens to sit within a fraction of a percent of the hard `Ricr` cutoff. This is a genuine, inherent property of any finite-precision implementation of a hard-thresholded diagnostic, not a fixable defect in either the search algorithm or the underlying formulas.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full 999-timestep, all-320-column statistics (from 1DMIX-018's fix) already quantify the bounded impact: `hbl` median diff 0.0042 m, only 0.246%/0.605% of ocean columns exceed 5 m/1 m diff; `visc_az` median diff exactly 0.0, only 0.024% of active cells exceed 1e-2 abs diff. This is the accepted, documented characterization per this issue's own acceptance criterion (option (b): "document as a characterized, bounded limitation... not a broad or systematic port defect"). Full pytest suite: `50 passed, 3 skipped` throughout. Temporary Fortran instrumentation reverted (never committed); temporary Docker build/run/input directories (~120 MB) deleted after extracting results.

**Addendum (2026-09-19, 6-month `lab_sea` rerun, 655,200 wet column-timesteps, ~4.4x the original sample)**: same mechanism, confirmed at a larger and more extreme scale, not a new or different cause. Bulk statistics remain excellent (`hbl` median diff *improved* to 0.0011 m; `visc_az` median diff still exactly 0.0), but the tail is measurably wider than the original 41-day sample suggested: 3.6%/1.5%/0.31% of wet column-timesteps now exceed 1 m/5 m/20 m `hbl` diff (vs. 0.605%/0.246% exceeding 1 m/5 m in the original sample), and the single worst case (`t=3341,x=4,y=12`) disagrees by 112.5 m (MITgcm: full-column convection, `hbl`=117.5 m = the water depth at all 7 wet levels; Python: 5 m) — far larger than the original write-up's illustrative 67.4 m vs. 41.0 m example. Directly inspected this worst case's raw captured state: a near-uniformly-mixed, weakly-stratified polar water column (theta spans only 0.03°C, salt only 0.004 psu across all 7 wet levels) — i.e. a column sitting right at the edge of static instability along its *entire* depth, not just at one specific crossing level. This is the same Rib/Ricr floating-point-threshold mechanism in a more extreme regime: when the whole profile is nearly degenerate rather than just one level, a rounding-level difference anywhere in it can flip the diagnosis between "shallow boundary layer" and "fully convected column" instead of just shifting one crossing depth by a few meters. Widens this issue's own quantified bound (larger tail than originally stated) but does not change its conclusion (inherent finite-precision sensitivity of a hard-thresholded diagnostic, not a port defect) — recorded here rather than as a new issue, since the mechanism and evidence are the same, just from a longer, more representative sample.

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). No source files changed for this issue (investigation-only; the algorithm and formulas were confirmed correct, not modified).

---

## RESOLVED: Standalone KPPMIX harness diverges from full-model MITgcm at the deepest grid levels — root cause was a silent float-parsing bug in the capture pipeline, not physics

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-16T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T08:50:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-012

### Issue
A standalone-subroutine harness (`mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_standalone_driver/`) compiles and calls `KPPMIX` directly, fed by inputs from a real instrumented full-model run. Across 10 captured timesteps (1D_ocean_ice_column), `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t` diverged from the full model by a bounded but real amount (~5e-3 m²/s) at the deepest 4-5 grid levels, from timestep 1 onward. The original hypothesis ("boundary layer reaches the domain bottom") was disproven by direct measurement (`hbl≈33m`, nowhere near the 23-level domain's actual bottom); four further hypotheses (stale memory, bathymetry truncation, level-dependent viscosity, vertical Ri-smoothing) were each tested and disproven with hard evidence.

### Resolution and justification
Added temporary, throwaway debug instrumentation directly inside `kpp_calc.F` (reverted after use, never committed) dumping the exact `shsq`/`dbloc`/`Ritop` values at the point they are passed into `KPPMIX`. This revealed the raw MITgcm text output at the affected levels contains genuinely nonzero values (e.g. `dbloc≈0.001-0.003`, `Ritop≈8-17`) — completely different from the `0.0` that the parsed NetCDF showed. Root cause found in `scripts/parse_mitgcm_split.py`: Fortran's fixed-width `E`-format drops the "E" character when an exponent needs 3 digits to fit the field width (e.g. `0.105188567206-104` instead of `...E-104`), which happens for the vanishingly tiny (but physically real) `shsq` values at these genuinely quiescent deep levels. Python's `float()` cannot parse the "E"-less form and raises `ValueError`, which every call site in the parser caught via a blanket `except (ValueError, IndexError): continue` — silently dropping the *entire* line, including the otherwise-valid sibling fields `dbloc`/`Ritop`, leaving pre-initialized zeros in their place. The standalone driver, fed these wrongly-zeroed values as input, correctly (per `Ri_iwmix`'s real formula, `kpp_routines.F:1206-1208`) computes KPP's maximum-shear-instability diffusivity ceiling for a degenerate `Ri=0/0` case — mathematically confirmed by hand to reproduce the mismatched value (`viscArNr(1) + difm0 = 1.93e-5 + 0.005 = 0.0050193`) exactly. The full model, using its own correct (never mis-parsed) internal values, correctly computes background-only mixing since the real Richardson number there is not actually degenerate.

Fixed `parse_mitgcm_split.py` with a `_ffloat()` helper that also accepts Fortran's bare-exponent form, applied at all 25 `float(parts[N])` call sites. Found and fixed a second, independent bug in `scripts/export_kpp_input_for_fortran.py`: it used `!r` (repr) directly on numpy scalars without casting to `float()` first; under NumPy 2.x, `repr(np.float64(x))` includes an `np.float64(...)` wrapper that Fortran's list-directed `READ` cannot parse, causing a `Bad real number in item 1 of list input` runtime error once the first bug's fix exposed genuinely tiny values that needed export. Fixed all affected call sites to cast via `float(...)` first, matching the pattern already correctly used elsewhere in the same file. Proactively applied the identical `_ffloat()` fix to `scripts/parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py`, which shares the exact same vulnerable pattern (not yet observed to cause a problem there, but the same latent bug).

### Verification and remaining bounds
Re-parsed the original raw MITgcm STDOUT (`1D_ocean_ice_column/output_kppmix_extend/output.txt`, unchanged) with the fixed parser, regenerated the canonical `KPP_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_kpp_{inputs,outputs}_1D_10_kppmix_extend.nc` (confirmed `Ritop`/`buoy_freq_sq` now genuinely nonzero at the previously-zeroed levels), re-exported to the standalone driver's input format, reran the standalone driver, and regenerated `outputs_from_mitgcm_standalone/mitgcm_kpp_outputs_standalone_1D_10.nc`. **Result: max diff across all 10 timesteps and all 23 levels is 1.77e-17 — floating-point roundoff, an exact match.** Full pytest suite: `50 passed, 3 skipped` (unrelated pre-existing gap), confirmed after each fix. Temporary debug instrumentation in `kpp_calc.F` reverted (never committed); temporary Docker build/output directories (~115 MB) deleted after extracting results.

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). Files changed: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/parse_mitgcm_split.py`, `scripts/parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py`, `scripts/export_kpp_input_for_fortran.py`; regenerated (gitignored `*.nc`, not tracked by git but part of this project's working data) `KPP_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_kpp_{inputs,outputs}_1D_10_kppmix_extend.nc` and `outputs_from_mitgcm_standalone/mitgcm_kpp_outputs_standalone_1D_10.nc`.

## RESOLVED: Root MITgcm-comparison test suite had no generated `.npz` inputs (and a stale script reference)

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-15T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T08:30:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-011

### Issue
Root `tests/test_kpp_mitgcm_validation.py` collected 16 parametrized cases (1-D ocean-ice column, Lab Sea grid) but all 16 skipped: its docstring referenced a `parse_mitgcm_kpp_validation.py` script and `.npz` fixture format that never existed anywhere in the repo. Deeper investigation while fixing it found the file could never have run even with data present: a stale `sys.path` from before the 2026-09-18 repo reorganization, and a call to `KPPDriver.compute_mixing(..., f_coriolis=...)` — `f_coriolis` never existed in the real signature (`coriol`) — plus a `get_kpp_parameters_for_experiment` `TODO` that never actually configured lab_sea-specific EOS parameters despite claiming to.

### Resolution and justification
Took option (b) from this issue's own proposed action: rewrote the test to consume `scripts/run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py::run_python_kpp_on_dataset`'s real, already-proven output directly (the same function used for every other KPP validation capture in this project, including the land-masking and per-column bathymetry-truncation fixes from 1DMIX-018), rather than resurrecting a `.npz` format and a driver-API calling convention that predates significant, real fixes (1DMIX-013's forcing validation, 1DMIX-018's harness bugs). Uses the already-captured `1D_ocean_ice_column` (10 timesteps) and `lab_sea` (subsampled to 20 of 999 timesteps for test speed) NetCDF files. Tolerances are set from the real, measured agreement levels recorded in this project's own issue history (1DMIX-013, -018, -019), not aspirational bit-level values that were never actually achieved for a multi-column case.

### Verification and remaining bounds
`conda run -n ecco python3 -m pytest MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests/test_kpp_mitgcm_validation.py -v` → `4 passed` (real assertions executing for the first time, not 16 skips). Full configured scientific suite (`Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests`): `50 passed, 3 skipped` (the 3 skips are the pre-existing, separately-tracked 1DMIX-010 h5py gap, unrelated). Structural suite: `EXECUTED PASS` (`devel-loop/loop_state/verification/685e8b53a2eede7fdf062bb583341812b20d67a52e62fbfda0ca4bc3322f920f.json`). Classified `harness_change`/`documentation` (test-only, no scientific kernel touched); not independently reviewed by Richard, consistent with that category's acceptance bar.

## RESOLVED: KPP replay harness (`run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`) had no per-column bathymetry/land handling, corrupting every multi-column comparison

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T07:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T08:00:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-018

### Issue
The KPP replay harness had never been run against a real multi-column (bathymetry-varying) capture before — every prior use was `1D_ocean_ice_column` (single column, no land, uniform full depth). Running it for the first time against the existing, previously-uncompared `KPP_port_validation/inputs_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_kpp_inputs_lab_sea_1000_0820T0946.nc` (20×16 grid, 999 timesteps) surfaced two compounding harness bugs: (1) the land-skip check assumed NaN-masked land, but this capture masks land with exact `0.0` at every level; (2) the driver had no per-column seafloor awareness — it always assumed the full `nz`-level grid was wet, so for any column shallower than the grid's deepest level (the overwhelming majority of real ocean columns), the boundary-layer search ran past the true bottom into MITgcm's zero-padded region and settled on a nonsensical `hbl` at the domain's deepest level.

### Resolution and justification
Added `_is_land_column()` (checks NaN **or** all-zero) and `_wet_level_count()` (counts real levels from the surface down to the first exact-zero level, matching MITgcm's own zero-padding convention) to `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`, applied in both the parallel (`_process_column`) and serial code paths; profile outputs zero-padded back to the full grid depth below the real seafloor to match MITgcm's own output convention there (confirmed directly: MITgcm's own `visc_az`/`ghat` are also exactly 0 below a column's real seafloor). Point verification before the full rerun: a known column with true depth 45 m (top 4 of 23 levels wet) gave `hbl=5450.0` (the grid's deepest level) untruncated vs. `hbl=45.0` (exact match to MITgcm) truncated to its 4 real levels.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full 999-timestep, all-320-column rerun (`149,850` real ocean column-timesteps, `169,830` correctly-skipped land column-timesteps): `hbl` median diff **0.0042 m**, mean **0.080 m**, p95 **0.165 m**, p99 **0.55 m**, max **53.9 m** (only 0.246%/0.605% of ocean columns exceed 5 m/1 m diff respectively) — down from a pre-fix 11-timestep sample showing mean diff 2984 m and a physically-impossible max of 5450 m. Regression-checked against the existing `1D_ocean_ice_column` route (single-column, always-full-depth, unaffected by either fix by construction): identical results to before (`HBL max diff 0.0128 m`, unchanged) — confirms zero regression on the only other exercised route. Full pytest suite: `46 passed, 3 skipped`, no regressions. A small, real, bounded residual remains after both fixes and is tracked separately (see open issue 1DMIX-019) since it is a genuine scientific question, not a harness artifact.

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). Files changed: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`.

## RESOLVED: GGL90 validation harness's `run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` derives `dt` from the netCDF time coordinate (raw iteration index) instead of the real MITgcm `deltaT`

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-16T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-19T06:10:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-015

### Issue
`scripts/run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` computed `dt = float(np.diff(inputs_ds['time'].values)[0])`, but the captured `time` coordinate is the raw MITgcm iteration index (0..19), not seconds — giving `dt=1.0` instead of the real `deltaT=1200.` for the `vermix` experiment. This understated the GGL90 port's real single-step-replay agreement (`tke_after` looked worse than it actually was); harness-only, did not affect `mixing_length`/`diff_kz` (dt-independent) or the port's own physics.

### Resolution and justification
Classified `harness_change` (Fortran instrumentation only, no scientific kernel touched — same classification as the precedent 1DMIX-016 fix to this same file), matching the Arch operating contract's acceptance for that category: structural suite plus actual harness failure modes, no mandatory independent review.

Added a `PARAM_deltaT=dTtracerLev(1)` write to `mitgcm_verification_mods/{ggl90_mods,vermix/code_validation}/ggl90_calc.F`'s existing `GGL90_MODEL_PARAMETERS` dump (both copies kept identical, as established by 1DMIX-016), citing the exact variable the TKE forward step itself uses (`deltaTloc = dTtracerLev(kSrf)`, confirmed by reading the update loop directly) rather than the base scalar `deltaT`, in case a future config sets a level-dependent tracer timestep. Rebuilt and reran the instrumented `vermix` experiment via the documented Docker helpers (`experiment_compile.sh vermix -mods .../vermix/code_validation -build build_docker_ggl90_1dmix015 -j 8`, then `experiment_run_no_compile.sh vermix input_ggl90_merged -build build_docker_ggl90_1dmix015 -output output_docker_ggl90_1dmix015`; native arm64 host, no `arch -arm64` wrapper needed). Confirmed `PARAM_deltaT= 0.12000000E+04` in the new STDOUT capture. Reparsed via `scripts/parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py`; new capture saved alongside (not replacing) the originals: `GGL90_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_ggl90_{inputs,outputs}_vermix_20_1dmix015_fix.nc` (existing `_vermix_20.nc`/`_1dmix016_fix.nc` files untouched — never opened for writing). Fixed `scripts/run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` to read `dt = float(inputs_ds.attrs['deltaT'])` (raising `ValueError` with a clear message if the attribute is absent — an older, pre-fix capture — rather than silently guessing) instead of differencing the iteration-index time coordinate.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Reran the acceptance oracle (`scripts/run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` + `scripts/compare_ggl90.py`) against the new real (non-ad-hoc, non-monkeypatched) capture: `tke_after` mismatches **40/520 → 2/520** (max_rel 4.872e-02), matching the issue's ad-hoc-demonstrated prediction (max_rel 0.049) almost exactly. `visc_az`/`diff_kz`/`mixing_length` unchanged at 0/520 (dt-independent, as expected). Full focused suite (`conda run -n ecco python3 -m pytest Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests -q`) → `46 passed, 3 skipped`, identical to pre-fix (zero regressions). Structural suite (`tools/esx/verify.py --suite structural --owner arch --fresh`) → `EXECUTED PASS` (evidence: `devel-loop/loop_state/verification/3214f70e4ae96a6959771450132e66d6d0dd5bb8ab8e620491fe28b28bc4a043.json`). Not independently reviewed by Richard, consistent with this category's acceptance (harness_change, no scientific kernel touched) and the 1DMIX-016 precedent's identical classification.

### Traceability
No commit yet (pending owner authorization). Files changed (this repo): `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F`, `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/vermix/code_validation/ggl90_calc.F`, `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` — all three git-tracked in this repo (unlike the stale "untracked" note in 1DMIX-016's own record, from before the repo reorg). MITgcm build/run artifacts outside this project's git tree: `/Users/ifenty/git_repo_others/MITgcm/verification/vermix/{build,output}_docker_ggl90_1dmix015/`.

## RESOLVED: GGL90 density gradient used in-situ instead of potential density

**Date Identified**: 2026-07-20T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-07-20T00:00:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-001

### Issue
GGL90 computed N² (buoyancy frequency squared) from in-situ density gradients. MITgcm's `grad_sigma.F` / `do_oceanic_phys.F` instead evaluate adjacent levels at a common reference pressure (potential density) before differencing. Including compressibility effects overestimated stratification in deep water (>1000 m) and could over-suppress mixing there.

### Resolution and justification
Added `compute_ggl90_buoyancy_frequency_squared()` in `1D_Mixing_Model/main/eos.py`; updated `main/mixing_adapter.py` and `GGL90/ggl90_core_driver.py` to call it, matching MITgcm's potential-density convention.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Migrated from the Three-Man-Team era `potential_bugs_and_inconsistencies.md`; no fresh ESX verification receipt exists for this historical fix (no `tools/esx/verify.py` evidence hash) — treat as source-evidence-supported, not re-verified under the current regime. Full derivation archived (1DMIX-047) at `docs/OLD_MARKDOWN_NO_LONGER_NEEDED/three-man-team-archive/GGL90_DENSITY_GRADIENT_FIX_SUMMARY.md` and `docs/OLD_MARKDOWN_NO_LONGER_NEEDED/three-man-team-archive/DENSITY_GRADIENT_ANALYSIS.md` (moved out of top-level `docs/` by 1DMIX-047; previously these two paths lacked the `docs/` prefix and this citation predated the actual move).

### Traceability
No commit SHA recorded (root was not git-tracked at the time). Related archived record: `docs/OLD_MARKDOWN_NO_LONGER_NEEDED/three-man-team-archive/potential_bugs_and_inconsistencies.md`.

## RESOLVED: KPP buoyancy-gradient denominator convention question

**Date Identified**: 2026-07-20T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-07-20T00:00:00Z
**Status**: False Positive
**UUID**: 1DMIX-006

### Issue
Suspected sign and/or denominator error (double-counting `rho_const`) in `compute_buoyancy_gradients()` relative to MITgcm's KPP density-gradient formula.

### Resolution and justification
Verified against MITgcm `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F` lines 1886-1933 (`DBLOC = gravity * (RHOK - RHOKM1) / (RHOK + rhoConst)`, where RHOK/RHOKM1 are density *anomalies*). Python's `dbloc = gravity * (rho_deep - rho_shal_at_deep) / rho_deep` matches exactly once `rho_deep` is recognized as the full in-situ density (`rho_anom + rho_const`) — no double counting.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Source-level comparison only; no numerical regression receipt.

### Traceability
No commit SHA (no code change made).

## RESOLVED: `h5py` missing from the `ecco` environment blocked one test file's collection

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-15T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-15T00:00:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-010

### Issue
`1D_Mixing_Model/tests/test_mitgcm_kpp_lab_sea_validation.py` failed at pytest *collection* with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'h5py'` under the `ecco` conda environment. Fixing the dependency alone then exposed two further, independent authoring bugs in the same file: (1) `generate_test_cases(pytest.lazy_fixture('mitgcm_data'))` at module level — `pytest.lazy_fixture` is not real pytest API (it required a long-deprecated third-party plugin) and this could never have worked, since a fixture's value does not exist at collection time; (2) the `kpp_params` fixture built `KPPParameters()` with library defaults (`rho_const=1029.0`, `gravity=9.81`) while the test's own assertion expected the lab_sea-specific MITgcm values (`rho_const=1027.0`, `gravity=9.8156`) — the fixture never actually configured lab_sea parameters despite its docstring saying it would.

### Resolution and justification
1. `pip install h5py` into the `ecco` conda environment (approved by the Project Owner as part of closing out ESX-migration issues).
2. Rewrote the parametrization using the standard `pytest_generate_tests(metafunc)` hook, loading the HDF5 dataset directly (via a shared `_load_mitgcm_data()` helper) instead of misusing a fixture at collection time; parametrizing with an empty case list when the dataset file is absent, so pytest reports a clean skip instead of a collection error.
3. Fixed the `kpp_params` fixture to construct `KPPParameters(rho_const=1027.0, gravity=9.8156)` explicitly, matching the lab_sea `data` values the test itself already asserted.

### Verification and remaining bounds
`conda run -n ecco python3 -m pytest 1D_Mixing_Model/tests/test_mitgcm_kpp_lab_sea_validation.py -v` → `1 passed, 3 skipped` (`test_kpp_parameters` now passes; `test_kpp_column`, `test_mitgcm_data_loaded`, `test_all_columns_summary` correctly skip because the underlying `mitgcm_instrumentation/lab_sea_kpp_data.h5` dataset does not exist in this repo — that remaining data gap is tracked separately as open issue 1DMIX-011, not fabricated as passing here). Full suite: `1D_Mixing_Model/tests tests` → `42 passed, 19 skipped, 0 failed` (2026-09-15). `esx/project.json` no longer needs to `--ignore` this file.

### Traceability
No commit SHA (root not git-tracked; `1D_Mixing_Model/` is a separate nested git repo — this fix should be committed there under that repo's own commit policy, not yet done pending Project Owner go-ahead per `commit_policy.mode = owner_authorization`).
