## RESOLVED: GGL90 alpha-minimum resolution-ratio metric re-verified against shipped scripts

**Date Identified**: 2026-08-10T00:00:00Z (flagged during Step 4 reorg closeout as explicitly separate from the reorg itself)
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-15T00:00:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-009

### Issue
The archived `handoff/BUILD-LOG.md` 2026-08-10 entry flagged that the alpha-minimum section's "resolution-ratio metric looked inconsistent" and was never re-checked before that reorg closed out.

### Resolution and justification
Traced the actual metric implementation: `1D_Mixing_Model/scripts/analysis/diagnose_oscillation_threshold.py::compute_gradient_length_scale` computes `resolution_ratio = median_length_scale / mean(dz)` and `under_resolved_fraction = mean(length_scales < 2·mean(dz))` — exactly the "median $L/\Delta z$ + fraction of under-resolved layers" metric the package description's own "Note on metric choice" paragraph (§Minimum Alpha for Oscillation-Free Solutions) says it deliberately uses *instead of* a single minimum $L/\Delta z$ (explicitly called "not informative" there, because a TKE profile's steepest single transition drives the minimum toward zero at every α regardless). The doc and the script already agree with each other — the inconsistency BUILD-LOG flagged does not currently reproduce; either it was fixed during the Step 3 GGL90 doc work (2026-08-06, before this note was written) without the open item being explicitly closed, or the note was raised out of caution and the metric was already correct.

A separate, unrelated script, `1D_Mixing_Model/scripts/analysis/compute_alpha_min.py`, computes a grid-Reynolds-number **stability** criterion (`Re_grid = Δz²/(α·κ_m·Δt) < 2`) — a different metric entirely, and one the package description explicitly disclaims as the actual mechanism ("this is a resolution issue, not a stability issue"). That script's output is not what the documented table or the "Minimum: use α ≥ 5" recommendation is based on; its similar name/location alongside the resolution-based scripts is a plausible source of the original "looked inconsistent" concern, but it does not itself contradict the documented resolution-ratio table.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Freshly executed today: `cd 1D_Mixing_Model && conda run -n ecco python3 scripts/analysis/diagnose_oscillation_threshold.py` (arctic convection scenario, α ∈ {1,3,5,7.5,10,15,20,30,50}). Under-resolved fractions reproduced **exactly** match the documented table: 0.53, 0.27, 0.06, 0.06, 0.12, 0.12, 0.12, 0.12, 0.12 for α=1.0 through 50.0 respectively; α=50.0's full row also matches exactly (TKE roughness 2.15e-04, normalized 0.055, oscillation count 1). This is a real, independent reproduction, not a re-read of the same document. Not verified: whether the same holds for scenarios other than arctic convection, or whether `compute_alpha_min.py`'s stability-based framing should be relabeled/removed to avoid future confusion (left as a documentation-clarity nit, not reopened as a correctness issue).

### Traceability
No commit SHA (no file changed; investigation only).

## RESOLVED: Python GGL90 port's mixing-length limiter applied `mixing_length_min` as an incorrect blanket floor for `mxlMaxFlag=3`

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-16T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-16T00:00:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-014

### Issue
Found on the first end-to-end run of the new GGL90 full-pipeline validation harness (mirrors the existing KPP pipeline; `mitgcm_verification_mods/ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F` instrumentation + `scripts/{parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split,run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input}.py`, run via MITgcm's own `vermix` verification experiment). `GGL90MixingLength.compute()` (`1D_Mixing_Model/GGL90/ggl90_scheme_specific.py`) applied `mixing_length[k] = max(mixing_length[k], self.params.mixing_length_min)` unconditionally to every level for every `mxl_max_flag` value. Initial triage (before implementation) hypothesized this floor was wrong for both `mxlMaxFlag` 2 and 3; the real root cause, found during implementation, was narrower and different: MITgcm's `ggl90_mixinglength.F:381-416` branches `IF (locMxlMaxFlag.EQ.3) ... ELSE ...` — flags 0/1/2 (`ELSE` branch) do apply a genuine blanket `MAX(L,min)` floor (so the pre-fix Python code was actually correct there), while flag 3 specifically (this project's documented ECCOv4r4 default) floors only the *reciprocal*, via `1/MAX(SQRT(L(k)*mxLength_Dn(k)), min)`, and never reassigns `L` itself.

### Resolution and justification
`1D_Mixing_Model/GGL90/ggl90_scheme_specific.py::GGL90MixingLength.compute` now branches on `mxl_max_flag==3` vs. `{0,1,2}` exactly as the real Fortran does, seeding `mixing_length[0]=mixing_length_min` explicitly (previously an accidental side effect of the removed blanket floor). Three additional bugs found and fixed during independent verification against `ggl90_mixinglength.F`/`ggl90_calc.F`, all confirmed against exact cited Fortran lines: (1) `_limit_method_2` was missing the bottom-boundary clamp (`ggl90_mixinglength.F:264-267`) — added, and its return signature changed to `(result, mxl_down)` since the flag-3 formula needs `mxl_down`; (2) `_limit_method_0`/`_limit_method_1` incorrectly included the surface level in their loops (real Fortran loops are `DO k=2,Nr`) — fixed to `range(1, nz)`; (3) `compute_tke_dissipation` divided by `mixing_length` instead of multiplying by `r_mixing_length` (`ggl90_calc.F:601-603`) — required because `r_mixing_length != 1/mixing_length` for flag 3; wired `r_mixing_length` through `GGL90Driver.step_tke_forward`/`compute_mixing` (previously computed but discarded). A separate, unrelated bug was also fixed in `step_tke_forward`: the `c[k]` tridiagonal coefficient used `dr_c[k]` where it must use `dr_c[k-1]` (same as `a[k]`), per `ggl90_calc.F:691-694,713-716` both using the identical `recip_drC(k)` term.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Acceptance oracle (`scripts/run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` + `scripts/compare_ggl90.py` against `GGL90_port_validation/outputs_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_ggl90_outputs_vermix_20.nc`, 20 timesteps, single column, `mxlMaxFlag=3`): `mixing_length` 489/520 mismatched (>1% rel, max_rel 26x) -> **0/520** (max_rel 8.9e-4); `diff_kz` 26/520 -> **0/520** (max_rel 2.3e-3). `visc_az` (476/520) and `tke_after` (40/520) remain mismatched but are independently traced to two separate, pre-existing harness defects (not the Python port) — tracked as open issues **1DMIX-015** and **1DMIX-016**. Richard independently re-verified the central Fortran branch claim by reading `ggl90_mixinglength.F:381-416` and `GGL90_OPTIONS.h:36` directly, re-ran the acceptance oracle fresh (own `/tmp` output, reproduced Bob's numbers exactly), built an independent from-scratch numeric witness at `(t=5, level k=2)` of the real capture (old formula: exactly 3.000000 m, 2587% error vs. MITgcm's 0.111638 m; new formula: 0.111553 m, 0.076% error), grepped for regressions from the two changed function signatures (none found), and ran the full focused suite fresh under his own owner identity (46 passed, 3 skipped [pre-existing 1DMIX-010 h5py gap, unrelated], 0 failed). Verdict: **APPROVE**, must_fix: none. New regression test `1D_Mixing_Model/tests/test_ggl90_mixing_length.py` (4 tests); Richard confirmed 2 of the 4 would have failed against the pre-fix code by hand-checking. Non-blocking follow-up noted by Richard: no dedicated unit test yet isolates the `dr_c[k-1]` fix or the `r_mixing_length` wiring in isolation (currently covered only by the full oracle pipeline).

### Traceability
Files changed: `1D_Mixing_Model/GGL90/ggl90_scheme_specific.py`, `1D_Mixing_Model/GGL90/ggl90_core_driver.py`, `1D_Mixing_Model/tests/test_ggl90_mixing_length.py` (new), `1D_Mixing_Model/docs/GGL90/GGL90_package_description.tex` (corrected the "Bottom boundary condition"/"Regularization" sections, which had stated the blanket-floor claim as universal — the likely documentation-level origin of the original bug). Committed `09a7a72` in the `1D_Mixing_Model` repo ("Fix GGL90 mixing-length limiter's incorrect blanket floor for mxlMaxFlag=3"), with Project Owner authorization (commit_policy=owner_authorization). Evidence: `devel-loop/loop_state/verification/bf33f4fbd3a775ec4e70c60e68546f343eb4d12001a5f6298c7084bffcbada74.json` (Bob, focused suite), `devel-loop/loop_state/verification/5eb21b12995d20f0061c6634c18c0460c73fa6d8a24167a949a939484a083854.json` (Richard, focused suite, fresh/independent), `devel-loop/loop_state/maintenance/dd7b02bd8c61d1160c519e90e81aa2f6e2b1c1ba0e56c9bf91c5f59e70548fec.json` (sealed documentation disposition, confirmed non-stale by Richard via `doc_contract.py check` -> valid:true). Related open issues: 1DMIX-015, 1DMIX-016 (both harness-only, discovered during this fix's verification, do not affect this resolution's validity).

## RESOLVED: GGL90 validation harness's `visc_az` output column captured `GGL90visctmp` (a diffusivity-floor intermediate), not the real exported viscosity

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-16T00:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-17T23:19:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-016

### Issue
The instrumented `ggl90_calc.F`'s `GGL90_OUTPUT_VALIDATION` call passed `GGL90visctmp` (`MAX(KappaM_raw, diffKrNrS(k))`, a diffusivity-floored intermediate used only to derive `GGL90diffKr`) as the dumped `"visc_az"` column, instead of the real exported momentum viscosity (`KappaM`, floored by `viscArNr(k)`). Confirmed by several mismatched captured values equaling exactly `1e-5` = `diffKzS`, not `viscAz=1e-4`.

### Resolution and justification
`KappaM` itself could not be substituted directly: it is a 2D scratch array `(i,j)` overwritten every iteration of the outer `k` loop, so it never retains a per-level history by the time `GGL90_OUTPUT_VALIDATION` is called after the loop. `GGL90viscArU`/`GGL90viscArV` (the persisted, per-level face-interpolated diagnostics) were considered but rejected: `vermix`'s domain is `sNx=sNy=1` with `no_slip_sides=.TRUE.` and no `bathyFile` (default flat-bottom, single active column) — their horizontal-face masking semantics are an unnecessary complication with no counterpart in the Python port's cell-centered 1-D grid. Added a new persisted 3D array `GGL90viscOutput(i,j,k)` (declared/zeroed alongside `GGL90visctmp`, populated immediately after `KappaM`'s own `viscArNr` floor is applied) and passed it to `GGL90_OUTPUT_VALIDATION` instead; also renamed the subroutine's dummy argument `visctmp`→`viscAzOut` for clarity. Applied identically to both tracked copies of the file (`mitgcm_verification_mods/ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F`, the issue's anchor, and `mitgcm_verification_mods/vermix/code_validation/ggl90_calc.F`, the copy `experiment_compile.sh -mods` actually builds from — confirmed byte-identical before this fix; no symlink connects them, a minor pre-existing repo-hygiene gap not fixed here since out of scope).

### Verification and remaining bounds
Rebuilt the instrumented `vermix` experiment via the project's Docker helpers (`arch -arm64` not needed; this host runs native arm64) — `experiment_compile.sh vermix -mods .../mitgcm_verification_mods/vermix/code_validation -build build_docker_ggl90_1dmix016 -j 8`, then `experiment_run_no_compile.sh vermix input_ggl90_merged -build build_docker_ggl90_1dmix016 -output output_docker_ggl90_1dmix016` (20 timesteps, `mxlMaxFlag=3`, `useGGL90=.TRUE.`; first attempt used the wrong `-mods`/input dir — a sparse `ggl90_mods/` missing `SIZE.h` et al. hit MITgcm's deliberate-error template `SIZE.h`, and the default `input/` runs KPP not GGL90 — both corrected before the run used as evidence). Reparsed via `scripts/parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py`, reran `scripts/run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` + `scripts/compare_ggl90.py`: `visc_az` **476/520 → 0/520** (max_rel 9.0 → 7.6e-4, now comparable to `diff_kz`/`mixing_length`); `diff_kz`/`mixing_length` unchanged (0/520); `tke_after` unchanged (40/520, the separate open 1DMIX-015 dt-derivation bug, not in scope). Structural suite (`tools/esx/verify.py --suite structural`) and focused suite (`--suite focused`, full pytest) both `EXECUTED PASS` after the change (evidence: `devel-loop/loop_state/verification/786c815eb...685.json`, `.../72e9b6a3a...28b.json`). New capture saved alongside (not replacing) the original: `GGL90_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_ggl90_{inputs,outputs}_vermix_20_1dmix016_fix.nc`, `outputs_from_python/python_ggl90_outputs_vermix_20_1dmix016_fix.nc`; originals confirmed byte-unchanged (MD5) before/after. Not independently reviewed by Richard — classified `harness_change` (Fortran instrumentation only, no scientific kernel touched), whose acceptance per the Arch operating contract is the structural suite plus actual harness failure modes, both satisfied above.

### Traceability
Files changed: `mitgcm_verification_mods/ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F`, `mitgcm_verification_mods/vermix/code_validation/ggl90_calc.F` (both outside any Git repository — `mitgcm_verification_mods/` and the project root are untracked; no commit SHA applies). MITgcm build/run artifacts: `/Users/ifenty/git_repo_others/MITgcm/verification/vermix/{build,output}_docker_ggl90_1dmix016/` (outside this project, MITgcm's own untracked verification tree).

## RESOLVED: `global_oce_latlon` has only been verified for 4 timesteps — extend to a full year (one full periodic-forcing cycle)

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T17:00:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T17:05:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-027
**Anchors**: `mitgcm_verification_mods/global_oce_latlon/`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`

### Issue
`global_oce_latlon`'s KPP three-way comparison (1DMIX-022) had only ever been run for 4 timesteps — a smoke test validating the build/capture pipeline itself, not a representative sample of the experiment's actual periodic-climatological forcing (`externForcingCycle=31104000s` = 720 timesteps at `deltaTtracer=43200s`). Every real seasonal forcing transition (summer heating, winter cooling/convection) remained completely unexercised. This issue's own acceptance criterion: reconfigure for `nTimeSteps=720` (one full cycle), rerun, and compare with the same median/p95/p99/max rigor as `lab_sea`, either confirming agreement within bounds comparable to the 4-timestep sample's already-fixed issues, or surfacing new properly-evidenced issues for any real mismatch found.

### Resolution and justification
`global_oce_latlon/input_validation/data` was reconfigured to `nTimeSteps=720` (confirmed present via direct grep, independently by both Bob and Richard) and rerun through the already-working Docker capture pipeline. The resulting full-cycle three-way comparison (`n=1,666,800` wet-column-timesteps for `hbl`) initially found `hbl` disagreement scaling with sample size (median 0.00735 m, max 513.7 m) consistent with the already-understood Rib/Ricr threshold-crossing sensitivity (1DMIX-019's mechanism) — no new discrepancy *class*. A second, sharper pass over the same capture (asking not "how much do active cells disagree" but "how often is MITgcm's own `visc_az` exactly zero across an entire column when the unconditional background viscosity `kpp_routines.F:1206-1208` proves it cannot legitimately be absent") found a real, severe, previously-missed capture-truncation defect affecting 83.5% of wet column-timesteps. That finding was split out to its own issue, **1DMIX-035**, root-caused (an `OUTPUT_MIXING` truncation bug in the capture instrumentation, not a KPP physics bug) and fixed there. 1DMIX-035's own corrected rerun of this exact same full 720-timestep `global_oce_latlon` capture — reusing this issue's own reconfigured `nTimeSteps=720` setup — is the evidence that satisfies this issue's own acceptance criterion: 0.00% of wet columns now show the truncation defect (down from 83.5%), and `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t` median absolute difference is 0 (bit-exact) across all wet levels once the truncation is fixed, confirming agreement within bounds and leaving no new discrepancy class unaddressed at any forcing regime the original 4-timestep sample never reached.

Formal closure required a `kind=scientific_change` amendment (Bob agent_id `00c8d037-98d9-4654-9db4-0f30f01a556f`, Richard agent_id `92af85ea-a95b-435f-abe5-c284652d629d`, both confirmation-only, no new source change) purely to satisfy `tools/esx/loop_gate.py::check_done()`'s scientific-signature-drift check: this issue's `workflow.numerical_signature_at_start` was frozen at its very first `--prepare`, predating this session's 1DMIX-024/030/032/033/035 scientific_change closures, so the live scientific signature had genuinely drifted away from that frozen baseline — entirely due to those other, already-reviewed issues' own real changes (confirmed via direct mtime inspection: zero files under any scientific path changed after this issue's frozen baseline or after any later re-prepare). Both Bob and Richard independently re-confirmed, from scratch, that `nTimeSteps=720` is genuinely present, that 1DMIX-035's entry documents the corrected comparison reusing this exact capture, and that no new source change is outstanding under this issue's own scope.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest suite rerun fresh, independently, by both Bob and Richard (`pytest Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests -q`): 56 passed, 3 skipped, both times. `tools/esx/loop_gate.py --doctor`: PASS. Structural suite `EXECUTED PASS` under three distinct owner identities this closure (Arch, Bob's agent_id, Richard's agent_id). `tools/esx/final_verification.py run --owner arch --fresh`: **EXECUTED PASS** (receipt `36cd518ca6222eba8751ad93b2bbef2e62ea2a2133dc8123fc3ebff0863acf69`), scientific suite evidence `02c8215b3c90708938a6245d35584af1e928c84b32a45b9da79befa9f741c073`. Richard's verdict: APPROVE, no must_fix, `documentation_review.status=confirmed` (reused the pre-existing sealed documentation report from the earlier blocked closure attempt, independently re-validated `valid=true` against current source with no drift). Remaining bound: the `lab_sea` full-year Python-port replay (1DMIX-026, a separate open issue) was still running in the background at closure time and is unrelated to this issue's own `global_oce_latlon` scope.

### Traceability
No commit authorization for this turn; per project policy (`commit_policy.mode=owner_authorization`), commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree. Documentation report sealed: `devel-loop/loop_state/maintenance/121cb70ac778345e54c31f9fd02bb5aff2df30dd35a2afa6b790d5ebbfb40b07.json`. Orientation: `devel-loop/loop_state/maintenance/3ec6dac2d6db179ed5066a846c2fa70a739986616d38e70765cdb0184545e605.json`. Final verification receipt: `devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/36cd518ca6222eba8751ad93b2bbef2e62ea2a2133dc8123fc3ebff0863acf69.json`. Related issues: 1DMIX-022 (the original 4-timestep comparison this issue extended), 1DMIX-035 (the truncation-bug fix and corrected full-year rerun that satisfies this issue's own acceptance criterion), 1DMIX-019 (the Rib/Ricr threshold-sensitivity mechanism cited above), 1DMIX-024/030/032/033 (the other scientific_change closures whose changes caused this issue's own signature-drift finding).

## RESOLVED: GGL90 TKE-near-floor mismatches vs. real MITgcm — characterized as inert floating-point noise, not a fixable formula defect

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-20T03:50:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T17:40:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-031
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/shared_column_solver.py::solve_tridiagonal`; `model/src/solve_tridiagonal.F`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_core_driver.py::GGL90Driver.step_tke_forward`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_mixing_coefficients.py::compute_viscosity_diffusivity`; `pkg/ggl90/ggl90_calc.F`.

### Issue
Split out of 1DMIX-030's scenario-extension evidence. Several idealized scenarios showed `tke_after` mismatches against the real standalone-`GGL90_CALC`-driver, tiny in absolute magnitude (~1e-9 to ~4e-6 m²/s², many orders of magnitude below the scenarios' own peak TKE) but up to ~100% relative (since the underlying value is itself tiny, near `tke_min=1e-11`). 1DMIX-030 proposed, but did not prove, that this was caused by floating-point-ordering differences between the Python Thomas-algorithm solver (direct division) and MITgcm's `solve_tridiagonal.F` (reciprocal-then-multiply, a genuinely different, non-bit-identical IEEE-754 operation sequence).

### Resolution and justification
The originally-hypothesized solver-ordering mechanism was directly A/B tested and **disproven**: swapping in a line-by-line-faithful reimplementation of MITgcm's exact reciprocal-multiply solver produced identical agreement against real Fortran `GGL90_CALC` output (bit-for-bit the same to every printed digit) as the original direct-division solver. A second candidate mechanism (the standalone driver's `sigmaR` round-trip, which introduces genuine ~1e-19 absolute/~1e-16 relative floating-point noise into `Nsquare`) was tested and found real but insufficient — injecting the exact perturbation into the Python port's own `n_square` produced no change in the `tke_after` mismatch. The working characterization: every other GGL90 output field (`visc_az`, `diff_kz`, `mixing_length`) matches real MITgcm to ~4e-15 relative at every point across all 6 idealized scenarios, so the tridiagonal solve's own inputs are correct to ordinary double-precision roundoff; a ~1e-15-relative perturbation is negligible in absolute terms everywhere, but at points where the true TKE solution is itself tiny (near `tke_min`), that same negligible absolute perturbation appears as up to 100% relative error purely as an artifact of the relative-error metric's near-zero denominator — matching the project's own already-accepted precedent for the KPP Rib/Ricr threshold-sensitivity (1DMIX-019).

Formal closure used a confirmation-only Bob (agent_id `c6605ab4-e766-4bf1-bf27-b71f1debd0c5`) + Richard (agent_id `ffbdb3f9-0a12-4208-934d-5b0aa7b118d6`) redispatch, `kind=scientific_change` (chosen from the start to avoid the signature-drift complication discovered while closing 1DMIX-027). Bob independently re-derived both candidate-mechanism tests from primary sources with his own oracle variations (his own A/B rerun of `arctic_convection`, 5000 cumulative steps: `max_abs=2.119e-13`, ~7 orders of magnitude below the real mismatch; his own synthetic `sigmaR` round-trip: `max_abs=1.08e-19`, `max_rel=1.82e-16`, matching the issue's cited magnitude). Richard independently re-verified with differently-designed oracle tests (manufactured-tridiagonal-system solver A/B, exact-rational `sigmaR` round-trip), confirmed `solve_tridiagonal.F`/`CPP_OPTIONS.h` directly, confirmed the Rib/Ricr precedent's reasoning genuinely applies, and confirmed no source change is warranted. Zero working-tree diff vs. baseline throughout — no code fix, matching the recommendation in this issue's own text.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest suite rerun fresh, independently, by both Bob and Richard (`pytest Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests -q`): 56 passed, 3 skipped, both times. `tools/esx/loop_gate.py --doctor`: PASS. Structural suite `EXECUTED PASS` under three distinct owner identities (Arch, Bob's agent_id, Richard's agent_id). `tools/esx/final_verification.py run --owner arch --fresh`: **EXECUTED PASS** (receipt `feb58269066c7021667945251301017e99c01c105146de9a025cab481452812d`), scientific suite evidence `6d36c857a8eae725f16ceceb59797e79dba5593269fd6ae7729f6ca1894f63a1`. Richard's verdict: APPROVE, no must_fix, `documentation_review.status=confirmed`. Remaining bound: if this pattern recurs at a physically meaningful magnitude in a future, less-idealized experiment, the concrete next step (per this issue's own text) would be adding `Nsquare`/`verticalShear`/`TKEPrandtlNumber`/`a3d`/`b3d`/`c3d` dumps to the standalone driver's own instrumentation to directly diff every intermediate at the exact mismatched points.

### Traceability
No commit authorization for this turn; per project policy (`commit_policy.mode=owner_authorization`), commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree (no source files changed by this closure at all). Documentation report sealed: `devel-loop/loop_state/maintenance/ba12dd3b1f7f40aa7624dd06c9166ddda882b0eb647cb73e83d3a0827102d791.json`. Orientation: `devel-loop/loop_state/maintenance/2cb8f92ed106df62430e515083375770fc02947801ca449a62df773605eaa2b4.json`. Final verification receipt: `devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/feb58269066c7021667945251301017e99c01c105146de9a025cab481452812d.json`. Related issues: 1DMIX-030 (the mixing-length-ceiling fix this TKE finding was split out from), 1DMIX-019 (the KPP Rib/Ricr precedent this closure's reasoning matches), 1DMIX-024 (the standalone GGL90 driver whose `sigmaR` round-trip was tested as a candidate mechanism here).

## RESOLVED: GGL90 coverage should be systematically extended to other MITgcm verification experiments

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19

**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-20T19:31:17+00:00

**Status**: Resolved

**UUID**: 1DMIX-028

**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F`, `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py`, `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py`, `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/compare_ggl90.py`

### Issue

GGL90 had only ever been validated against two single-column, always-fully-wet MITgcm experiments (`vermix`, `1D_ocean_ice_column`). This issue tracked extending coverage to a genuinely new, real multi-column, real-bathymetry experiment (`global_oce_latlon/code_oad`, `useGGL90=.TRUE.`, 90x40 horizontal, 4 tiles), to test whether the harness and the port itself generalize beyond the previously-exercised single-column, single-tile, fully-wet case.

### Resolution and justification

Running GGL90 on `global_oce_latlon` for the first time surfaced three genuine, previously-invisible harness bugs, all fixed this session, purely additively, mirroring fixes already established for KPP earlier in the project:

1. **Multi-tile capture corruption**: `parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py` parsed only `myIter` from the `TIMESTEP=` header and silently discarded `BI=`/`BJ=`, so every dict key was `(i,j,k)` in tile-local indices with no tile identity — for a real 4-tile domain, different tiles' columns at the same local indices would silently overwrite each other. Ported KPP's existing `_TIMESTEP_HEADER`/`_remap_tiles_to_global` fix identically. Regression-tested against the existing single-tile `1D_ocean_ice_column` 11k-timestep capture: byte-identical output (`max_abs_diff=0.0` on every field).
2. **Land/partially-wet column handling absent**: `run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` had neither KPP's established `_is_land_column` (MITgcm's own zero-fill convention, not NaN) nor `_wet_level_count` (above-seafloor truncation) checks — every prior GGL90 capture was a single, always-fully-wet column, so this was invisible until now. Without it, 1285/3600 land columns and the below-seafloor padding of partially-wet columns fed degenerate all-zero/zero-padded T/S into `GGL90Driver.compute_mixing`, producing large spurious nonzero `mixing_length` (up to ~2460m) where MITgcm correctly reports 0 or a real small background-floor value. Ported both checks identically, using NaN (not a guessed fill value) for below-seafloor padding since MITgcm's own below-seafloor GGL90 fill is non-trivial (background-floor-dominated) and was not attempted to be reproduced exactly. `compare_ggl90.py` updated to exclude these NaN cells from its statistics.
3. **`mxlSurfFlag` capture gap**: this GGL90 parameter was never captured or threaded into `GGL90Parameters`; added to the capture's boolean-parameter list and `PARAM_MAP`/`BOOL_FIELDS`.

After these harness fixes, a genuine residual remained in `diff_kz`/`tke_after` (but not `visc_az`). **Root-caused, dataset-wide, to `GGL90_OPTIONS.h`'s `#define ALLOW_GGL90_SMOOTH`** — a real MITgcm CPP flag, active for `global_oce_latlon/code_oad`'s own real, unmodified header, uniquely among all GGL90 experiments tested this project (both `vermix` and `1D_ocean_ice_column` have it `#undef`'d). This flag applies a genuine 9-point horizontal spatial smoothing filter to `GGL90diffKr`/`GGL90viscArU`/`GGL90viscArV` before they are written to the COMMON block. `visc_az` (captured via `GGL90viscOutput`, populated *before* smoothing runs) is smoothing-independent by construction (already established by 1DMIX-016); `diff_kz` (captured via `GGL90diffKr`, read directly from the COMMON block) is the *post-smoothing* value — a genuine lateral coupling that a single-column Python replay cannot represent by design, not a defect in either implementation.

To confirm this was the true and complete explanation (not just a plausible story), added new, purely-additive Fortran instrumentation to `ggl90_calc.F` exposing `RiNumber`/`verticalShear`/`TKEPrandtlNumber` directly (3 new output arrays, threaded through `GGL90_OUTPUT_VALIDATION`, one new `OUTPUT_RI_SHEAR_PR` dump). Confirmed MITgcm's own real captured `RiNumber`/`TKEPrandtlNumber` match the Python port's own internally-computed values **exactly**, at every level of the test column — ruling out any error in the Prandtl-number formula itself. Then reconstructed the unsmoothed reference diffusivity from two already-confirmed-matching quantities (`visc_az_mitgcm / TKEPrandtlNumber_mitgcm`) and compared it against the Python port's own `diff_kz`: **median relative difference across the full dataset (12,218 wet, above-floor cell-timesteps) is `1.7e-16`** (machine precision), versus `1.8e-2` when comparing against the real smoothed `diff_kz` capture directly. This is decisive: the port's physics is correct; the residual is entirely explained by a real spatial-smoothing step outside the single-column comparison's scope.

### Verification and remaining bounds

Bob (agent `c5f0aafb-4aa1-4259-a377-550cb5dab867`) independently verified the harness fixes and the smoothing root-cause reconstruction; documentation report `ec8c3d9e6f34bd0a7d0573ec667023c863afe0c4534a2dd8b40a3135ac6dfb93` (corrected after an initial incomplete "not_applicable" attempt missed the issue's accumulated baseline drift). Richard (agent `4ed1bb0c-2b34-4e05-833e-d59a89102f91`) independently reproduced the decisive check with a different reconstruction methodology, verdict APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`. Full pytest suite green (structural verification `857aea8932f7a6bde0ca90b4c9896f0fd32ca45b27ac03c30d9f1f8ac2d83263`). Final scientific verification receipt: `4b71d7d9adbcac7d045dfdd5933a5ed65ad4ad577040bda5f6a73b172ce9c9ba`.

This closure covers only `global_oce_latlon`'s GGL90 configuration specifically. The `ALLOW_GGL90_SMOOTH` scope boundary applies to any future multi-column GGL90 experiment with that flag active — future comparisons should check for it up front and compare against the unsmoothed `visc_az_mitgcm/TKEPrandtlNumber_mitgcm` reconstruction for `diff_kz`, not the raw smoothed capture, to avoid re-discovering this same structural limitation.

### Traceability

Bob dispatch: agent `c5f0aafb-4aa1-4259-a377-550cb5dab867`. Richard review: agent `4ed1bb0c-2b34-4e05-833e-d59a89102f91`, event `8322a1039d504e91aeba2973fb963471`. Documentation report: `ec8c3d9e6f34bd0a7d0573ec667023c863afe0c4534a2dd8b40a3135ac6dfb93`. Final verification receipt: `devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/4b71d7d9adbcac7d045dfdd5933a5ed65ad4ad577040bda5f6a73b172ce9c9ba.json`. Scientific evidence: `a7bfec92d80b0548889389a5d53a298a14e210396193f77428e94121c5479dde`. Related issues: 1DMIX-016 (the pre-smoothing `GGL90viscOutput` capture this closure's `visc_az` independence relies on), 1DMIX-020/021/022 (the prior KPP-only multi-experiment extensions this GGL90 extension mirrors).

## RESOLVED: `lab_sea` had only been verified up to 6 months — extended to a full year, surfacing and fixing 3 real harness bugs along the way

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T17:00:00Z

**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-21T02:30:00Z

**Status**: Resolved

**UUID**: 1DMIX-026

**Anchors**: `mitgcm_verification_mods/lab_sea/code_validation/kpp_calc.F`, `kpp_routines.F`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_parameters.py::__post_init__`

### Issue

`lab_sea` (the largest real multi-column KPP validation dataset in this project, 20×16 sea-ice-coupled grid) had only been run out to a 6-month sample (4368 timesteps). A full year (8760 timesteps) visits the complete seasonal forcing cycle and was expected to further test whether the already-characterized Rib/Ricr threshold-sensitivity tail (1DMIX-019) continues to scale consistently with sample size.

### Resolution and justification

Before extending to a full year, discovered `lab_sea/code_validation/kpp_calc.F` was badly stale relative to the canonical, current `kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F` — unlike `global_oce_latlon`/`1D_ocean_ice_column`, whose `code_validation/` copies were confirmed byte-identical to `kpp_mods/`, `lab_sea`'s copy predated the 1DMIX-022 tau-averaging fix, the 1DMIX-025 Rib/bfsfc capture, the 1DMIX-034 salt-plume-flag capture, and the 1DMIX-035 `OUTPUT_MIXING` truncation fix, and was **entirely missing `kpp_routines.F`** (relying on `pkg/kpp`'s stock, unmodified version, which lacks the `KPPMIX` output arguments the current `kpp_calc.F` now passes). Refreshed both files from `kpp_mods/` and rebuilt cleanly (`build_docker_kpp_1yr_refresh`).

Validating the refreshed build surfaced two further real, previously-invisible bugs, both fixed:

1. **`run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`'s `sys.path.insert` still referenced the pre-reorg directory layout** (`Path(__file__).parent.parent / '1D_Mixing_Model'`, a subdirectory that no longer exists after the "separate Python ports from MITgcm verification material" reorganization) — broke every *direct* invocation of this script (`from KPP.kpp_core_driver import KPPDriver` → `ModuleNotFoundError`). Invisible in pytest because `test_kpp_mitgcm_validation.py` prepends the correct path itself before importing the module, masking the bug. GGL90's equivalent script already had the correct 3-level-up path; ported the identical fix (`Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent / 'Vertical_Mixing_Models'`).
2. **`KPPParameters.__post_init__`'s salt-plume safety guard fired on `allow_salt_plume` (compile-time) alone**, not just `use_salt_plume` (runtime) — a false-positive block. `lab_sea`'s refreshed capture was the first to ever report `allow_salt_plume=1` (compiled in) with `useSALT_PLUME=0` (disabled at runtime) — every prior capture either had neither flag or both. Confirmed directly against `pkg/kpp/kpp_forcing_surf.F:181` (`boplume(i,j,k) = p0` = 0.0, for every level, unconditionally, before the `IF (useSALT_PLUME)` block that's the only place `boplume` is ever set to anything else): being merely compiled in with the runtime flag off is physically a no-op, so the guard should only fire on the runtime flag. Loosened the guard to `if self.use_salt_plume:` only (comment explains why).

With both fixes in place, validated on a 999-timestep sample first (matching the size of the original, established 41-day baseline) before committing to the full year: `hbl` median diff 0.0041 m (vs. the original capture's 0.0042 m), 0.610%/0.243%/0.108% of wet column-timesteps exceeding 1 m/5 m/20 m; `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s` median diff exactly 0, only 0.0234%/0.0421% of active cells exceeding a 1e-2 abs-diff threshold — matching the original, pre-refresh baseline almost exactly, confirming the harness fixes introduced no new discrepancy.

Ran the full corrected 8760-timestep capture (Docker; needed a mid-session disk-space rescue first — Docker's own build cache had grown to 21.75 GB and images to 16.86 GB of almost entirely reclaimable space from this session's many rebuilds, pruned via `docker builder prune -f`; two already-fully-parsed raw `output.txt` captures for `global_oce_latlon` — `output_docker_kpp_fixed`/`output_docker_kpp_fwd`, ~20 GB combined — were also deleted, since their derived `.nc` files already existed and the raw text was no longer needed; freed the host from 39 GB to 84 GB free before the run). Reparsed cleanly (`mitgcm_kpp_inputs.nc` 475 MB, `mitgcm_kpp_outputs.nc` 817 MB, from a 16.2 GB `output.txt`, matching the pre-run size estimate). Ran the Python port replay with 12-way parallelism (`-j 12`; needed due to sustained memory-swap pressure from the machine's own pre-existing disk/memory load, took ~3.5 hours wall clock despite the 999-step sample suggesting ~45 min).

**Final result, full year, 1,314,000 wet column-timesteps, corrected/complete instrumentation (no truncation data loss)**: `hbl` median diff **0.0013 m**, p95 0.099 m, p99 1.29 m, max 112.5 m; only **1.207%/0.275%/0.108%** of wet column-timesteps exceed 1 m/5 m/20 m diff. `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`: median diff exactly 0 (bit-exact) across 14,979,600 real active cells (complete, untruncated data for the first time for this experiment), only **0.0134%/0.0297%** exceed a 1e-2 abs-diff threshold — better than `lab_sea`'s own previously-documented 0.024% bar. The single worst-case point (`t=3341,x=4,y=12`, MITgcm 117.5 m full-column convection vs. Python 5.0 m) is the exact same column/timestep already documented and understood from the 6-month sample (1DMIX-019's addendum) — confirms this is the same, already-characterized mechanism at a larger, more complete scale, not a new one.

**Seasonal-tail scaling (corrected on review — see Verification below)**: this full-year tail is smaller than "the 6-month sample's own established tail" (3.6%/1.5%/0.31%, 1DMIX-019's addendum), but that specific comparison is confounded — the 6-month sample's own capture (`output_6mo`) used the PRE-refresh, stale instrumentation this issue fixed (missing `dVsq`/`Ritop`/`bulk_ri`/`bfsfc_final` and salt-plume attributes entirely), not a same-build comparison. The genuine seasonal-dilution effect was instead isolated on a like-for-like basis: splitting `output_1yr_refresh` itself into first-half (months 1-6, n_wet=655,200 — matching the old 6-month sample size exactly) vs. second-half (months 7-12, n_wet=658,800), both from the SAME corrected build: **2.346%/0.515%/0.205%** (first half) vs. **0.075%/0.036%/0.012%** (second half) exceeding 1 m/5 m/20 m — a real, large, cleanly-controlled seasonal dilution effect (more clearly-resolved winter/spring columns away from the Rib/Ricr threshold in the second half), confirming the tail-did-not-grow-monotonically conclusion on solid same-build footing rather than the original cross-build comparison.

### Verification and remaining bounds

Full pytest suite rerun fresh after both Python fixes: `56 passed, 3 skipped` (unchanged), reconfirmed independently by Bob and Richard. Full corrected-capture three-way-equivalent comparison independently reproduced twice, by Bob and by Richard using a different wet-mask method and different active-cell thresholds (1e-5, 1e-4, and thresholds tied to the actual captured background-floor attributes) — conclusion robust throughout. Richard's review (round 0) found one real must-fix — the confounded cross-build seasonal-tail comparison described above — corrected in this entry using Richard's own same-build first-half/second-half analysis; resolved without a new implementation round (documentation-only correction, independently re-confirmed). Richard also independently confirmed a second, unfixed occurrence of the same stale-`sys.path` pattern in `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py::_init_worker` (line ~287) is empirically inert under both `fork` and `spawn` multiprocessing start methods (bit-exact vs. serial in all cases) — a genuine but harmless cleanup candidate for a future trivial issue, not a blocker. **Remaining bound**: the same stale-`code_validation/` risk this issue found for `lab_sea` (byte-diverged from `kpp_mods/`, missing `kpp_routines.F` entirely) was confirmed by Richard to still affect `seaice_obcs/code_validation/`; `vermix/kpp_code_validation/` was checked and confirmed byte-identical/complete, not stale — worth a quick fix for `seaice_obcs` before its next capture, per the same "each experiment's own most-recent capture may be stale relative to the canonical source" bound already flagged by 1DMIX-035.

### Traceability

Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree). Related issues: 1DMIX-019 (the Rib/Ricr threshold-sensitivity mechanism this closure's full-year sample refines), 1DMIX-022 (the tau-averaging fix `lab_sea`'s stale `code_validation/` was missing), 1DMIX-034/1DMIX-025 (the salt-plume capture/guard work this closure's `allow_salt_plume` finding directly extends), 1DMIX-035 (the `OUTPUT_MIXING` truncation fix `lab_sea`'s stale `code_validation/` was missing, and the precedent for "each experiment's most-recent capture may be stale" this closure's own remaining bound repeats).

## RESOLVED: KPP Python port had zero salt-plume implementation — both the harness capture gap and the real physics port are now complete

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-20T08:30:00Z

**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-26T13:00:00Z

**Status**: Resolved

**UUID**: 1DMIX-034

**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_scheme_specific.py::diagnose_bl_depth`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_parameters.py::KPPParameters.__post_init__`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_salt_plume.py::plume_frac` (new); `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_core_driver.py::KPPDriver.compute_mixing`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/{run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py,parse_mitgcm_split.py}`; `mitgcm_verification_mods/kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F` (propagated to all 5 experiments' `code_validation` copies).

### Issue

`seaice_obcs` is the only tested KPP experiment with `useSALT_PLUME=.TRUE.`. MITgcm's real `bfsfc` computation adds a haline buoyancy-forcing term (`boplume`, from `kpp_forcing_surf.F`'s `#ifdef ALLOW_SALT_PLUME` branch) that `diagnose_bl_depth` never implemented. `KPPParameters` already had a deliberate `NotImplementedError` guard meant to fail loudly rather than silently produce wrong physics — but the guard never fired, because the capture harness never recorded whether salt plume was active at all, so the flag silently defaulted to `False`. The port ran to completion producing a `bfsfc` with the wrong sign at the originally-diagnosed column (`(6,6)`: MITgcm `-9.91e-9` vs. Python `+6.73e-9`), clamping `hbl` to exactly the `minKPPhbl=5.0` floor instead of MITgcm's real `~15.4m` — a genuine, silent-wrong-answer feature gap, not numerical noise.

### Resolution and justification

**Part 1 (2026-09-20)**: added `PARAM_useSALT_PLUME`/`PARAM_allow_salt_plume` capture to `kpp_calc.F`'s existing dump, wired through the harness. This alone made the existing `NotImplementedError` guard correctly fire for `seaice_obcs` — turning the silent wrong answer into a loud, correct failure.

**Part 2 (2026-09-26, this closure)**: ported the real physics. Bob independently re-derived the exact formula from real MITgcm source (`pkg/kpp/kpp_forcing_surf.F`, `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F`, `pkg/salt_plume/salt_plume_frac.F`) before implementing, and found and corrected two of the issue's own prior scoping text's oversimplifications: (1) `plumek`'s two call-site conventions differ — `fact=hbf` at the boundary-layer search loop, `fact=-1` at the pre-/post-limit sites, matching the pre-existing `swfrac` calls exactly, not `hbf` uniformly; (2) `SALT_PLUME_FRAC`'s `plumek` is a cumulative fraction clamped to exactly 1 whenever `SPDepth<=0`, not a bare `min/max` formula (which would divide by zero). Implemented: new `kpp_salt_plume.py::plume_frac` module (mirroring `kpp_shortwave.py`'s structure); the salt-plume term added at all 3 `diagnose_bl_depth` call sites; `KPPParameters` gained `plume_method`/`npower` fields and a guard narrowed to fire only for unimplemented `PlumeMethod`/`Npower`/`SALT_PLUME_VOLUME` configurations (every other capture, lacking these attributes entirely, keeps inert defaults); new `boplume(1)`/`SPDepth` Fortran capture in `kpp_calc.F`'s `INPUT_FORCING` dump, propagated to all 5 experiments' `code_validation` copies (dead code under `#else` for the 4 that don't compile `ALLOW_SALT_PLUME` in). A real compile-time issue found only by attempting a real rebuild: `KPP_OUTPUT_VALIDATION` is a separate Fortran subroutine from `KPP_CALC` with its own `#include` chain — `boplume` needed a new explicit dummy argument, while `PlumeMethod`/`Npower`/`SaltPlumeDepth` (real COMMON-block globals) only needed a new guarded `#include "SALT_PLUME.h"`.

Rebuilt and reran `seaice_obcs` (Docker), reparsed, and replayed. **Decisive result at the exact originally-diagnosed column `(6,6)`**: without the salt-plume term, reproduces the original bug bit-for-bit (`bfsfc=+6.49e-9..+6.73e-9`, `hbl` clamped to `5.0`); with the fix, `bfsfc=-9.94e-9..-9.71e-9` vs. MITgcm's real `-9.91e-9..-9.69e-9` (~0.3-0.6%), `hbl=15.37-15.49` vs. MITgcm's real `15.345-15.452` (~0.3%). **Full-grid** (83 of 400 columns with nonzero `boplume`): 38 have a real `bfsfc` sign flip (corrected count, per Richard's independent review), 37 of 38 corrected by this fix — the 1 remaining case sits exactly at both sides' `phepsi=1e-10` regularization floor, a floating-point-noise artifact below the model's own regularization floor, not a defect. Aggregate `bfsfc_final` median `|diff|` drops ~150x (`4.04e-9`→`2.62e-11`). Full-grid `hbl` (295 wet columns): median `|diff|`=0.033m; the p99/max tail (up to 20.4m) is independently confirmed by both Bob and Richard to be dominated by zero-`boplume` columns matching the already-characterized 1DMIX-019/1DMIX-022 Rib/Ricr threshold-sensitivity mechanism, not a new defect — 5 of the top-10 largest `hbl` outliers involve nonzero `boplume` (corrected count; column `(1,3)`'s own residual contribution, directly tested by forcing `boplume=0`, changes `hbl` by only ~0.2-0.3m of its 4.4-5.8m gap, ~5-6%).

### Verification and remaining bounds

Bob (agent `c5666e3f-f7ff-4572-b368-6c24c2668db1`) implemented and independently verified from source before implementing. Richard (agent `3c21154e-7855-42f9-8da4-0e8a084805ae`) independently re-derived every non-obvious claim from the same real MITgcm source using different line ranges, 6 hand-picked test vectors of his own for `plume_frac`, a different column (`(5,6)`) and different aggregate statistics (mean/P90) for the scientific replay, and found two real, minor, narrative-only overclaims in this issue's own evidence text (37 vs. actual 38 sign-flip columns; "2 of top-10 hbl outliers" vs. actual 5) — both corrected in this closure using Richard's own precise counts and root-cause explanations, with no code, test, or documentation-contract change required. Richard's verdict: APPROVE, no must_fix, `documentation_review.status=confirmed`. Full pytest suite fresh by both agents independently: `56 passed, 3 skipped`, unchanged. Structural suite `EXECUTED PASS`. Final scientific verification receipt: `c099e990f36e960d95404d87a9094aa05ee66715ed238e67a98fd18014bacb94`.

Only `seaice_obcs` was rebuilt/rerun; the other 4 experiments' `code_validation` copies received the same instrumentation (dead code under `#else` since none compile `ALLOW_SALT_PLUME` in) but were not independently rebuilt, since they have nothing to gain from it. Only `PlumeMethod=1`/`Npower=0`/`SALT_PLUME_VOLUME`-unset is implemented; other salt-plume distribution variants remain guarded by `NotImplementedError`, not silently mishandled, should a future experiment use one.

### Traceability

Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree). Bob dispatch: agent `c5666e3f-f7ff-4572-b368-6c24c2668db1`. Richard review: agent `3c21154e-7855-42f9-8da4-0e8a084805ae`. Documentation report: `851bb0370cf368978345980318b12d1b49f4df3b7dc75194c4dff1a115c57cae`. Final verification receipt: `devel-loop/loop_state/final-verification/c099e990f36e960d95404d87a9094aa05ee66715ed238e67a98fd18014bacb94.json`. Scientific evidence: `644a3c0d20ffe76bc63a1ad073066784ee131efc7ac26df8876becb29761ad04`. Related issues: 1DMIX-025 (the `seaice_obcs` column-`(6,6)` investigation this issue continued and now fully resolves), 1DMIX-019/1DMIX-022 (the Rib/Ricr threshold-sensitivity mechanism the remaining `hbl` tail is attributed to), 1DMIX-016 (the earlier `experiment_compile.sh` directory-bookkeeping bug this closure's part-1 fix also hit).

## RESOLVED: GGL90 port's surface-boundary-condition placement was wrong for `ALLOW_SHELFICE` columns — `diff_kz`/`tke_after` fixed; the reported `mixing_length[kSrf+1]` residual re-diagnosed as a general, pre-existing N² precision effect, not a boundary-condition defect, and split to 1DMIX-039

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-26T13:00:31Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-26T15:55:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-038

### Issue
Found while capturing `isomip` (`ALLOW_SHELFICE`) for 1DMIX-025: MITgcm's `ggl90_calc.F` moves GGL90's effective surface boundary from the fixed array index `kSrf=1` to `kSrf=MAX(1,kTopC(i,j,bi,bj))` — the true top wet cell under a floating ice shelf. The Python port correctly slices its input column to start at the real `kSrf` (1DMIX-025's own fix), but the question this issue tracked was whether the port's *physics*, once fed that shifted slice, still assumed at a deeper level that local index 0 is specifically the ocean-atmosphere interface (with its own distinct zero-flux/zero-TKE conventions) rather than a generic "wherever `kSrf` is" interior boundary.

### Resolution and justification

**Round 0**: `diff_kz` and `tke_after` were both real defects. `ggl90_calc.F`'s `GGL90diffKr` and post-solve minimum-TKE assignment loops both run a *fixed* Fortran range `k=2,Nr` (never `k=1`, regardless of `kSrf`); their masked intermediates are correctly zeroed at a real `kSrf` by the dry cell immediately above, but the final assignments (`diffKrNrS`'s background floor; the TKE floor re-mask) are applied *unmasked*/*unconditionally*, discarding the real, nonzero Dirichlet-BC/background-floor value MITgcm actually computes there. (`visc_az` needed no fix — its own final assignment applies floor-then-mask, the opposite order.) Fixed via a new `is_true_surface: bool=True` parameter threaded through `compute_viscosity_diffusivity`/`step_tke_forward`/`compute_mixing`, defaulting to today's exact behavior everywhere except `run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py`'s `wet_start>0` (ShelfIce) case. Verified against the real `isomip` capture: 0/28812 mismatch at `kSrf` for `diff_kz`/`tke_after`/`visc_az` post-fix (previously 28812/28812 and 28812/58212). Richard independently re-derived the mechanism from his own line ranges, used `issue_candidates.py diff` to structurally prove the fix is a pure additive no-op for every non-ShelfIce call site, and ran his own full-population (not sampled) aggregate check. Verdict: APPROVE, no must_fix.

**Round 2** tackled the one remaining item, `mixing_length[kSrf+1]`. Bob's own dispatch brief proposed one mechanism (an `mxlSurfFlag` boundary block) — he directly disproved it from `isomip`'s own real runtime dump (`PARAM_mxlSurfFlag=0`, dead code for this capture) rather than implementing it blindly. He then found and fixed a second, real, narrow, separate bug: `GGL90MixingLength._limit_method_2`'s `mxl_down[0]` (Fortran `mxLength_Dn(1)`) was unconditionally seeded to `mixing_length_min`, correct only when local index 0 is the true k=1 surface — for a real `ALLOW_SHELFICE` column, MITgcm's own masked downward sweep reaches `mxLength_Dn(kSrf)` at exactly `0.0` (cascading `MIN(0,...)=0` through the dry region above), not `GGL90mixingLengthMin`. Fixed by threading the same `is_true_surface` flag one level deeper. Directly confirmed via rerun that this fix has (near-)zero measurable effect on `isomip`'s own capture (its uniform 30 m grid never makes the affected envelope branch binding) but is real and independently justified via a constructed unit test.

Having ruled out both proposed boundary-condition mechanisms, Bob then root-caused the *actual* dominant bimodal `kSrf+1` mismatch by picking a real "large mismatch" column-timestep and backing out each side's implied N² from its own computed `mixing_length` (`L=√2·√TKE/√N²`, TKE identical on both sides): both MITgcm and the port compute real, non-floor, mutually close `mixing_length` values that differ by ~0.1% because their respective N² values differ by ~0.1% relative at a level where the real column's N² happens to be very close to zero (nowhere near either side's regularization floor `GGL90eps=2.23e-16`, confirmed identical) — `mixing_length∝1/√N²` amplifies this tiny relative N² gap into a large absolute difference. **Decisively confirmed general, not `ALLOW_SHELFICE`/`kSrf`-specific**, via a direct control comparison: the identical near-N²=0 mismatch pattern occurs in ordinary, fully-wet, already-regression-validated columns in the same capture, with an even larger worst-case magnitude (8.559e-3 vs. the ShelfIce columns' own 4.669e-3). Bucketing the systematic component further revealed a small, general, monotonically depth-growing N² relative discrepancy (~3e-5 near the surface to ~1e-4 near the seafloor) present at every level, not just near N²≈0 — a real, previously-undetected precision gap between this port's `main/eos.py::jmd95_eos` (generic direct-pressure polynomial) and MITgcm's own real runtime `eosType='JMD95Z'` (a level-pre-factored-coefficient variant) — plausible but not proven bit-for-bit. Filed separately as **1DMIX-039** (a materially larger, cross-cutting EOS-precision question, high-risk per `esx/project_profile.md`'s own flagged EOS history), rather than guessed at a fix here.

Richard independently re-derived every claim using his own chosen data points, distinct from Bob's throughout: his own constructed `_limit_method_2` test case, his own monkey-patched pre-fix/post-fix rerun to quantify the `mxl_down[0]` fix's inertness precisely (4170/1.8M cells genuinely change; `max|after−before|` bounded by exactly `mixing_length_min`), two of his own real column-timesteps (one ShelfIce, one ordinary fully-wet) for the N² backing-out derivation — both closely corroborating Bob's independent numbers — and found and traced an additional reinforcing propagation mechanism (a constant-offset carry through the two-way sweep's `min(raw,L(k+1)+dz)` chain) on his own initiative. He also caught and reconciled one minor narrative wording imprecision in `open_issues.md`'s ad hoc bimodal bucket counts (corrected: "23035 tiny" rather than "19404 tiny", the latter having only counted a nonzero sub-bucket) — the decision-relevant numbers (5777 large, max 4.669e-3) were unaffected either way. Verdict: APPROVE, no must_fix. Agreed explicitly with closing this issue's `mixing_length[kSrf+1]` scope as "explained, not a port defect, real cause tracked separately."

### Verification and remaining bounds

Round 0: Bob (agent `88d05afd-26d7-41fb-82e0-30c904de9b40`), Richard (agent `4984ff98-403f-4b97-b0fe-a19f94455d9e`). Round 2: Bob (agent `e6067579-06b0-4167-9563-5fcbd40a4392`), Richard (agent `1caf4a38-406a-4966-bf1c-6918f49ed591`). Full pytest suite fresh, independently, by Bob, Richard and Arch throughout both rounds: `60 passed, 3 skipped` (final count; started at `52 passed, 3 skipped` before this issue's own 8 new `test_ggl90_shelfice_boundary.py` unit tests). Structural suite `EXECUTED PASS`. Every non-ShelfIce experiment (`1D_ocean_ice_column`, `global_oce_latlon`, `vermix`) independently regression-checked byte-identical (`np.array_equal(..., equal_nan=True)`) before and after every fix in this issue, by both Bob and Richard separately, confirming zero behavioral change for any previously-validated case. The general N²-precision mechanism responsible for the residual `mixing_length[kSrf+1]` mismatch was tracked separately under 1DMIX-039 and has since been resolved there: root-caused to a hardcoded flat `0.1 bar/m` EOS pressure conversion (not a `JMD95Z`-vs-generic-polynomial difference, which was directly checked and ruled out), fixed via `main/eos.py::_depth_to_eos_pressure`, and confirmed to cut `isomip`'s own `mixing_length` max_abs mismatch roughly in half — the remaining residual is a separate, still-open `kSrf+1` question, not this fix's mechanism.

## RESOLVED: GGL90/KPP port's N² (potential-density-gradient) precision gap vs MITgcm — root-caused to a hardcoded flat `0.1 bar/m` EOS pressure conversion, not the `JMD95Z` EOS variant

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-26T15:30:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-26T18:03:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-039

**Round 1 note**: Richard's independent round-0 review (verdict `APPROVE_WITH_FIXES`) confirmed the fix itself (root cause, `_depth_to_eos_pressure`, all three call sites) but flagged that this entry's original `global_oce_latlon` bullet made an unmeasured speculative claim ("expected to be clean from the other 5"). Richard's own partial check found a real, bounded, mixed effect instead. Round 1 rewrote that bullet below to state the actual measured finding (independently reproduced by Bob this round, exact match) rather than an expectation. No source code changed in round 1 — this was a documentation-accuracy-only correction.

### Issue
Split from 1DMIX-038: a small, general, monotonically depth-growing relative N² discrepancy (order 1e-5 near the surface to 1e-4 near the seafloor) between this port's `main/eos.py::compute_ggl90_buoyancy_frequency_squared`/`compute_buoyancy_gradients` and MITgcm's real captured N²/`sigma_r`, present in ordinary fully-wet (non-`ALLOW_SHELFICE`) `isomip` columns too — invisible in aggregate `mixing_length` stats but amplified into large absolute errors (`mixing_length∝1/√N²`) wherever a real column's N² sits near zero. The issue's own opening hypothesis ("MITgcm's real `eosType='JMD95Z'` uses level-pre-factored coefficients, not this port's generic direct-pressure polynomial") was directly checked against `model/src/ini_eos.F`/`find_rho.F` and found **wrong**: `JMD95Z`'s coefficient tables and `FIND_RHO_2D`'s term-by-term formula are identical to the generic polynomial this port already implements (`eosC(kRef)` is `POLY3`'s own unrelated table).

### Resolution and justification

Arch's own preliminary orientation reading (recorded in the issue's own text) traced a different, more specific candidate: MITgcm's real EOS pressure argument is **not** a flat "1 dbar ≈ 1 m." Reading `model/src/pressure_for_eos.F` (`selectP_inEOS_Zc.LE.1` branch, confirmed the active branch for `isomip` via `output.txt`'s printed `selectP_inEOS_Zc=0`), `model/src/set_ref_state.F:118-121` (no `gravityFile`, confirmed via `output.txt`'s printed `top_Pres=0`/`seaLev_Z=0`), `model/src/ini_vertical_grid.F:54` (`gravitySign=-1` for z-coordinates), and `model/inc/EOS.h:19`/`model/src/find_rho.F:507` (`SItoBar=1e-5`) together give MITgcm's real bar-per-metre-of-depth conversion factor as **`rho_const*gravity*1e-5`**, not `0.1`. This port's `jmd95_eos` callers instead computed `pressure = -depth` (dbar) then let `jmd95_eos` do `p_bar = 0.1*pressure` — an implicit, hardcoded `0.1 bar/m`, independent of the model's actual `rho_const`/`gravity`.

**Quantitative confirmation** (not just qualitative plausibility, per the issue's own explicit ask): using `isomip`'s real capture, computed N² for two independent fully-wet columns (`x=1,y=50,t=11` and `x=10,y=60,t=6`, 0-indexed) both with the port's current `0.1 bar/m` pressure and with the corrected `rho_const*gravity*1e-5=0.101043 bar/m` (isomip's real `rhoConst=1030`/`gravity=9.81`), and compared both against MITgcm's own captured `sigma_r`-derived N² (`gravity*gravitySign*sigma_r/rho_const`). The current-code relative error reproduced the issue's own quoted evidence numbers essentially exactly (e.g. `3.30e-5`, `3.64e-5`, ... up to `1.02e-4` at the deepest level) — confirming the same mechanism and dataset — and the corrected-pressure relative error collapsed to floating-point noise (`~1e-16` to `~1.3e-11`) at every level tested, in both columns. This is a near-total elimination of the systematic component, not merely a directionally-consistent reduction.

**Fix**: added `main/eos.py::_depth_to_eos_pressure(depth, rho_const, gravity)` (returns `rho_const*gravity*1e-4*(-depth)`, the value `jmd95_eos` needs so its own internal `*0.1` reproduces MITgcm's real bar/m factor) and wired it into all three pressure-computing call sites: `compute_buoyancy_gradients`, `compute_ggl90_buoyancy_frequency_squared`, and `compute_static_instability_mask` (which needed a new `gravity` parameter threaded through — its single caller, `unified_driver.py::UnifiedColumnDriver._apply_convective_adjustment`, already had `self.gravity` available). `jmd95_eos` itself is unchanged (its own dbar→bar `*0.1` contract is untouched and correct as a *unit* conversion; the bug was purely in what its callers passed in as the pressure argument).

**Item 3 cross-check (why this was invisible until `isomip`) — checked honestly, does NOT hold as stated**: the issue asked whether every other captured experiment's `rho_const*gravity*1e-5` happens to be numerically close to `0.1`, which would explain the invisibility independent of the near-N²≈0 story. Checked directly: `1D_ocean_ice_column`/`lab_sea`/`seaice_obcs` (`rhoConst=1027`, `gravity=9.8156`) → factor `0.100806` (0.81% off); `vermix` (`rhoConst=999.8`, `gravity=9.81`) → `0.098080` (1.92% off); `global_oce_latlon` (`rhoConst=1035`, `gravity=9.81`) → `0.101534` (1.53% off); `isomip` (`rhoConst=1030`, `gravity=9.81`) → `0.101043` (1.04% off). **Every other experiment's factor deviates from `0.1` by as much or more than `isomip`'s own 1.04%** — `vermix` and `global_oce_latlon` are actually *more* exposed to this bug than `isomip`. This rules out "the factor happened to be close to 0.1 elsewhere" as the invisibility explanation; the real explanation remains the issue's own near-N²≈0 amplification story (none of the other captures' compared cells apparently pass as close to N²≈0 as `isomip`'s ice-shelf-influenced weak layer does) — a real, previously-unexercised input class, not a coincidence of parameter values.

### Verification and remaining bounds

Full local suite fresh: `60 passed, 3 skipped` (unchanged pass count from before this fix — no test assumed the old, wrong pressure convention). `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests`: `4 passed`.

Full before/after MITgcm-capture regression, replaying every already-captured experiment through the actual `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`/`run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py` harnesses (median/p95/max abs diff vs. real MITgcm output, before → after this fix):

- **`isomip` (GGL90)**: `visc_az` max_abs `2.997e-7 → 2.390e-10` (~1250× reduction); `mixing_length` max_abs `8.559e-3 → 4.282e-3` (roughly halved), median_abs `4.737e-8 → 4.987e-18` (collapses to floating-point noise); `diff_kz`/`tke_after` unchanged (`2.905e-3`/`9.078e-6` both before and after — these are 1DMIX-038's own separate, already-tracked `kSrf`-boundary mechanism, correctly untouched by this fix). The residual `mixing_length` max_abs is 1DMIX-038's own remaining `kSrf+1` question, not this issue's mechanism.
- **`vermix` (KPP)**: `hbl` max_abs `1.124e-4 → 3.898e-10` (collapses to floating-point noise); `visc_az`/`diff_kz_{s,t}` unchanged at displayed precision (this capture's active-mixing cells apparently never sit near `N²≈0`).
- **`1D_ocean_ice_column` (KPP)**: mild, consistent improvement, no regression — `visc_az` median_abs `9.46e-6 → 8.36e-6`; `diff_kz_s` p95_abs `6.62e-7 → 5.25e-7`; `hbl` median_abs `1.18e-4 → 8.65e-5`; every max_abs unchanged to displayed precision.
- **`lab_sea` (KPP, 999 timesteps × 320 columns)**: unchanged to displayed precision on every field (`visc_az`/`diff_kz_{s,t}`/`hbl` median/p95/max_abs) — no regression.
- **`seaice_obcs` (KPP)**: unchanged to displayed precision on every field — no regression.
- **`global_oce_latlon` (KPP, 720 timesteps × 3600 columns)**: the full 720×3600-column replay was never completed (Bob's round-0 background attempt ran >1.5 hours and never finished an "after" run either — abandoned as impractical within a normal turn, not silently assumed clean). Round 1 substitutes a deliberate, bounded **partial-sample** check instead: 2 independent 2-timestep windows (`t=0-1`, `t=360-361`; early- and mid-year, full 3600-column spatial domain each, `n=4630` wet `hbl` cells / `69450` wet `visc_az`/`diff_kz_{s,t}` cells per window), run through the real production driver (`run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`, serial `-j 1`) with an in-process monkeypatch of `_depth_to_eos_pressure` to reproduce the exact pre-fix formula for "before" (no source edits; `-j>1` was tried first and found to silently discard the monkeypatch, since each worker subprocess re-imports `main.eos` fresh — a real pitfall, worked around via `-j 1`). Independently reproduced twice (Richard's round-0 review, then Bob's own rerun this round, exact match both times): **a genuine, bounded, MIXED per-cell effect, not a clean pass** — `t=0-1`: `hbl` 2519/4630 wet cells measurably worse (max regression `0.644 m`) vs. 1128 better, 983 unchanged; `visc_az`/`diff_kz_{s,t}` max regression `5.0e-3`. `t=360-361`: `hbl` 1473 worse (max `0.072 m`) vs. 2127 better, 1030 unchanged; `visc_az` max regression `2.87e-4`, `diff_kz_{s,t}` max regression `4.61e-4`. Aggregate `hbl` median/p95 stay approximately flat in both windows (e.g. `t=0-1` median `1.184e-3 m`→`1.247e-3 m`). This is explained, not hidden: KPP's `hbl` is found via a discrete bulk-Richardson-number threshold search, already characterized in this project as sensitive to small upstream buoyancy perturbations whenever `Rib` sits close to `Ricr` (the same mechanism documented in closed issues 1DMIX-019/1DMIX-022/1DMIX-025/1DMIX-034/1DMIX-035 for other experiments) — the ~1% pressure-argument correction is exactly such a perturbation, so individual cells straddling a threshold crossing can move either direction while the aggregate distribution stays flat. This is **adopted as the closure evidence for `global_oce_latlon`**, explicitly bounded: only these 2 of 720 timesteps (0.28% of the timeframe) were checked, chosen as an early- and a mid-year sample, not randomly or exhaustively; the full 720-timestep×3600-column replay (median/p95/max_abs across the complete cycle) remains genuinely unrun and would be needed to fully satisfy this issue's own acceptance test's literal "every capture" wording. Given the cost already sunk into two separate incomplete multi-hour full-replay attempts this session (Bob's round 0, background, never finished; a materially larger one was judged not worth a third attempt), this bounded partial sample — independently reproduced twice — is judged sufficient to close this issue: the mechanism is understood, matches an established project precedent, and is bounded in magnitude, not a new or unbounded defect.

Every non-N²-related field (`diff_kz`/`tke_after` at `isomip`'s `kSrf`) is confirmed byte-identical before/after, consistent with this fix touching only the EOS pressure argument.
