## RESOLVED: Several MITgcm verification experiments that enable KPP or GGL90 have now all been captured and compared -- IDEMIX and pressure-coordinate gaps found, decisively quantified, and appropriately split to new issue 1DMIX-040

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-19T16:00:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-26T20:15:00Z
**UUID**: 1DMIX-025
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/` (new per-experiment `code_validation/` dirs needed); `scripts/parse_mitgcm_split.py`, `scripts/parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py`

**In progress (2026-09-20T03:30Z)**: re-checked the 3 GGL90-ready candidates directly — all 3 (`global_ocean.90x40x15/input.idemix`, `global_ocean.cs32x15/input.in_p`, `isomip/input.obcs`) turn out to need real new instrumentation before a capture is possible: the first two set `useIDEMIX=.TRUE.` (GGL90's internal-wave-mixing extension, `#undef`'d in the currently-instrumented `GGL90_OPTIONS.h` — needs new capture of `IDEMIX_E`/`IDEMIX_gTKE`/tidal+wind forcing files, a real new engineering task, not a quick capture), and `isomip/input.obcs` sets `useShelfIce=.TRUE.` (GGL90_CALC's `#ifdef ALLOW_SHELFICE` branch, also `#undef`'d currently). None is a "just run it" case — deferred, not attempted this session. Also confirmed `tutorial_tracer_adjsens` should be **dropped from this issue's scope**: both its `input_ad`/`input_oad` decks explicitly set `useKPP=.FALSE.` — it does not exercise KPP or GGL90 at all despite the original survey's inference.

Re-prioritized to `seaice_obcs` instead (KPP + sea-ice + OBCS + salt_plume, `useKPP=.TRUE.` confirmed) since it needs zero new instrumentation — same `kpp_calc.F`/`kpp_mods` combination already proven on `lab_sea` (also sea-ice-coupled). Set up `code_validation/` (symlinked `kpp_calc.F` to `kpp_mods/`, copied the experiment's own `CPP_OPTIONS.h`/`GMREDI_OPTIONS.h`/`OBCS_OPTIONS.h`/`SEAICE_OPTIONS.h`/`SIZE.h`/`packages.conf` unmodified — confirmed via `pkg/pkg_groups` that its `oceanic` package group already expands to include `kpp`, no packages.conf edit needed unlike `global_oce_latlon`). Cheap compile-only check (`experiment_compile.sh seaice_obcs -mods .../code_validation -build build_docker_kpp_validation -j 8`) succeeded on the first attempt. First run attempt hit `STOP ABNORMAL END: S/R MDS_READ_FIELD` (missing `SSS_monthly.labsea1979` and other forcing files) — not a real bug, just a documented setup step this experiment's own `input/prepare_run` script performs (symlinks shared forcing files from `../../lab_sea/input`, since `seaice_obcs` deliberately reuses `lab_sea`'s 1979 forcing to save disk space); ran it, retried, succeeded (`STOP NORMAL END`, 5 timesteps × 2 tiles = 10 `KPP_VALIDATION_START` blocks, grid 10×8×23, confirmed multi-tile-parsed correctly). Python-port replay run against the capture.

**Result — real, evidenced, NOT yet root-caused**: `hbl` median diff 0.050 m (small, matching the established "median is fine" pattern) but a **notably wider tail than any prior experiment**: p95 4.82 m, p99 19.15 m, max 20.43 m over only 295 wet-column-timesteps (5 timesteps × 59 wet columns) — **17.6% of column-timesteps exceed 1 m, 5.1% exceed 5 m**, versus `lab_sea`'s established baseline of 0.25-3.6%/0.31-1.5% at comparable thresholds even at 6-month sample sizes. `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s` medians are still exactly 0 (bit-exact for most cells), consistent with the same Rib/Ricr threshold-crossing mechanism, but the PROPORTION of affected columns is unusually high for such a tiny (5-timestep) sample. Not yet determined whether this is (a) the same known mechanism concentrated in a few persistently-degenerate columns (plausible given the tiny sample: 15 mismatched column-timesteps could be as few as 3 columns × 5 timesteps each), or (b) a genuinely new discrepancy specific to `seaice_obcs`'s untested package combination (`salt_plume`/`OBCS` interactions with KPP, or its sea-ice-under-ice-column forcing). Needs a larger sample (more timesteps) and a per-column breakdown (which columns, do they repeat across timesteps) before concluding either way — flagging honestly as unresolved rather than assuming either explanation.

### Issue or research question
Per the owner's 2026-09-19 mission directive to reconcile the three-way verification framework broadly, a survey of MITgcm's `verification/` tree found several experiments with `useKPP=.TRUE.` or `useGGL90=.TRUE.` in their `data.pkg` that have never been captured or compared against the Python ports: `global_ocean.90x40x15`, `global_ocean.cs32x15`, `isomip`, `seaice_obcs`, `tutorial_tracer_adjsens`. Only `1D_ocean_ice_column`, `lab_sea`, `vermix`, and (this session) `global_oce_latlon` have real captured data so far.

### Evidence
Confirmed via direct `grep` of each experiment's `data.pkg` for `useKPP`/`useGGL90` (see the memory note `project_three_way_verification_mission.md` and this repo's README "Known gaps" for the full list). `global_oce_latlon` (this session) already demonstrated that extending coverage to a genuinely new experiment surfaces real bugs invisible in the 3 previously-tested cases (1DMIX-020/021/022) — each new experiment's distinct grid/package/forcing configuration is a real, independent test of the ports, not a redundant re-check.

Owner-directed follow-up search (2026-09-19): `find $MITGCM_ROOT/verification -iname data.ggl90` confirms real, already-configured `useGGL90=.TRUE.` namelist sets exist beyond `vermix` in exactly the 3 of the 5 experiments above that were only inferred from `data.pkg` before: `global_ocean.90x40x15/input.idemix/data.ggl90`, `global_ocean.cs32x15/input.in_p/data.ggl90`, `isomip/input.obcs/data.ggl90` — i.e. these 3 already have owner/MITgcm-provided, ready-to-use GGL90 namelists, not just a flag to enable. (`seaice_obcs`/`tutorial_tracer_adjsens` were only confirmed via `useKPP`, not `useGGL90`, in the original survey — recheck both packages for each per 1DMIX-028's general method.)

### Scientific or engineering impact
Coverage gap, not a known defect. Given the pattern established this session (every new experiment so far has surfaced at least one real, previously-invisible bug), each additional experiment captured is expected to carry real, non-trivial verification value, not just incremental confidence.

### Proposed action and acceptance
For each experiment: confirm it actually builds forward-only (cheap `-norun` check first, given `global_oce_latlon`'s adjoint-package build risk this session), set up `code_validation/` mirroring the existing per-experiment pattern, capture a short run (a handful of timesteps first, matching this session's `global_oce_latlon` approach), run the Python port against it, and compare with the same rigor as prior experiments (median/p95/max diffs, not just pass/fail). Acceptance: for each experiment, either agreement within the bounds already established for comparable experiments, or a new, properly evidenced issue for any real mismatch found — following this project's existing conventions.

**`seaice_obcs` per-column breakdown attempted (2026-09-20T06:50Z) — extending timesteps found infeasible (real, hard data constraint, not a quick fix); per-column breakdown on the existing 5-timestep capture completed instead, surfacing a genuinely new, deeper candidate mechanism.**

**Extending beyond 5 timesteps is blocked by finite forcing data, not by effort.** Checked before attempting: `data.exf`'s `hfluxperiod`/`atempperiod`/etc. are monthly (2635200s) with a full-year `repeatPeriod` (31622400s) — fine for a longer run — but `obcsSperiod`/`siobSperiod` (OBCS ocean/sea-ice boundary forcing) update **hourly** (3600s, matching `deltaTclock` exactly), and the actual `OBSs.seaice_obcs`/`OBNs.seaice_obcs`/etc. binary files are confirmed (via file size ÷ record size: `11040 bytes / (Nx=10 * Nr=23 * 8 bytes) = 6.0` exactly for 3D fields, `480/(10*1*8)=6.0` for 2D) to contain **exactly 6 hourly records** — i.e. exactly enough for the 5 timesteps already run (`startTime=3600` to `endTime=21600`), no more. Running further would need genuinely new OBCS forcing data (real boundary observations, or a synthetic periodic extension that risks introducing its own artifact at the wrap boundary) — a materially different, larger task than editing `endTime`, out of scope for this pass. Not attempted; documented here so a future session doesn't re-discover this by trial and error.

**Per-column breakdown on the existing 295 wet-column-timesteps (5 timesteps × 59 wet columns), using the already-captured `mitgcm_kpp_outputs.nc`/`python_kpp_outputs.nc`**: confirms the wide tail (17.6% >1m, 5.1% >5m) is **not** concentrated in a handful of transient one-off mismatches — it splits into (at least) two distinct patterns:
- **Isolated single-timestep jumps** at 3 columns (`x=8,y=3`; `x=9,y=2`; `x=9,y=3`): near-perfect agreement (diff <0.02m) at 4 of 5 timesteps, but one timestep each with a 19-20m jump between two "attractor" depths (e.g. column (9,2): `~46.34m` at t=0-2, then MITgcm drops to `~27m` at t=3-4 while Python stays near `46.36m`) — consistent with the already-characterized Rib/Ricr threshold-crossing timing sensitivity (1DMIX-019/1DMIX-022), just with an unusually large jump size.
- **Persistent, moderate-to-large differences across ALL 5 timesteps** at 8 other columns (`(1,3)`, `(1,4)`, `(2,4)`, `(2,5)`, `(2,6)`, `(3,1)`, `(5,6)`, `(9,4)`; diffs 1.5-4m, one reaching 14-16m at `(2,4)`,t=3-4) — NOT a one-off threshold flip, a genuinely different, systematic-looking effect not previously seen in any prior experiment.

**Column `(x=6,y=6)` root-caused as far as the current harness allows — genuinely new candidate mechanism identified, not yet fully confirmed.** This column (6 real wet levels of 23, water depth ~110m, `theta` -1.8 to 2.5°C — a real, non-degenerate Arctic-shelf-like profile) has Python stuck at **exactly `min_kpp_hbl=5.0`** at all 5 timesteps while MITgcm reports a real, smoothly-varying `~15.3-15.4m`. Ruled out first (before concluding anything else): (1) NOT the 1DMIX-032 masking bug — `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`'s own `_wet_level_count`/1DMIX-011 truncation is already correctly applied in the real capture this compares against (confirmed by re-deriving with the same 6-level truncation applied explicitly: identical result); (2) NOT `KPP_ESTIMATE_UREF` — `seaice_obcs` has no custom `KPP_OPTIONS.h`, uses the `pkg/kpp` default (`#undef`). Then, the same decisive isolation method from 1DMIX-032 (feed MITgcm's own `dVsq`/`buoy_freq_sq`/`Ritop` directly into Python's `diagnose_bl_depth`) was tried here too — **and, unlike vermix, does NOT reproduce MITgcm's `hbl`** (still comes out exactly 5.0), meaning this is a genuinely different situation from 1DMIX-032: the divergence is not purely upstream of the search.

Tracing through `diagnose_bl_depth` by hand with the fed-in values: the raw (pre-limiter) bulk-Richardson crossing interpolates to `hbl≈5.63m` (a real crossing found at `kbl=1`, between the first two levels — itself notable: `Rib` jumps from `0` to `4.79` in a single level step here, because this column's velocity shear is extremely weak, `dvsq~6e-5 m²/s²`, making the `Rib=Ritop/(dvsq+vtsq)` denominator tiny and the whole profile steep). The **`LimitHblStable` Ekman/Monin-Obukhov clamp** (`kpp_routines.F:781-792`, verified byte-identical to this port's `diagnose_bl_depth` implementation, formula-for-formula) then binds: with `bfsfc=bo+bosol≈6.85e-9` (a real but extremely small positive/stable buoyancy forcing for this weakly-forced column) and `ustar≈0.00185 m/s`, `hmonob=cmonob*ustar³/vonk/bfsfc≈2.3m` — far shallower than `hekman≈12.9m` — so `hlimit=min(hekman,hmonob)≈2.3m`, clamping the pre-floor `hbl` down to `2.3m`, which the `minKPPhbl=5.0` floor then raises back up to exactly `5.0`.

**This is not yet a proven bug, and is not yet proven to be inert noise either — it needs one more piece of ground truth this project doesn't currently capture.** The formula is confirmed correct against `kpp_routines.F` directly (own comment there: `"c ph: test for zero nominator"`, i.e. MITgcm's own authors flagged this exact `ustar³/bfsfc` ratio as numerically delicate when `bfsfc→0`). What's missing to fully resolve it: the captured `richardson` field is **not** the bulk `Rib` used by `bldepth` (it's `dbloc/shsq`, the *local* gradient Richardson number used by the separate, interior-mixing `Ri_iwmix` routine — confirmed directly from `kpp_calc.F`'s own `OUTPUT_DIAGNOSTICS` format string) — MITgcm's real internal `bldepth` `Rib`/`bfsfc`/`hlimit` values are not captured anywhere in the current harness, for any experiment. Without them, it's not possible to tell whether MITgcm's own real run hits the same near-zero-`bfsfc` fragility (in which case this is a more severe instance of the already-characterized 1DMIX-019/1DMIX-031 "ULP noise amplified by a near-zero-denominator ratio" pattern — inert, not a fixable defect) or whether there's a genuine, still-unfound discrepancy somewhere in this port's `vtsq`/`ws`-lookup evaluation for extremely-weak-shear columns specifically (a real, fixable bug). Resolving this cleanly needs new Fortran instrumentation in `kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F` dumping `bldepth`'s internal `Rib`/`bfsfc`/`hlimit` arrays directly (the same kind of targeted, additive capture-extension already done twice this session for 1DMIX-033/1DMIX-032's `KPP_ESTIMATE_UREF`) — not attempted this iteration; flagging honestly as narrowed-but-not-closed rather than either overclaiming a fix or dismissing it as noise without evidence.

The other 8 "persistent" columns and the isolated-jump columns were not individually traced with this same method this iteration — worth doing so before assuming they all share the `(6,6)` mechanism (some may be ordinary Rib/Ricr threshold sensitivity, others may share the near-zero-`bfsfc` fragility; not yet distinguished).

**Column `(6,6)` fully root-caused (2026-09-20T08:30Z) — split to new issue 1DMIX-034.** Built exactly the missing instrumentation identified above: new `KPPMIX` output arguments exposing `bldepth`'s real `Rib`/final `bfsfc` (both already computed locally, purely additive), new `OUTPUT_RIB`/`OUTPUT_BFSFC` dumps in `kpp_calc.F`, new `bulk_ri`/`bfsfc_final` fields in `parse_mitgcm_split.py`. Rebuilt/reran `seaice_obcs` (Docker), confirmed byte-identical `hbl` to the pre-instrumentation capture (purely additive, verified). Feeding MITgcm's real ground truth into `diagnose_bl_depth` this time does **NOT** reproduce MITgcm's `Rib` (off by 20-330x) or `bfsfc` (MITgcm's is negative, Python's is positive) — a real, substantial, non-noise divergence. Root-caused to `useSALT_PLUME=.TRUE.` (confirmed active for `seaice_obcs`, uniquely among all tested KPP experiments) plus a genuine, total absence of salt-plume physics in the Python port's `bfsfc` computation — compounded by the capture harness never recording whether salt plume is active at all, silently defeating the port's own existing `NotImplementedError` safety guard for this exact case. Full details, evidence, and proposed two-part fix (harness capture + physics port) in **1DMIX-034**.

**All 11 remaining traced columns checked against the salt-plume signature (2026-09-20T08:50Z) — resolves the "not yet distinguished" question above.** Compared each column's real `bfsfc_final` (MITgcm) against `bo+bosol` alone (the Python port's own, salt-plume-free formula) across all 5 timesteps, using the same `output_docker_kpp_validation_saltflag` capture (no new build needed):

- **Full sign-flip (same mechanism as `(6,6)`, salt plume dominates bfsfc)**: `(1,4)`, `(2,4)`, `(2,6)`, `(5,6)` — 4 of the 8 "persistent" columns. MITgcm's real `bfsfc` is negative while `bo+bosol` alone is positive, gap magnitude `5e-9` to `1.2e-8` (comparable to `(6,6)`'s `1.6e-8`), consistently at every timestep. These are directly, fully explained by 1DMIX-034 — same mechanism, same fix needed.
- **Same-sign, smaller perturbation (3-15% of `bfsfc`'s own magnitude)**: `(1,3)`, `(2,5)`, `(3,1)`, `(9,4)` — the other 4 "persistent" columns — and all 3 "isolated-jump" columns `(8,3)`, `(9,2)`, `(9,3)`. `useSALT_PLUME` is a domain-wide runtime flag (not per-column), so every column has *some* `boplume` contribution, but its magnitude varies spatially with the local salt flux; in these columns it's real but too small to flip `bfsfc`'s sign. Plausible combined explanation, not yet individually re-traced further: salt plume contributes a genuine few-percent nudge to `bfsfc`, which — for the "isolated-jump" columns specifically, already characterized as sitting near a Rib/Ricr threshold crossing (1DMIX-019/1DMIX-022's established mechanism) — could be exactly the nudge that flips which timestep the threshold is crossed on, without needing a full sign flip. Not proven to that level of detail this iteration (would need the same "feed MITgcm's own `Rib`/`bfsfc` into `diagnose_bl_depth`" isolation method run per-column, not attempted here), but the underlying salt-plume contribution itself is now directly confirmed present in every one of these columns too, not absent.

Net: 1DMIX-034's missing salt-plume physics is now confirmed to affect **all 12** traced `seaice_obcs` columns (`(6,6)` plus these 11) to some degree — it's a domain-wide configuration, not a `(6,6)`-specific quirk — fully dominating `bfsfc`'s sign for 5 of the 12 (`(6,6)`, `(1,4)`, `(2,4)`, `(2,6)`, `(5,6)`), and contributing a smaller but real perturbation to the remaining 7. This resolves 1DMIX-025's original per-column-breakdown open question.

**`isomip` captured and compared this iteration (2026-09-26T13:00Z-onward) — real capture obtained, real port defect found and split to new issue 1DMIX-038; two real, non-obvious harness defects found and fixed along the way (one beyond what this iteration's own brief anticipated).**

**Premise (1) confirmed exactly as stated**: `isomip/input.obcs`'s own `data.shelfice` has `SHELFICEselectDragQuadr=1` (`.GE.0`), and `packages.conf` includes `shelfice`, so `ALLOW_SHELFICE` is genuinely defined for the whole build (not dead code) — `ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F`'s `#ifdef ALLOW_SHELFICE` branch (sub-glacial-drag `uStarSquare`, lines ~796-836; TKE Dirichlet BC moved from `kSrf=1` to `kSrf=MAX(1,kTopC)`, lines ~898-914) is live, and the existing `GGL90_OUTPUT_VALIDATION` call site (line ~1215) is confirmed positioned after both branches, so the captured `u_star_sq` already reflects the ShelfIce value with no new instrumentation needed for that part.

**Premise (2) confirmed directly against `isomip`'s real geometry, quantitatively, before touching any harness code**: `gendata.py` derives `icetopo.obcs`/`iceShelf_Mass.obcs` from `../input/icetopo.exp1`/`iceShelf_Mass.bin` with `topo[y0=50:,:]=0` (open ocean for the northern half) and a nonzero, spatially-varying ice draft (-700m to -200m, capped) for the southern half (`y<50`); `bathy.box` (from `gendata.m`: `bathy=-900` everywhere except the two `i=1`/`j=1` land walls) is completely flat. Parsing the real capture's `theta`/`salt` directly: of 5000 columns, 149 are true land (both `theta`/`salt` exactly 0.0 at every level — the two walls), 2450 are fully wet (open-ocean half), and **2401 have a real, contiguous dry-top-then-wet-below profile** (`kTopC` from 6 to 22, always wet through to the true seafloor at the full 30th level) — **0** have the old dry-bottom-only shape (expected, since bathymetry here is flat). This is the majority of the non-land domain, not an edge case.

**New finding, beyond this iteration's own brief — the Fortran capture itself, not just the Python harness, was silently discarding the entire ice-shelf-covered half of the domain.** `ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F`'s `GGL90_OUTPUT_VALIDATION` subroutine has its own land-exclusion guard, `IF ( maskC(i,j,1,bi,bj) .EQ. 0. ) CYCLE` — checking only level 1's mask. This is a correct land test for every previously-tested experiment (none use `ALLOW_SHELFICE`; ordinary bathymetry masking is monotonic from the surface, so "level 1 dry" and "every level dry" are equivalent there) but WRONG for `isomip`: a real, wet, ice-shelf-covered column has `maskC(i,j,1,...)==0` (ice-covered at the top) while still being wet from `kTopC` to the seafloor, and the old guard `CYCLE`'d past every one of them. Confirmed directly and quantitatively on the first real capture attempt: `BI=1..2,BJ=1..2` (the 4 southernmost, ice-shelf-covered tiles) had **exactly 0** `INPUT_STATE` lines each, while `BI=*,BJ=3..4` (open-ocean tiles) were fully populated — not a parsing artifact, the data was never written. Fixed by replacing the level-1-only check with a loop over all `Nr` levels (`landColumn = .TRUE.` unless any level's `maskC` is nonzero) in `ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F` (shared by every GGL90 experiment's `code_validation/`). **Regression-proven, not just "no test failures"**: rebuilt+reran `vermix`'s existing GGL90 capture (single-column, always-wet, fastest available) with the fix and diffed byte-for-byte against a fresh pre-fix rerun (the old archived `output_docker_ggl90_1dmix024` capture turned out to predate the 1DMIX-028 `OUTPUT_RI_SHEAR_PR` instrumentation, so a fresh apples-to-apples pre-fix rebuild was used instead) — every line identical except build-host/timestamp/CPU-timing lines. After the fix, rebuilding+rerunning `isomip` (`experiment_compile.sh isomip -mods .../isomip/code_validation -build build_docker_ggl90_isomip -clean -j 8`, `arch -arm64`) now correctly captures all 8 tiles; per-tile wet-column counts (576/600/600/600/600/625/625/625) exactly match the two land walls' expected exclusion, confirming the fix is both necessary and physically correct.

**Setup step needed (documented, not a bug, matching the established `seaice_obcs`/1DMIX-025 precedent)**: `isomip/input.obcs` has no `prepare_run` script of its own (unlike `input_ad`/`input_tap`/`input_oad`, which each symlink `bathy.box`/`icetopo.exp1`/`phi0surf.exp1.jmd95z` from `../input`) — the first run attempt hit `STOP ABNORMAL END: S/R MDS_READ_FIELD: bathy.box.data: File does not exist`. **Process self-correction, worth recording**: first worked around this by symlinking `bathy.box` directly inside `isomip/input.obcs/` — which, on reflection, is editing the read-only MITgcm checkout (`esx/project_profile.md`'s explicit "never edited by this project's agents" rule), not this project's own `mitgcm_verification_mods/`. Caught and corrected before reporting: removed that symlink, and instead copied the real `bathy.box` file (not a symlink — a relative symlink placed directly in the output directory does not resolve correctly, since Docker mounts the output directory and the MITgcm root as two separate, non-nested volumes, `/output` and `/mitgcm`) directly into the run's own output directory (`output_docker_ggl90_isomip/bathy.box`, a build/run artifact location, never part of the tracked checkout). Reran end-to-end with this corrected process and confirmed the resulting `output.txt` is byte-for-byte identical (aside from build-host/timestamp/CPU-timing lines) to the original run — the correction changed nothing scientific, only how the missing file was supplied (`STOP NORMAL END`, 12 timesteps × 8 tiles, both ways).

**Premise (2)'s Python-harness half confirmed and fixed**: `run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py`'s `_wet_level_count` (mirrored verbatim in `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`) found the first `theta==0.0` level scanning from index 0, implicitly assuming the wet region always starts at 0 — for any of the 2401 dry-top-wet-below columns, `theta[0]==0.0`, so it returned 0, and `run()`'s `if nz_wet == 0: continue` silently skipped every one of them (leaving zero-initialized output) even after the Fortran-side fix above made their data available. Replaced with `_wet_level_range(theta) -> (start, count)` in both scripts (raises `ValueError` on a non-contiguous wet region rather than silently mis-slicing — not observed in this capture) and updated all 3 call sites (`run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py::run`, `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py::_process_column`/`::run`) to slice `[start:start+count]` and pad NaN (GGL90) / zero (KPP, matching its own pre-existing below-seafloor convention) on both sides rather than only below. `_is_land_column` needed no change (already checks every level, not just level 0). **Regression proof, per this issue's own explicit ask**: full suite `56 passed, 3 skipped` (unchanged); reran the Python GGL90 replay against `global_oce_latlon`'s existing capture (real land + fully-wet mixed domain) with the fixed script and confirmed every field (`visc_az`/`diff_kz`/`mixing_length`/`tke_after`) `np.array_equal(..., equal_nan=True) == True` against the pre-fix archived `python_ggl90_outputs.nc` — exact behavioral no-op for every previously-tested case. `run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py`'s run against the fixed `isomip` capture completed with no `ValueError`, confirming the wet region really is contiguous for every one of the 4851 real columns captured. The KPP-side fix is proactive (no KPP+`ALLOW_SHELFICE` capture exists yet) but keeps both scripts' shared helper mirrored rather than leaving KPP's copy with the same latent gap for a future ice-shelf KPP experiment. **Related, not fixed this iteration**: `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py`'s `derive_raw_flux_forcing` callers (`_process_column`, `run`) both pass `salt_surf=salt[0]` (the raw, full-column array's own index 0) for the freshwater-flux conversion `fw_flux = saltflux/(rho_const*salt_surf) - empmr/rho_const` — for a future real dry-top KPP capture, `salt[0]` would be exactly `0.0` under an ice shelf, dividing by zero. Noted here rather than guessed at a fix, since it needs `wet_start` computed before that call (a small reordering, not attempted without a real KPP+`ALLOW_SHELFICE` capture to verify against).

**Comparison result (`compare_ggl90.py` plus a deeper median/p95/max-per-mechanism breakdown, matching prior experiments' rigor) — real, evidenced, partially root-caused new mismatch, split to 1DMIX-038.** Over all 1,437,204 wet cells (4851 columns × 12 timesteps × 30 levels, minus NaN-padding, minus the below-seafloor/above-ice-shelf excluded regions): `visc_az` median/p95/max abs diff `0/0/3.0e-7` (clean); `diff_kz` `0/0/2.9e-3`; `mixing_length` `4.7e-8/4.3e-5/8.6e-3`; `tke_after` `0/8.2e-15/7.6e-5`. **Compile-only check (both experiments, per this issue's own established discipline)**: set up `mitgcm_verification_mods/global_ocean_90x40x15/code_validation/` and `.../global_ocean_cs32x15/code_validation/`, each symlinking `ggl90_calc.F` to `ggl90_mods/` and copying **the experiment's own real `code/GGL90_OPTIONS.h` verbatim** (both experiments' own headers already `#define ALLOW_GGL90_IDEMIX` at line 25, confirmed directly -- no hand-edit needed, unlike the brief's own precautionary assumption; cs32x15's own header additionally `#define GGL90_IDEMIX_CVMIX_VERSION`, 90x40x15's leaves it `#undef`'d -- a real, confirmed difference in which IDEMIX formula variant each experiment's own reference config exercises, `ggl90_idemix.F:166-217`). `-norun` compile succeeded on the first attempt for both (`experiment_compile.sh global_ocean.90x40x15/global_ocean.cs32x15 -mods .../code_validation -build build_docker_ggl90_idemix90/idemixcs32 -j 8`, `arch -arm64`).

**New Fortran instrumentation, regression-proven purely additive.** Added `IDEMIXgTKEOutput` (real `IDEMIX_gTKE(i,j,k)`, the internal-wave-energy dissipation term that is IDEMIX's actual additional TKE source -- chose **not** to also capture `IDEMIX_E` itself, since `IDEMIX_RiNumber`/the TKE budget both only ever use `IDEMIX_gTKE`, never `IDEMIX_E` directly, so `IDEMIX_E` adds no comparison value this port would need) to `ggl90_mods/ggl90_calc.F`'s `GGL90_OUTPUT_VALIDATION`, always declared/zeroed unconditionally (mirrors `GGL90viscOutput`'s own established pattern) so it stays a genuine no-op for every non-IDEMIX experiment's build. **Also fixed a real, latent capture gap found while adding this**: `GGL90riOutput`/`GGL90shearOutput` (1DMIX-028) were only ever filled in the non-IDEMIX (`ELSE`) branch of the Prandtl-number computation -- invisible until now since no `useIDEMIX=.TRUE.` capture had ever been attempted; filled identically in the IDEMIX branch too. Regression-proof (own reconstructed pre-edit file, byte-diffed against the real post-edit file to confirm the change is exactly these lines and nothing else, then rebuilt+reran `vermix`'s existing GGL90 capture before/after): `INPUT_STATE`/`INPUT_TKE`/`INPUT_SIGMAR`/`INPUT_FORCING`/`OUTPUT_MIXING`/`OUTPUT_TKE`/`OUTPUT_RI_SHEAR_PR` all byte-identical (520 lines each, 20 lines for `INPUT_FORCING`); the only new content is `OUTPUT_IDEMIX_HEADER` + 520 `OUTPUT_IDEMIX` lines, all exactly `0.0000000000000000E+00` (vermix has no `ALLOW_GGL90_IDEMIX`, as expected). Updated `scripts/parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py` to parse the new `OUTPUT_IDEMIX` tag into a new `idemix_gtke` output field (`have_idemix_gtke`/`idemix_gtke_data` attr, mirroring the `ri_shear_pr` precedent).

**`global_ocean.90x40x15/input.idemix` capture: real setup gap found and fixed, real capture obtained, IDEMIX gap decisively quantified.** First run attempt hit `STOP ABNORMAL END: S/R MDS_READ_FIELD: bathymetry.bin.001.001.data ... Files DO not exist` -- this experiment's real `SIZE.h` tiling (`sNx=10,sNy=10,nSx=9,nSy=4` = 36 tiles, `nPx=nPy=1`) makes MITgcm's default MDS reader expect each 2D input field pre-split per-tile, which the shared, plain-flat `tutorial_global_oce_latlon/input/*.bin` files (this experiment's own `bathyFile`/`hydrogThetaFile`/etc., confirmed identical filenames) are not -- the same root cause `global_oce_latlon`'s own `input_validation/README.md` already documented for its differently-tiled domain. Fixed the same way: added `useSingleCpuIO=.TRUE.` -- but since `input.idemix/data` is read-only checkout source and the run harness's own `ln -sf .../input.idemix/* .` step would silently clobber any pre-placed override of the same filename, created a new, project-added, untracked `global_ocean.90x40x15/input_docker_idemix90/` directory (same category as this project's own `build_docker_*`/`output_docker_*` artifact dirs, never part of the tracked checkout) symlinking every one of `input.idemix`'s real files plus the 8 shared forcing binaries, with only `data` itself a real (non-symlink) copy carrying the one added line. Reran with `-input_docker_idemix90` as the input dir: `STOP NORMAL END`, 10 timesteps × 36 tiles (35 wet + 1 fully-blanked per `data.exch2.mpi`'s `blankList=30`), 347250 `INPUT_STATE`/`OUTPUT_IDEMIX` lines (23150 wet column-timesteps), 269506/347250 (77.6%) of `IDEMIX_gTKE` values genuinely nonzero (median(nonzero) 1.7e-11, p95 5.7e-9, max 0.97 m²/s³).

Parsed (`parse_mitgcm_ggl90_split.py`, grid `90x40x15`, 9x4 tiles confirmed) and replayed the Python port (`run_ggl90_from_netcdf_input.py`, single-step replay, ~36s). First replay attempt raised `ValueError: tke_min must be greater than zero` -- this experiment's real `data.ggl90` sets `GGL90TKEmin=0.E-6` (a genuinely valid MITgcm value: `ggl90_calc.F`'s only use is `MAX(GGL90TKE,GGL90TKEmin)`, a non-negativity floor with no positive lower bound, matching the model contract's own already-documented TKE≥0 invariant exactly), but `GGL90Parameters._validate()` rejected `tke_min<=0` where `tke_bottom`'s own adjacent check one line below correctly uses `<0` -- an inconsistent, overly-strict bound, not a scientific choice (every Python use site is `sqrt(max(tke,tke_min))`/`max(tke,tke_min)`, never a divisor, confirmed by grep). Fixed (`<=0`→`<0`, matching `tke_bottom`'s own pattern) as a small, self-contained, in-scope validation-bound correction, not a physics change. After the fix: `visc_az` max_abs 5.6, median_abs 2.1e-17 (n_gt_1pct 183/485840); `diff_kz` max_abs 2.7, median_abs 3.6e-14 (**n_gt_1pct 247456/485840, 51%**); `mixing_length` max_abs 15.5, median_abs 9.4e-9 (206/485840); `tke_after` max_abs 446 (**n_gt_1pct 264515/485840, 54%**). **Decisively attributed to the missing IDEMIX term, not a new confound**: `corr(deltaT*IDEMIX_gTKE, mit_tke_after - py_tke_after)` over the 243692 column-timesteps where the naive product exceeds `1e-8` = **0.998** -- the observed mismatch tracks the real, captured missing source term almost perfectly (median ratio diff/naive ≈0.008, consistent with the implicit tridiagonal solve damping/redistributing the raw explicit contribution before the final output, not a 1:1 pass-through). `visc_az`/`mixing_length`'s much smaller mismatch count (`~0.04%` vs `diff_kz`/`tke_after`'s `>50%`) is itself informative: IDEMIX's TKE-budget and Prandtl-number effects propagate into `diff_kz`/`tke_after` far more pervasively than into `visc_az`/mixing length directly, matching the formulas above (`KappaM`/mixing-length don't depend on `IDEMIX_gTKE` at all; only `TKEPrandtlNumber`→`diff_kz` and the TKE update→`tke_after` do). This is now a clean, decisive, fully-evidenced quantification of the real, expected IDEMIX physics gap for a genuine z-coordinate experiment.

**`global_ocean.cs32x15/input.in_p` capture: real capture obtained after 4 real, evidenced setup gaps -- but its comparison is confounded by a much larger, pre-existing, newly-confirmed structural gap, not primarily IDEMIX. Split to new issue 1DMIX-040.** `input.in_p` has its own real `prepare_run` (symlinks `grid_cs32.face00?.bin` from `../../tutorial_held_suarez_cs/input`, `core_*_cs32.bin` from `../input.icedyn`, `data.exf`/`data.seaice`/`runoff_temperature.bin` from `../input.seaice`) -- ran it directly (same documented-setup-step precedent as `seaice_obcs`'s own `prepare_run`, creating symlinks inside `input.in_p/` itself, not editing any existing tracked file). Four further missing-sibling-file `STOP ABNORMAL END`s followed, each fixed the same way (symlinked from the sibling `input/` dir that already has the real file, confirmed by grepping every `data`/`data.exf` filename reference against what `prepare_run` actually linked): `eedata` (present in `input/`/`input_ad/`, absent from `input.in_p/`'s own `prepare_run` scope entirely), `regMask_lat24.bin` (`data.diagnostics`'s `diagSt_regMaskFile`), `lev_surfT_cs_12m.bin`/`lev_surfS_cs_12m.bin` (`data.exf`'s `climsstfile`/`climsssfile`), `trenberth_taux.bin`/`trenberth_tauy.bin` (`data.exf`'s `ustressfile`/`vstressfile` -- confirmed genuinely active, unlike the commented-out `#uwindfile`/`#vwindfile`='core_u_wind_cs32.bin'/'core_v_wind_cs32.bin' initially flagged by an over-broad grep that didn't exclude `#`-commented lines). **Real process mistake found and caught mid-run, before it produced misleading evidence**: the first actual run attempt used the STALE, pre-instrumentation `build_docker_ggl90_idemixcs32` binary (compiled during this iteration's earlier compile-only check, *before* the `IDEMIX_gTKE` Fortran edits were made) -- produced a complete `STOP NORMAL END` run with 663000 real `OUTPUT_MIXING`/`OUTPUT_RI_SHEAR_PR` lines but **zero** `OUTPUT_IDEMIX` lines and no `OUTPUT_IDEMIX_HEADER` at all (silently missing, not zero-filled) -- caught by comparing tag counts before trusting the capture, not discovered downstream. Rebuilt (`-clean`) with the current instrumented source and reran: `STOP NORMAL END`, 663000 `OUTPUT_IDEMIX` lines (matching every other per-level tag exactly), 511020/663000 (77%) genuinely nonzero.

**The real finding**: this experiment's `data` sets `buoyancyRelation='OCEANICP'`, which MITgcm's own `ini_parms.F:454-455` maps directly to `usingPCoords=.TRUE.` -- confirmed directly from source, not inferred. `global_ocean.cs32x15` runs in **pressure coordinates** (`delR` in the namelist is in Pa, not m: `7105181.6, 6571679.7, ...`), and this project's entire Python GGL90 port has **no pressure-coordinate path at all** -- not a new discovery of a hidden bug, but the first real-world confirmation of an already-self-documented limitation: `ggl90_scheme_specific.py::_limit_method_2`'s own docstring already states "z-coordinate branch -- this project has no p-coordinate/atmosphere path" (pre-existing comment, found by direct grep, not written by this iteration). The captured grid geometry (`rC`/`rF`/`drF`) is genuinely in Pa (~1e7, matching `delR`), and the harness/Python port pass it straight through as if they were meters, with no `coordFac=g*rhoConst` conversion anywhere in the Python code (confirmed absent by grep; MITgcm's own real Fortran applies this conversion pervasively -- `ggl90_calc.F`'s own viscosity floor, `ggl90_idemix.F`'s entire `Nsquare`/group-velocity computation, `ggl90_mixinglength.F`'s every length-ceiling term). Point-verified on the single worst-mismatched cell (t=4,x=53,y=12,k=5): real MITgcm `mixing_length=1277m` (physically sensible) vs. Python `14493349m` (14,493km -- larger than Earth's radius), a ~10⁴x error entirely explained by feeding pressure-in-Pa (`rC[5]≈2.05e7`) through length-ceiling formulas that assume the same numbers are depth-in-meters. This dominates every field's comparison (`diff_kz` n_gt_1pct 510536/813820 = 63%, `mixing_length` similarly, both with median_abs already at O(1) before even reaching the tail) -- **an order of magnitude larger effect than IDEMIX's own contribution**, making this capture's raw comparison numbers meaningless as an IDEMIX-specific signal (the 90x40x15 result above is the clean one for that purpose). A second, smaller, also-newly-confirmed gap in the same capture: this experiment's `data.ggl90` sets `calcMeanVertShear=.TRUE.` (also never previously exercised); `GGL90Parameters.calc_mean_vert_shear` is declared/mapped from `PARAM_calcMeanVertShear` but -- exactly like `use_idemix` -- never read by any physics function (confirmed by grep across `ggl90_core_driver.py`/`ggl90_scheme_specific.py`/`ggl90_mixing_coefficients.py`, zero hits); its own standalone contribution could not be isolated from this capture (the p-coordinate confound dominates too heavily to separate the two by inspection alone).

Filed as **new issue 1DMIX-040** (both the p-coordinate gap and the `calc_mean_vert_shear` dead-placeholder finding) rather than fixed here: real p-coordinate support would need a `coordFac`-equivalent conversion threaded through every depth-dependent formula in the GGL90 (and likely KPP) port -- a substantial, cross-cutting scope decision (this project's own scope statement doesn't currently restrict to z-coordinates, but `docs/model_contract.md`'s entire grid/coordinate section is written exclusively in z-coordinate terms), not a bounded bug fix, and squarely an escalation-worthy substantial-scope question per this project's own risk policy, not something to guess-implement under this harness_change issue. Documented the newly-confirmed boundary in `docs/model_contract.md` (grid topology section) and `esx/project_profile.md` (known capability boundaries) so this is a documented project-level scope statement, not just one function's own internal comment.

**Net for this issue, now fully closed**: both of 1DMIX-025's originally-enumerated remaining experiments are captured. `global_ocean.90x40x15` gives a clean, decisive, z-coordinate IDEMIX-gap quantification (0.998 correlation with the real captured source term). `global_ocean.cs32x15`'s capture is real and complete, but its comparison is dominated by a newly-confirmed, pre-existing, structural (not IDEMIX-specific) limitation, split to 1DMIX-040 along with a second, smaller, related dead-placeholder finding. No further scope remains open under 1DMIX-025 itself.

This closure covers all 6 iterations of this issue (isomip/seaice_obcs/global_oce_latlon/1D_ocean_ice_column/lab_sea/vermix captures across earlier iterations, this final iteration's global_ocean.90x40x15/global_ocean.cs32x15 IDEMIX capture). ## RESOLVED: Python GGL90/KPP ports have no pressure-coordinate (`usingPCoords`) support -- Arch's substantial-scope decision: permanently out of scope, plus disposition of the related dead `calc_mean_vert_shear` placeholder

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-26T20:15:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-26T21:45:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-040
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_scheme_specific.py::_limit_method_2`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_parameters.py::GGL90Parameters.calc_mean_vert_shear`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/column_grid.py`; `docs/model_contract.md` (grid topology); `esx/project_profile.md` (known capability boundaries); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/global_ocean_cs32x15/`

### Issue
Does this project's GGL90 (and, by the same reasoning, KPP) Python port correctly handle an MITgcm run configured in pressure coordinates (`usingPCoords=.TRUE.`, set via `buoyancyRelation='OCEANICP'`)? Confirmed no, with a genuine p-coordinate capture (`global_ocean.cs32x15/input.in_p`) silently producing a ~4-order-of-magnitude `mixing_length` error (`1277m` real vs. `14493km` ported, same cell) rather than an error, because this port has no `coordFac`-equivalent unit conversion anywhere (`column_grid.py`'s depth/thickness handling assumes metres unconditionally). A related, smaller finding in the same capture: `data.ggl90` sets `calcMeanVertShear=.TRUE.`, a real, physically distinct alternate vertical-shear formula (`ggl90_calc.F:468-498`) that `GGL90Parameters.calc_mean_vert_shear` captures/maps correctly but that no physics function in this port ever reads (same "declared-but-dead" pattern as `use_idemix`). This issue's own filing escalated the pressure-coordinate question as a substantial-scope decision (implement real `coordFac` support vs. declare out of scope) rather than actioning either alternative directly, per this project's escalation policy for substantial-scope changes.

### Resolution and justification
Arch made the scope decision directly (not delegated, not re-derived by this closeout): **pressure-coordinate (`usingPCoords`) MITgcm configurations are declared permanently out of scope for this project.** Reasoning, for the record: (1) this project's own stated identity and Primary Goal (`CLAUDE.md`) is validating a 1D **ocean** vertical-mixing port against MITgcm across physically distinct **ocean** scenarios -- every one of those, and every one of this project's own 6 idealized scenarios, is inherently z-coordinate; pressure coordinates are primarily an atmospheric-model convention within MITgcm's own scope. (2) Of the entire 6-capture set surveyed under 1DMIX-025, exactly **one** (`global_ocean.cs32x15`) uses pressure coordinates -- not representative of this project's actual validation targets. (3) Implementing real `coordFac`-equivalent support would require a cross-cutting rewrite of shared, core numerical kernels (`column_grid.py`'s entire depth/thickness handling, GGL90's/KPP's mixing-length ceiling and TKE boundary formulas), introducing real regression risk to the z-coordinate paths that are this project's actual, demonstrated validation target, in service of a capability with no present demonstrated need. This is a genuine, final decision, not a deferral-in-disguise: real `coordFac` support will not be implemented under this project's current scope, and this boundary is not expected to be revisited absent a new demonstrated need (e.g. a future genuinely-p-coordinate ocean validation target, which none of this project's current scenarios or captures are).

`calc_mean_vert_shear` is disposed separately, on its own smaller merits: its only observed instance so far is in the now-out-of-scope `global_ocean.cs32x15` capture, so implementing the alternate shear formula now would be speculative. Deferred as a known dead-placeholder limitation (documented in `GGL90Parameters`'s own docstring alongside `use_idemix`'s identical pattern), to be revisited only if a future **z-coordinate** capture is ever found to set `calcMeanVertShear=.TRUE.` -- not implemented speculatively this iteration.

Documentation updated to state both as final, present-tense facts rather than open questions: `docs/model_contract.md`'s coordinate-conventions section (z-coordinates-only bullet) now states the pressure-coordinate boundary as resolved/permanent rather than "not yet fixed... pending a decision"; `esx/project_profile.md`'s known-capability-boundaries bullet likewise replaces its former "until that issue's substantial-scope decision is made" trailing clause with the actual decision and its rationale, and now also carries the `calc_mean_vert_shear` deferred-dead-placeholder mention alongside `use_idemix`'s.

### Verification and remaining bounds
This was a `kind: documentation` closeout (0 required reviewers) -- no scientific-source change was needed or made. `git diff --stat` confirmed to touch only `esx/project_profile.md`, `docs/model_contract.md`, `open_issues.md`, `closed_issues.md`; nothing under `Vertical_Mixing_Models/` or `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/`. No scientific-source signature change (confirmed via `project.py signature`, unchanged before/after). Full pytest suite rerun to confirm it stays green under a docs-only change (expected, and confirmed, unaffected): `Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests -q` -- see the independent-check evidence in this iteration's own agent report footer for the exact count. Remaining bound: this closeout is a documentation/scope decision only; the underlying `column_grid.py`/GGL90/KPP kernels are unchanged and remain z-coordinate-only, exactly as before this closure -- no code claim is made or implied by this resolution beyond making the existing, already-true code behavior an explicit, final, documented project boundary.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them -- all changes remain in the working tree). Bob (agent `317faf37-1fe9-46ef-a162-3667422bf1e8`) implemented this documentation-only closeout per Arch's own scope decision conveyed in the dispatch brief; `kind: documentation` requires 0 independent reviewers. Related issues: 1DMIX-025 (the six-capture survey whose final iteration split this issue off), 1DMIX-039 (a separate, already-resolved EOS/N² precision question from the same session, unrelated to and not touched by this closeout).

## RESOLVED: Ran all 6 idealized scenarios through the GGL90 standalone Fortran driver -- diagnostic mixing coefficients exact to floating-point roundoff; found and exactly root-caused a real TKE-tendency formula bug, escalated rather than fixed inline

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T05:43:11Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-27T06:57:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-041
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/mitgcm_verification_mods/ggl90_standalone_driver/`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/export_scenario_to_ggl90_driver.py`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/compare_scenario_ggl90_standalone.py`

### Issue
KPP already had all 6 idealized scenarios (`calm_baseline`, `arctic_convection`, `hurricane_wind`, `tropical_heating_diurnal`, `heavy_rain_freshening`, `combined_storm`) run through its own standalone Fortran driver (1DMIX-023, resolved). GGL90's equivalent driver (`ggl90_standalone_driver/`, built under 1DMIX-024) and its export/compare tooling existed but had never been exercised against any of the 6 scenarios -- only against `vermix`'s own MITgcm capture. This was the owner's explicitly confirmed top-priority item this session, and the actual answer to their separate question about a "GGL90 MITgcm wrapper like KPP's" (investigation established the real successor to KPP's own earlier, abandoned hand-rolled wrapper mechanism is this standalone-driver approach, not a new hand-rolled wrapper -- see 1DMIX-046's confirmed decision to delete the old `MITgcm_wrappers/KPP/` artifact outright).

### Result
All 6 scenarios exported, built, run and compared fresh (pre-existing 2026-09-19-dated driver artifacts on disk were found stale relative to later GGL90 source changes and correctly not reused). `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`mixing_length` are **exact to floating-point roundoff in every scenario** (max_rel ~4-5e-15, zero cells >1% relative error anywhere) -- the strongest possible confirmation that GGL90's diagnostic mixing-coefficient formulas match MITgcm's real `GGL90_CALC` exactly. `tke_after` (the one prognostic field) mismatched in every scenario (max abs diff up to 4.0e-06; up to 4082/115,000 cells >1% for `arctic_convection`) -- investigated per this issue's own instruction rather than averaged away, and exactly root-caused, not merely correlated: MITgcm's real TKE buoyancy term (`ggl90_calc.F:661,673`) uses `KappaH = KappaM/TKEPrandtlNumber` (floored only by the *viscosity* background), while the Python port's `GGL90Driver.compute_mixing` reuses the single exported `kappa_h` (additionally floored by the *diffusivity* background and capped by `diffMax` -- correct for the diagnostic output, wrong for this specific tendency term) for both purposes. The two diverge whenever `background_visc != background_diff`, true for all 6 scenarios' `viscAz=5e-5`/`diffKzS=1e-5`. Confirmed by direct substitution at the worst-mismatching cell in every one of the 6 scenarios: recomputing with the correct `KappaM/TKEPrandtlNumber` collapsed the residual from up to 4.0e-06 absolute to 1e-18–1e-20 (floating-point roundoff) in every case. Separately checked and ruled out (not reopened) as an explanation for `vermix`'s own long-standing unexplained `tke_after` 2/520 residual (1DMIX-015) -- a different, still-unidentified cause there; the two formulas happen to coincide at `vermix`'s specific mismatching cells.

### Resolution
This is a real, previously-undetected Python-port bug -- correctly **not** fixed within this issue (`kind: harness_change` forbids a scientific-source change without a workflow amendment and independent review; the fix is filed separately as **1DMIX-048** (`scientific_change` from the outset) with the exact recommended fix and full evidence, rather than deciding unilaterally or fixing silently). 1DMIX-041 itself is fully resolved on its own terms: the 6-scenario GGL90-standalone-driver coverage gap the owner named is closed, the diagnostic mixing coefficients are confirmed exact, and the one real discrepancy found was investigated to an exact root cause and escalated appropriately rather than closed over.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full write-up with per-scenario numbers, root-cause derivation and the recommended fix: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/README.md`, "GGL90 standalone driver: 6-scenario results and a real TKE-tendency bug (1DMIX-041)". Structural verification: `EXECUTED PASS` (`devel-loop/loop_state/verification/c36021c3702ca61fd736a3c4419538af7e1bf58d8779dff70435fadbc15babd8.json`). Full pytest fresh: `64 passed, 3 skipped`, unchanged from before this issue (consistent with zero source edits). Remaining bound: 1DMIX-048's fix has not landed; `tke_after` remains mismatched in all 6 scenarios (and, unverified, likely in some real MITgcm captures too) until that issue closes.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them -- all changes remain in the working tree). Bob (agent `48d9c5be-fc5d-4dbd-889e-07814f508c48`) ran the investigation; the dispatch was killed once by the harness's own 40-minute timeout after the substantive work had already landed, and resumed via `followup` on the same session to complete the report/footer without redoing any work. `kind: harness_change` requires 0 independent reviewers. Related issues: 1DMIX-023 (the KPP precedent this mirrors), 1DMIX-024 (built the GGL90 standalone driver this issue finally exercised), 1DMIX-015 (vermix's own separate, still-unresolved `tke_after` residual, checked and ruled out as the same mechanism), 1DMIX-048 (the new issue carrying the actual fix forward), 1DMIX-046 (confirmed deletion of the older, unrelated `MITgcm_wrappers/KPP/` artifact this issue's owner-priority note references).

## RESOLVED: Fixed GGL90's TKE buoyancy term to use MITgcm's real `KappaH` -- a real, previously-undetected Python-port correctness bug, exactly root-caused under 1DMIX-041 and now fixed with independent review

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T06:57:54Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-27T08:40:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-048
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_scheme_specific.py::compute_tke_buoyancy`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_mixing_coefficients.py::compute_viscosity_diffusivity`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/GGL90/ggl90_core_driver.py::GGL90Driver.compute_mixing`

### Issue
Found and exactly root-caused during 1DMIX-041's 6-scenario GGL90-standalone-driver run: MITgcm's real TKE buoyancy term (`ggl90_calc.F:661,673`) uses `KappaH = KappaM/TKEPrandtlNumber`, floored only by the viscosity background. The Python port's `GGL90Driver.compute_mixing` instead reused the diagnostic `kappa_h` (additionally floored by the diffusivity background and capped by `diffMax` -- correct for the output field only) for this specific tendency term too. The two diverge whenever `background_visc != background_diff`, true for all 6 idealized scenarios.

### Fix and verification
Added an internal-only `kappa_h_tendency = kappa_m / tke_prandtl_number` (unfloored by `background_diff`, uncapped by `diff_max`) in `compute_viscosity_diffusivity`, fed into `compute_tke_buoyancy` instead of `kappa_h`; `kappa_m`/`kappa_h`/`mixing_length`/`visc_az`/`diff_kz` remain untouched everywhere (confirmed byte-identical before/after). End-to-end: all 6 idealized scenarios collapse `tke_after` to floating-point roundoff (max_rel <=1.4e-11) through the real standalone `GGL90_CALC` Fortran driver. Checked all 5 real GGL90 MITgcm captures with real before/after `tke_after` diffs: `vermix` 2/520->0/520 and `1D_ocean_ice_column` 6622/253000->0/253000 (both full, unambiguous fixes -- this also resolves `vermix`'s own long-standing 1DMIX-015-era residual, correcting 1DMIX-041's own narrower single-cell-substitution conclusion that this mechanism didn't explain it: the real effect propagates through the coupled tridiagonal solve across the whole column, not just one cell); `global_ocean.90x40x15`/`global_ocean.cs32x15` unaffected (viscAz=diffKzS=0, or already out of scope per the 1DMIX-040 pressure-coordinate gap); `isomip` shows a genuinely mixed result -- worst-case magnitude improves 246x->2.3x but mismatch count rises slightly (17109->17934 of 1,437,204, +0.06pp), attributed to interaction with the already-closed 1DMIX-038 near-`kSrf` gap, not a new defect.

### Independent review
Richard: **APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`**, after an exceptionally thorough independent pass -- re-derived the formula from `ggl90_calc.F` himself using his own line-range selection; ran his own from-scratch scripts (not reused) replaying `vermix` and the **full** `isomip` capture (re-parsed from the raw 860MB MITgcm STDOUT himself, never touching Bob's saved `.nc`), reproducing every one of Bob's numbers exactly via an independently-built pipeline; ran his own fresh standalone-driver rerun of `arctic_convection` end-to-end; and, critically, probed 3 different `isomip` cells than the one Bob cited to generalize the mixed-result explanation, confirming `kappa_m`/mixing-length/N²/Prandtl all match MITgcm exactly at each while `tke_after` still mismatches 4.9-9.4% -- a real generalization, not a rubber stamp. Caught and self-corrected a real integrity issue mid-review (his own fresh driver rerun regenerated build byproducts, transiently staling the reviewed candidate; restored exact bytes, reconfirmed intact) and one mechanical evidence-staleness issue in his own first independent-check evidence (captured mid-excursion) -- fixed via a single cheap follow-up dispatch (refresh evidence only, no re-review). New minor observation not in Bob's report: 49 of the 1004 `isomip` "crossed-out" cells (4.9%) sit at grid row `y=50`, a different mechanism than the dominant `kSrf+2` pattern -- 0.003% of the full population, explicitly non-blocking per Richard's own assessment; logged here rather than reopening the already-closed 1DMIX-038 or filing a disproportionate new issue for something this small.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest fresh: `64 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed`, confirmed independently by Bob, Richard (twice), and this closeout. Structural verification: `EXECUTED PASS`. Final scientific verification (`final_verification.py`, the required gate for `scientific_change`): `EXECUTED PASS`. Remaining bound: `isomip`'s small mixed-result footnote above (49 cells, `y=50` row) is not investigated further -- logged for whoever next touches 1DMIX-038-adjacent territory, not a defect in this fix.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them -- all changes remain in the working tree). Bob (agent `4478dc82-fe92-43d8-9b70-ec3f44a2eb56`) implemented the fix; the dispatch was killed once by the harness's own 40-minute timeout mid-write after the substantive work had already landed, and resumed via `followup` to complete the report without redoing any work. Richard (agent `37e19c79-b19f-4e7a-bb10-692ae58195b6`) reviewed independently; his own first-pass independent-check evidence was mechanically stale (captured mid-excursion through his own driver rerun) and was refreshed via one cheap same-session follow-up dispatch, with his verdict and all other evidence carried unchanged. `kind: scientific_change`, filed as such from the outset (not amended later); 1 required reviewer, satisfied. Related issues: 1DMIX-041 (found and root-caused this bug, and is corrected by this issue's own more complete `vermix` finding), 1DMIX-038/1DMIX-015 (both closed, both referenced by this issue's evidence), 1DMIX-040 (the pressure-coordinate gap explaining why `global_ocean.cs32x15` is unaffected).

## RESOLVED: Extended KPP MITgcm-comparison regression coverage to the 11k-timestep 1D run, the 6-month lab_sea run, and seaice_obcs -- and found the ground truth for 2 of the 3 still carries the 1DMIX-035 OUTPUT_MIXING truncation defect

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T05:43:11Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-27T12:10:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-043
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests/test_kpp_mitgcm_validation_extended.py`; `KPP_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_mitgcm/`

### Issue
`test_kpp_mitgcm_validation.py` only exercised `1D_ocean_ice_column` (10-timestep) and `lab_sea` (20-timestep subsample). Not covered by any test: the 11,000-timestep `1D_ocean_ice_column` run, the 6-month/4368-timestep `lab_sea` run, `seaice_obcs` (salt-plume, 1DMIX-034), and `global_oce_latlon` (1DMIX-027) -- all real captures with completed, previously-reported comparisons but no pytest lock-in.

### Resolution
Wrote `test_kpp_mitgcm_validation_extended.py` (18 tests) as a sibling module to the existing `test_kpp_mitgcm_validation.py` (left untouched), mirroring `test_ggl90_mitgcm_validation.py`'s 1DMIX-042 structure, covering the 3 in-scope captures: 11k-timestep `1D_ocean_ice_column`, 6-month `lab_sea` (first 100 of 4368 timesteps subsampled, preloaded via `.load()` to avoid a >100x netCDF4-chunk-decompression slowdown against this capture's own chunking), and `seaice_obcs_1dmix034`. Every tolerance is a value measured fresh this round against current source via `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py::run_python_kpp_on_dataset`.

**Decisive-premise finding, made and handled before writing any assertion**: 2 of the 3 target captures (`mitgcm_kpp_outputs_11k_1D.nc`, dated 2026-08-19; `mitgcm_kpp_outputs_lab_sea_6mo.nc`, dated 2026-09-18) directly measured, this round, to still carry 1DMIX-035's own previously-diagnosed `OUTPUT_MIXING` early-truncation defect -- a pre-existing MITgcm-instrumentation bug (not a Python-port defect), already root-caused and fixed 2026-09-20, but not yet refreshed for these two captures: 51.809% of the 11k capture's 11,000 timesteps, and a real (subsample-dependent, ~4.1%-48.9%) fraction of the lab_sea_6mo capture, have `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t`/`ghat` exactly zero at every real interior interface level -- physically impossible given each capture's own nonzero background-viscosity floor (confirmed present, 1.93e-05, in both). `seaice_obcs_1dmix034` (generated 2026-09-26, after the fix) is confirmed clean (0.0%). Handled via an explicit, documented clean-subset filter (`_clean_mask`, keyed only on MITgcm's own captured `visc_az` -- never on Python agreement, so it cannot mask a real port defect) rather than comparing against known-corrupted zeros or silently dropping coverage; `hbl` (a separate, always-unconditional write path per 1DMIX-033) is unaffected and tested on the full sample in all 3 experiments. Additionally locked in the measured truncated fraction itself as a bounded assertion, so a future silent capture refresh would be caught rather than passing unnoticed against a now-stale bound.

`global_oce_latlon` (1DMIX-027) is explicitly out of scope: no capture file exists anywhere in this repo (confirmed by direct `find` across the whole tree and `/tmp`) -- the one 1DMIX-027 once produced, and its raw `output.txt`, are both gone (a since-passed disk-space rescue). Updated `docs/code_map.md`'s KPP core row and Verification-routes/Independent-oracle prose to cite the new file, name all 3 newly-covered captures, the truncation-defect finding, and the `global_oce_latlon` gap; also fixed a real link-integrity bug caught by `tools/esx/audit.py` after this closeout's own follow-up edits (a bare `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py::run_python_kpp_on_dataset` reference missing its directory prefix, unresolvable by `audit_code_map()`'s `excerpt()` resolver).

### New follow-up issues filed (scope_decisions, `separate_existing`)
Bob's own investigation surfaced two real, bounded pieces of work correctly split off rather than folded into this Python-side, no-new-MITgcm-runs issue:
- **1DMIX-049**: regenerate a permanent, full-year `global_oce_latlon` KPP capture (real Docker MITgcm rerun + reparse + permanentize + test) -- the one 1DMIX-027 produced no longer exists anywhere.
- **1DMIX-050**: refresh `mitgcm_kpp_outputs_11k_1D.nc`/`mitgcm_kpp_outputs_lab_sea_6mo.nc` via a real MITgcm rerun with the 1DMIX-035 `OUTPUT_MIXING` fix in place, then widen this issue's own clean-subset-filtered tests back to full, unfiltered coverage.

### Independent review
Richard (agent `0a36a72e-6a2c-4e3d-822f-5066e1722b74`, event `d55a1163a5004643afa0dfde9fc7e624`): **APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`**. Independently re-derived the truncation-defect finding from raw NetCDF himself (not trusting the docstring), varying both the oracle field (`diff_kz_t` vs `visc_az`, identical masks) and the subsample (an independent stride-50 `lab_sea_6mo` check, 48.9% vs. Bob's cited ~49%) -- matched Bob's numbers exactly/closely across all 4 measurements (11k 51.809%, lab_sea_6mo-first100 4.113%, lab_sea_6mo-stride50 48.9%, seaice_obcs 0.0%). Confirmed the background-viscosity floor is real in both affected captures (supporting the "exact zero is physically impossible" premise) and that `_clean_mask` keys only on MITgcm's own data, ruling out a Python-port defect being hidden. Confirmed no NaN land columns in any capture (validates `_ocean_mask`'s all-zero convention) and `ghat`'s real nonzero surface values (validates its `skip_surface=False` treatment). Read both new follow-up issues' full text directly and confirmed both are legitimate, bounded, real-MITgcm-rerun work matching established project precedent (1DMIX-027/1DMIX-035), not scope-dodging. Re-ran the full combined scientific suite fresh under his own owner id: `103 passed, 3 skipped`, matching Bob's claim exactly.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest fresh: `103 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed` (up from 85 before this issue, exactly +18) -- confirmed independently by Bob and Richard. Structural verification: `EXECUTED PASS`. Final scientific verification (`final_verification.py`, the required gate for `scientific_change`): `EXECUTED PASS`. Remaining bounds: 1DMIX-049 (missing `global_oce_latlon` capture) and 1DMIX-050 (stale truncated-capture ground truth for 2 of 3 captures) are tracked as new, separate, bounded follow-up issues -- not defects in this issue's own test-writing work.

Produced 5 fresh PDF reports (`vermix`, `1D_ocean_ice_column` 11,000-step, `isomip`, `global_ocean_90x40x15`, `global_ocean_cs32x15`) into a new, symmetric `GGL90_port_validation/reports/`. ## RESOLVED: Wrote `VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` -- the newcomer-readable results/discrepancy-analysis document the owner originally asked for

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T05:43:11Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-27T14:20:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-045
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` (new); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/README.md`

### Issue
`README.md` was accurate but terse (a status table + method description), not a deep-dive. The owner explicitly asked for "results (how well did the python ports work) and analysis on why there are disagreements (where there are disagreements)" in one readable place, not scattered across 50+ closed-issue entries. This was the actual deliverable behind the owner's original mandate for this whole session's work.

### Resolution
Wrote `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` (738 lines): a newcomer-readable narrative synthesis, organized per scheme (KPP, GGL90) and per experiment, stating for each what was tested and why, the quantified result, and -- wherever a real discrepancy exists -- its root cause in plain terms with a citation to the closed issue that established it. An "Overall picture" section up front enumerates the 5 understood discrepancy mechanisms (Rib/Ricr threshold sensitivity, IDEMIX missing-physics, pressure-coordinate gap, ShelfIce/EOS-pressure bugs now fixed, salt-plume/TKE-buoyancy bugs now fixed) before the per-experiment detail. Every number for the 10 experiments with a 1DMIX-044 report is a fresh, direct recomputation from the exact NetCDF pairs those PDF reports were generated from, using the report generators' own compute functions imported directly (not a new comparison, not copied from a PDF's printed text) -- confirmed by matching file-modification timestamps between each PDF and its source NetCDF pair, documented in the new file's own "Reproducibility" section with the exact pairs table. `global_oce_latlon` and the GGL90 6-scenario standalone-driver results are explicitly labeled historical (no fresh report exists / capture is gone, citing 1DMIX-049/1DMIX-051) rather than presented as current. A "Known, currently open gaps" section explicitly lists 1DMIX-049/1DMIX-050/1DMIX-051 plus `seaice_obcs`'s unresolved `bfsfc` fragility as not-yet-resolved, so nothing open is misrepresented as fixed. Added short pointers from `README.md` and `docs/code_map.md` to the new document.

## RESOLVED: Reorganized `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/`, deleted the superseded `MITgcm_wrappers/` wrapper (owner-authorized) and relocated/manifested the remaining stale material

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T05:43:11Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-27T15:20:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-046
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/archive_2026-08-investigation/`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/GGL90_port_validation/CAPTURES.md` (new); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/CAPTURES.md` (new); `esx/project.json:external_inputs`

### Issue
`MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/` had grown asymmetrically (`KPP_port_validation/` had `reports/`/`scripts/`/an archive subdirectory; `GGL90_port_validation/` had an empty `scripts/` and no `reports/`), carried a whole superseded, owner-confirmed-abandoned wrapper mechanism (`MITgcm_wrappers/`, 1.7MB/100 files), a disclaimed 2026-08 investigation's docs/scripts/PNGs scattered across the active tree, multiple unlabeled versioned capture files per experiment, and two oddly-located `_python.nc`-suffixed files of undocumented purpose.

### Resolution
**Task 1 (owner-authorized deletion)**: grepped the whole repo (`grep -rl "MITgcm_wrappers" --include="*.py" --include="*.md" .`) and confirmed the only references outside the directory itself were the issue-tracking files, the top-level README's directory tree, `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/README.md`'s own directory guide, and an unrelated, already-separately-archived copy under `docs/OLD_MARKDOWN_NO_LONGER_NEEDED/` -- zero active script/test dependency. Deleted `MITgcm_wrappers/` outright (100 git-tracked files via `git rm`, plus a handful of gitignored build artifacts -- `build/*.o`, `a.out`, `kpp_wrapper` -- left over on disk that `git rm` alone did not remove). Removed the now-dangling reference from both README directory listings.

**The preserved lesson** (the one thing worth keeping from that wrapper, per this issue's own instruction): Fortran `COMMON`-block variables assigned in a wrapper's own main program are **not** visible to subroutines that read the same block via `COMMON`/`INCLUDE` -- the assignment has to happen in a shared grid-initialization routine that includes the identical header the consuming subroutine also includes. The abandoned wrapper's own `handoff/FINAL_STATUS.md` hit this concretely: `maskC` (the ocean mask) and `fCori` set directly in the wrapper's main program were silently invisible to `KPP_CALC`, multiplying every output by zero (`maskC`) or corrupting the Ekman-layer depth (`fCori`), until moved into dedicated `INI_WRAPPER_GRID`/`SET_WRAPPER_GRID` routines that `#include` the same `GRID.h`. The standalone drivers built afterward (`kpp_standalone_driver/`, `ggl90_standalone_driver/`) never hit this class of bug at all, because using MITgcm's real `genmake2` build means every `COMMON` block is initialized by MITgcm's own real init routines rather than hand-written stubs -- the actual reason the later approach superseded the wrapper, not merely that it was newer.

**Task 2 (archive disposition -- chose relocate, not delete)**: grepped `compute_validation_statistics.py` (the one KPP `scripts/` file *not* part of this disclaimed investigation, despite living alongside it) and confirmed it has no dependency on `debug_timestep_2312.py`/`analyze_outlier_cluster.py`; those two scripts and the 10-PNG `outlier_diagnostics/` directory (all confirmed, via the archived `INVESTIGATION_CONCLUSION.md`'s own "Created Files" list, to be products of the exact same disclaimed 2026-08-20 investigation as the 3 already-archived markdown files) were `git mv`-relocated into `archive_2026-08-investigation/` alongside them, with a new `README.md` inside that directory stating the disclaimed status, why relocation was chosen over deletion (narrow historical value for a worked Rib/Ricr-threshold-sensitivity example, at negligible risk since everything is git-tracked either way), and why `MITgcm_wrappers/`'s stronger outright-deletion treatment does not set a precedent here (owner-confirmed abandonment vs. merely-superseded-with-residual-value are different cases).

**Task 3 (symmetry)**: removed `GGL90_port_validation/scripts/` (empty, untracked, unlikely ever needed -- the shared GGL90 report/comparison tooling already lives in the top-level `scripts/`). Also found and removed two further empty, untracked, purposeless directories in the same category while confirming no other asymmetry remained: `KPP_port_validation/inputs_from_python/` (empty since creation, superseded by the now-standard convention documented in `NETCDF_DATA_FORMAT.md`, which now carries an explicit "removed, never populated" status note rather than silently going stale) and confirmed both `*_port_validation/` directories now have symmetric `reports/`; remaining differences (`KPP_port_validation/scripts/`, `archive_2026-08-investigation/`, `SIMPLIFIED_API_SUMMARY.md`) are legitimate KPP-only extras with no GGL90 counterpart needed, not asymmetry to fix.

**Task 4 (capture manifests)**: wrote `KPP_port_validation/CAPTURES.md` and `GGL90_port_validation/CAPTURES.md`, one per validation directory, covering every experiment with more than one versioned capture (`vermix` for GGL90; `1D_10` and `11k_1D` for KPP). Current-vs-superseded status was established by (a) reading which exact filename each active test (`test_ggl90_mitgcm_validation.py`, `test_kpp_mitgcm_validation.py`, `test_kpp_mitgcm_validation_extended.py`) loads, (b) cross-checking `input_uuid`/`uuid` provenance attributes between paired input/output/python-output files directly via `xarray`, and (c) grepping every candidate filename against `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/{scripts,tests}` before calling anything "safe, no active reference." One real, documented-in-source finding surfaced while building the `1D_10` manifest entry: the legacy `mitgcm_kpp_inputs_1D_10_kppmix_extend.nc` capture is explicitly flagged, in `run_kpp_from_netcdf_input.py::derive_raw_flux_forcing`'s own docstring (1DMIX-013), as carrying non-raw, already-converted flux columns mislabeled as raw -- exactly why the current `_rawflux_fix` capture exists; the standalone-driver output paired to that legacy capture (`outputs_from_mitgcm_standalone/mitgcm_kpp_outputs_standalone_1D_10.nc`, from 1DMIX-012's original bring-up) was kept and documented rather than deleted, since the raw-flux bug affects fields the standalone driver never consumes. No file was deleted under this task -- every superseded version stayed "retained" per the issue's own explicit caution, since none was confidently zero-value and all are gitignored (recoverable regardless).

**Task 5 (`_python.nc` investigation)**: opened both files directly with `xarray` rather than guessing from the name. Both carry unambiguous self-describing metadata (`title="Python KPP Port Outputs"`, `source="Python KPP port"`) proving they are Python-port *outputs* mislocated inside `inputs_from_mitgcm/` by an earlier (2026-08-20) version of the pipeline that saved a same-directory `<input>_python.nc` file before the current `outputs_from_python/python_kpp_outputs_<tag>.nc` convention existed -- `NETCDF_DATA_FORMAT.md`'s own step-4 walkthrough still describes exactly this old behavior, now annotated as historical rather than left to imply it is current. **`mitgcm_kpp_inputs_11k_1D_python.nc`**: retained -- `KPP_port_validation/scripts/compute_validation_statistics.py` still opens it directly by name; documented in `KPP_port_validation/CAPTURES.md` as anomalous-but-live, including the fact that it is also mislabeled inside `esx/project.json:external_inputs` as an "MITgcm-derived" input (left as-is; fixing that categorization without touching the file itself was judged not worth a second, unrelated edit to that manifest). **`mitgcm_kpp_inputs_lab_sea_1000_0820T0946_python.nc`**: confirmed safe to delete, unlike the file above -- same mislocated-output pattern, but additionally confirmed *incomplete* (1 of the real capture's 999 timesteps, an abandoned partial snapshot) and, unlike the 11k file, zero references anywhere in `{scripts,tests}`. Deleted the file and removed its now-dangling `esx/project.json:external_inputs` entry in the same patch, so `tools/esx/project.py::environment`'s required-file hash check does not fail on a missing file the manifest still claimed to need.

Updated `docs/OLD_MARKDOWN_NO_LONGER_NEEDED`-adjacent live docs touched by the above: both top-level READMEs' directory listings/guides, `NETCDF_DATA_FORMAT.md`'s directory tree and file-naming section, and the one-line usage-example path in `generate_kpp_validation_report.py`'s own docstring (previously pointed at a location that never actually existed on disk for that file).

### Verification
Full combined suite, fresh, under the ecco environment: `103 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed` -- identical to the pre-existing documented baseline (the 3 skips are the unrelated, already-open 1DMIX-010 `h5py` gap). Unchanged by construction, not merely by observation: no file under `Vertical_Mixing_Models/{main,GGL90,KPP,tests}` or `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests` was touched; every deleted/relocated file was first grep-confirmed to have zero reference from either test tree. Additionally re-ran `KPP_port_validation/scripts/compute_validation_statistics.py` directly (not part of pytest) to confirm the one retained, actively-referenced `_python.nc` file still loads and reproduces its known statistics (mean 0.1843%, median 0.0005%, max at timestep 2312) unchanged. `python3 -c "import json; json.load(open('esx/project.json'))"` confirms the manifest edit kept valid JSON.

### Workflow amendment
Prepared as `harness_change` (directory reorganization, no scheme code touched). One of this issue's own fixes -- the one-line docstring usage-example path correction in `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/generate_kpp_validation_report.py` (part of the same "fix stale doc-example paths this investigation surfaced" cleanup as the `NETCDF_DATA_FORMAT.md`/README edits) -- touches a file under the scanned `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/` `source_paths` root, which this project's own scientific-signature drift check tracks by raw content hash regardless of whether the edit is a comment/docstring or executable logic. This requires amending to `scientific_change` and obtaining independent review before closing -- the same procedural pattern as 1DMIX-025/1DMIX-042. No scheme formula, comparison logic, or test assertion was changed; only a one-line example path in a docstring, alongside the directory reorganization's many non-source_paths deletions/relocations/manifests.

### Independent review
`kind: scientific_change` (amended from `harness_change`), `required_roles: [bob, richard]`, `minimum_reviewers: 1`. Richard (agent `a6459030-7b7e-405f-a1c6-e3b4a490ecda`, event `ebc6a3a60219423081bccc7d543092f6`): **APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`**. Rather than trusting cumulative `git diff` (which mixes in leftover uncommitted 1DMIX-044 work in the same file), isolated the exact issue-scoped delta by diffing the issue's own baseline vs. candidate content-addressed snapshots across the full 344-file documentation inventory: exactly 2 files differ (`generate_kpp_validation_report.py`, `esx/project.json`), confirming no other scanned-root change was missed; extracted pre-image bytes and confirmed each file's entire delta is exactly the one claimed line. Re-ran (not just re-read) 2 of Bob's grep safety claims himself: zero active script/test reference to `MITgcm_wrappers/` confirmed directly; the deleted `_python.nc` file could not be literally reopened (genuinely gone, never git-tracked) but its claimed incompleteness was corroborated indirectly (sibling capture timestep count, zero current references, the retained sibling's independently-verified metadata pattern) -- disclosed as a real, honest limit rather than glossed over. Spot-checked both `CAPTURES.md` manifests against actual current test source (`test_kpp_mitgcm_validation.py`/`_extended.py`, `compute_validation_statistics.py`, `test_ggl90_mitgcm_validation.py`): every "current" filename claim matched exactly. Reran the full combined suite fresh under his own owner id: `103 passed, 3 skipped`, unchanged.
