## RESOLVED: Refreshed 2 stale MITgcm KPP captures via a real Docker rebuild+rerun, eliminating the 1DMIX-035 OUTPUT_MIXING truncation defect from their ground truth

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T11:46:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-27T17:15:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-050
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_kpp_{inputs,outputs}_11k_1D.nc`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_mitgcm/mitgcm_kpp_{inputs,outputs}_lab_sea_6mo.nc`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests/test_kpp_mitgcm_validation_extended.py`

### Issue
Split off from 1DMIX-043. Two MITgcm KPP captures (`1D_ocean_ice_column` 11,000-timestep, `lab_sea` 6-month/4368-timestep) predated 1DMIX-035's `OUTPUT_MIXING` early-truncation fix (2026-09-20) and still carried the defect in their own ground truth: 51.8% and ~4.1-48.9% of timesteps respectively had `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t`/`ghat` incorrectly zero-filled at every real interior level. `1DMIX-043`'s tests correctly mitigated this via a clean-subset filter, but roughly half of the 11k capture's ground truth (and a real fraction of lab_sea's) was unusable for validating mixing-coefficient agreement.

### Resolution
Confirmed first, by direct diff, that both experiments' `code_validation/kpp_calc.F`/`kpp_routines.F` are real, non-stale symlinks to the canonical, already-fixed `kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F` (no stale-mirror bug found this round, unlike `lab_sea`'s own prior 1DMIX-038/039 history). Rebuilt and reran both experiments via Docker (Rosetta host, `arch -arm64` workaround used per this project's own documented precedent), reparsed, and replaced the two stale `.nc` files -- originals backed up intact to `/tmp/1dmix050_backup/` (4 files, ~374 MB combined, not deleted). Measured fresh against the new captures: **0.0% truncated** for both `1D_ocean_ice_column` (0/11,000 timesteps) and `lab_sea_6mo` (0/655,200 wet column-timesteps, measured over the complete run, not just a subsample), matching `seaice_obcs`'s own already-clean precedent exactly.

Widened `test_kpp_mitgcm_validation_extended.py` back to full, unfiltered mixing-coefficient/`ghat` coverage for both experiments (removed the now-unnecessary `_clean_mask`-based filtering from those assertions; retained `_clean_mask` only as the oracle-side evidence for each experiment's own new `..._capture_is_not_truncated` test, mirroring `seaice_obcs`'s existing pattern), and retuned every affected tolerance from fresh measurement against the new captures (caught and fixed a real self-inconsistency in an ad-hoc measurement script that had omitted `ghat`'s active-cell filter, before it could propagate into a wrong tolerance). Updated `docs/code_map.md`'s KPP row and `VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`'s two "Ground-truth caveat" sections to RESOLVED with the fresh numbers, and regenerated both affected PDF reports (1DMIX-044).

### Resolution
**Step 1 (bounded first step, this issue's own explicitly-scoped scope)**: confirmed the build compiles cleanly and `kpp_calc.F`/`kpp_routines.F` for this experiment are byte-identical to the canonical, current `kpp_mods/` copies (not stale). Ran an 8-timestep smoke test confirming the pipeline works end to end (correct 4-tile parse, non-degenerate per-quadrant statistics) and measured real timing to extrapolate the full run's cost: **the actual MITgcm run+parse for 720 timesteps is only ~15 minutes total, not the "hours" this issue's own text anticipated** -- the genuine multi-hour cost is the Python-port replay stage, by analogy to `lab_sea`'s own measured replay time.

## RESOLVED: Permanentized both schemes' standalone-Fortran-driver ("2-way") inputs/outputs and added the missing KPP-side summary report

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T20:35:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-28T04:45:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-053
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/{KPP,GGL90}_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_python_standalone/` (new); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/generate_kpp_scenario_report.py` (new); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/compare_scenario_standalone.py::compare`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/reports/kpp_scenario_standalone_summary.md` (new).

### Issue
This project validates in two directions but durably organized only one. Direction A (MITgcm capture to Python-port replay) has permanent homes under `{GGL90,KPP}_port_validation/{inputs,outputs}_from_mitgcm/`. Direction B -- the port's own 6 idealized scenarios exported, run through the *real* Fortran `KPPMIX`/`GGL90_CALC` standalone drivers, and compared against that Fortran output -- existed only as scratch files under the git-ignored `Vertical_Mixing_Models/output/<scenario>/`, and had already been silently lost and regenerated once. Separately, GGL90's Direction B had a summary report while KPP's equivalent comparison, exercised since 1DMIX-023, had none.

### Resolution and justification
**Storage convention chosen and documented**: per scheme, `{KPP,GGL90}_port_validation/inputs_from_python_standalone/<scenario>/` holds the exported driver input; `outputs_from_python_standalone/<scenario>/` holds both the real Fortran driver output and the Python port's own dense array, since the `.npz` is the Python side of the comparison rather than an input to the driver. 36 files across 6 scenarios were copied (not moved, so the existing scratch workflow still works), verified byte-identical to their sources by sha256 -- independently re-verified by Richard. Measured total 108 MB, correcting Arch's own 66 MB estimate in the dispatch brief. Because these are `.txt`/`.npz` rather than `.nc`, they are not covered by the existing ignore rule, so `.gitignore` gained explicit entries with a comment, giving them exactly Direction A's treatment: durable on disk, not pushed, and declared in `esx/project.json:external_inputs` so their actual bytes are hashed into every verification receipt. `CONVENTIONS_STANDALONE_DATA.md` in each scheme's tree records provenance and how to regenerate.

**A real premise contradiction was found and resolved rather than worked around.** Bob discovered mid-implementation that `compare_scenario_standalone.py::compare` has no return value, unlike its GGL90 sibling -- it only prints. His initial proposal was to capture that stdout. Arch rejected it: the printed values are `.3e`-formatted, so every report figure would enter through a three-significant-digit string round-trip, and the stdout carries only max/mean absolute difference with no median, p95, relative error or mismatch count -- so a stdout-derived report could not be a genuine sibling of GGL90's and the asymmetry this issue exists to close would survive in a new form. Arch instead authorized modifying `compare()` to return a dict of per-field statistics, produced by importing `summarize` from `compare_scenario_ggl90_standalone.py` **unmodified**. Sharing that one function makes both schemes' numbers mean literally the same thing, which is stronger than mirroring a formula by hand. KPP's own field set (`visc_az`, `diff_kz_s`, `diff_kz_t`, `ghat`, `hbl`) is preserved rather than forced into GGL90's names. Arch verified beforehand that no test asserts the old stdout format.

**New report**: `generate_kpp_scenario_report.py` mirrors `generate_ggl90_scenario_report.py`'s structure, consumes `compare()`'s return value, and reads the permanentized data rather than the scratch directory, producing `kpp_scenario_standalone_summary.md`.

**A real, newly-quantified discrepancy was surfaced and stated, not tuned away.** Five of six scenarios (`calm_baseline`, `arctic_convection`, `hurricane_wind`, `tropical_heating_diurnal`, `heavy_rain_freshening`) match the real Fortran `KPPMIX` to floating-point roundoff: worst `max_rel` about 2.3e-15, zero cells above 1% relative error. `combined_storm` does not: 45.0% of `visc_az` cells, 51.3% of `diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t` and 51.2% of `ghat` cells exceed 1% relative error (max_abs up to 6.1e-02 m^2/s), which the prior KPP comparison could never have shown because it computed only max and mean absolute difference. This is the first quantification of that scenario's disagreement beyond `hbl`, and it is split out as its own issue rather than absorbed here.

### Independent review
Richard (agent `c2981bc7-20fa-41dd-8cf0-eeb0290088cf`, event `6f3dbca354854dffa9c0854b1d752e0c`): **APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`**. Given a question deliberately disjoint from the implementation path. He confirmed `compare_scenario_ggl90_standalone.py::summarize` is byte-unmodified against HEAD and that `compare_scenario_standalone.py`'s diff is exactly the described return-value addition and nothing more. He then wrote his own from-scratch numpy parser and statistics script that imports neither `compare()` nor `summarize()`, recomputed `combined_storm`'s `visc_az` and `ghat` full six-statistic tuples from the raw permanentized npz and Fortran text, and reproduced the report table exactly -- including the 9.053e+11 `ghat` `max_rel` outlier, establishing no defect in the new shared-statistic code path. He cell-level-probed both of the report's scientific claims against the real arrays rather than accepting the narrative: the mismatching `visc_az` cells span from the shallow third of the boundary layer down to `hbl` at each timestep rather than clustering at a single depth, which is consistent with the shape-function-versus-`hbl` attribution; and of `ghat`'s 307 mismatching cells only 3 have an exactly-zero Fortran reference, so those 3 alone drive the 9.05e+11 figure through the 1e-12 floor -- a genuine artifact -- while the other 304 carry real 1-1.5% disagreement at physically meaningful magnitudes, which the report already attributes to the same `hbl` mechanism rather than sweeping into the artifact dismissal. That distinction is sharper than the report's own wording and confirms the dismissal is bounded rather than an explaining-away. He also verified all 36 copies byte-identical, confirmed `git ls-files` is empty for the 4 new directories, reran the generator to a scratch path and got byte-identical output modulo timestamp, and reran the full suite himself.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest: `110 passed, 3 skipped` -- unchanged from the pre-issue baseline, confirmed independently by Bob, Arch and Richard. Structural verification: `EXECUTED PASS`. Final scientific verification (`final_verification.py`, required for `scientific_change`): `EXECUTED PASS`. `audit.py` PASS after three bare `path::symbol` references in `docs/code_map.md` were corrected to carry their full directory prefixes -- the same link-checking defect class this project has hit twice before. Remaining bounds: the permanentized data is a snapshot of one export/run cycle, so regenerating the drivers' inputs would supersede rather than extend it; `CONVENTIONS_STANDALONE_DATA.md` records how; the `combined_storm` mechanism is attributed by consistency with the already-characterized Rib/Ricr threshold sensitivity and probed at cell level, not independently re-derived from the Fortran, which is why it is split to its own issue; and the new KPP report still cites internal issue IDs in the same style as its existing GGL90 sibling, so it will need the same citation sweep that 1DMIX-052 applies to the other reports.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them -- all changes remain in the working tree). Bob (agent `434a54dd-b6bc-4e61-a7a1-b9f254454533`) implemented across 3 turns: step 1 landed durably before the first turn hit its 80% per-turn call cap, which is exactly why nothing had to be redone; round 1 completed steps 2-4 but was recorded `incomplete` with a stale orientation because it changed three of the targets its own receipt was taken against; round 2 re-navigated and sealed with no new implementation. Richard reviewed independently in his own session. `kind: scientific_change`, `--budget-kind scientific_change` sized from measured work rather than from workflow kind. Related issues: 1DMIX-023 (the KPP standalone comparison this finally gives a report), 1DMIX-024 and 1DMIX-051 (the GGL90 driver and report this mirrors), 1DMIX-019/1DMIX-022 (the Rib/Ricr sensitivity the `combined_storm` finding is consistent with), 1DMIX-052 (the citation sweep this new report will also need).

## RESOLVED: `combined_storm`'s KPP disagreement is the `wscale` extrapolation clamp, not `hbl` propagation -- hypothesis disproved by experiment

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-28T04:45:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-28T05:40:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-056
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_routines.py::wscale`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_scheme_specific.py::diagnose_bl_depth`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_core_driver.py::KPPDriver.compute_mixing`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/scripts/kpp_hbl_substitution_experiment.py` (new); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests/test_kpp_combined_storm_hbl_substitution.py` (new); `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F:980`.

### Issue
1DMIX-053's new KPP standalone-driver report was the first KPP comparison to compute percentiles and relative error rather than only max and mean absolute difference, and it showed that five of the six idealized scenarios match the real Fortran `KPPMIX` to floating-point roundoff while `combined_storm` disagrees across 45-51% of cells in `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t`/`ghat`. Both that report and `docs/code_map.md` attributed the disagreement to the port's own small `hbl` difference (max_abs 0.32 m against a boundary layer deepening 70 m to 387 m) propagating through KPP's `hbl`-dependent boundary-layer shape function at every interior level. That attribution was a consistency argument, never a measurement.

### Resolution and justification
**The attribution was wrong, and the experiment that disproved it was the one this issue specified.** An optional `hbl_override` was added to `diagnose_bl_depth` and `KPPDriver.compute_mixing`, defaulting to `None` so the production path is untouched, and the port was rerun on `combined_storm` with the Fortran's own `hbl` injected at every timestep. The `hbl` difference went to exactly `0.000e+00` -- and the coefficient disagreement barely moved:

| variant | `hbl` max_abs | `visc_az` cells >1% rel | `visc_az` max_abs |
|---|---|---|---|
| baseline | 3.186e-01 | 270/600 | 3.064e-02 |
| Fortran `hbl` substituted | 0.000e+00 | 261/600 | 3.064e-02 |
| `keep_mitgcm_bugs=True` | 2.8e-14 | 4/600 | 2.674e-03 |

`hbl` is therefore not the dominant mechanism.

**The real mechanism** is `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_routines.py::wscale`'s pre-existing `keep_mitgcm_bugs` switch. The real unmodified Fortran `KPPMIX` leaves the `wscale` lookup-table extrapolation unclamped at `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F:980`, whose Sidorenko correction at `:990` is commented out in the MITgcm source; the Python port clamps it by default. Enabling `keep_mitgcm_bugs`, with no `hbl` override at all, collapses `hbl` agreement to 2.8e-14 m -- matching the other five scenarios -- and drops `visc_az`/`diff_kz_s`/`diff_kz_t` mismatches from 270-308/600 to 2-4/600.

**Scope of the regime, stated precisely after correction.** The clamp-differentiating branch is technically entered by `arctic_convection` (25 of 2350 `wscale` evaluation points) and `hurricane_wind` (277 of 2424) as well. A direct A/B toggle of `keep_mitgcm_bugs` across all six scenarios produces zero measurable change for the five calmer ones and a drastic change only for `combined_storm`: only its excursion is large enough for the clamp-versus-no-clamp difference to change the measured result. An earlier draft claimed `combined_storm` was the only scenario to reach the regime at all, which was literally false and was corrected.

**No production behaviour changed.** `keep_mitgcm_bugs` already existed and remains `False`; `hbl_override` defaults to `None`. Whether the default should change to match real MITgcm is a genuine question this project's exact-correspondence constraint raises, and it is filed separately as 1DMIX-057 rather than decided inside an investigation.

### Independent review
Richard (agent `6b1044dd-f4d1-4720-ae23-3ca550778886`) across 2 rounds, given a question deliberately aimed at the failure modes a disproof-plus-replacement is most prone to. Round 0 returned **APPROVE_WITH_FIXES** with one must_fix. He confirmed the substituted variant's exact-zero `hbl` difference was by construction rather than an accidental self-comparison, by checking the baseline and substituted runs genuinely differ in their inputs and that the surviving 261/600 mismatches are real. He read `pkg/kpp/kpp_routines.F` in the real MITgcm checkout himself and confirmed the `:980` active / `:990` commented-out citation exactly. He then instrumented the real `wscale()` across **all six** scenarios rather than only `combined_storm`, which is what produced the must_fix: the "only scenario to reach that regime" claim was false, and he supplied the measured point counts. He verified the additive-only nature of the override by structural diff, reran the full suite himself at `113 passed, 3 skipped` rather than trusting the reported count, and **deliberately broke both mechanisms in a scratch copy to confirm each witness test actually fails** -- establishing the witness is a real regression guard, not a tautology. Round 1 returned **APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`**, confirming the corrected wording across all three documents, that `docs/model_contract.md` was correctly left untouched because its own wording was already accurate, and that the four documents are mutually consistent.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest: `113 passed, 3 skipped` -- the pre-issue baseline of 110 plus exactly the 3 new witness tests, run independently by both Bob and Richard. Structural verification: `EXECUTED PASS`. Final scientific verification (`final_verification.py`, required for `scientific_change`): `EXECUTED PASS`. Remaining bounds: the 2-4/600 residual under `keep_mitgcm_bugs=True`, confined to the two deepest cells at the two timesteps where `hbl` bottoms out at the full column depth, is quantified but not root-caused; the GGL90-side and other-scenario implications of the `wscale` finding were not examined; and the port's default still differs from real MITgcm in this regime, which is 1DMIX-057's question.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them -- all changes remain in the working tree). Bob (agent `ceef8ab6-f3a0-4852-adbd-0cc6615c2316`) across 3 rounds: the experiment and root-cause, then the two documentation corrections he had himself flagged as budget-limited, then Richard's must_fix. Richard across 2 rounds in his own session. `kind: scientific_change`, `--budget-kind scientific_change` sized from measured work. Related issues: 1DMIX-053 (the report whose new statistics surfaced this), 1DMIX-019/1DMIX-022/1DMIX-023 (the Rib/Ricr sensitivity this finding displaces as the explanation), 1DMIX-057 (the default-value question this raises).

## RESOLVED: Three inherited `ghat` residual attributions replaced with per-capture measurements — the mechanism differs between captures

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-28T09:10:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-28T11:05:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-060
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/tests/test_kpp_mitgcm_validation_extended.py::test_11k_ocean_ice_column_ghat`; `::test_lab_sea_6mo_ghat`; `::test_seaice_obcs_ghat`; `::test_global_oce_latlon_ghat`.

### Issue
Three `ghat` tests attributed their residual disagreement with real MITgcm to "the same hbl-misdiagnosis tail". None of the three attributions had ever been measured -- each was inherited from a neighbouring test -- and the same claim had already been checked and disproved three times: 1DMIX-056 found `combined_storm`'s disagreement was the `wscale` extrapolation clamp rather than `hbl` propagation, 1DMIX-057 corrected the identical mis-attribution in the `global_oce_latlon` docstring, and 1DMIX-058 found that at `global_oce_latlon`'s worst `ghat` cell the Python `hbl` matches MITgcm's exactly. A wrong mechanism recorded as fact in a test had by then cost three separate iterations a correction round.

### Resolution and justification
Each capture was measured independently, and **the answer is capture-specific** -- which is why a single borrowed explanation was wrong regardless of which way it pointed. All three captures were first confirmed to carry `use_ghat=1`, ruling out 1DMIX-058's compute-versus-apply defect a priori.

**`11k_1D` -- attribution confirmed.** The worst cell carries a real 9.55 m `hbl` disagreement, and Pearson correlation between `|hbl diff|` and `|ghat diff|` is 0.73 across all 13,391 active cells, rising to 0.81 when the 13 exact-zero cells are excluded -- those being the tail's most extreme members rather than a separate mechanism. `bfsfc` sits on the same branch as MITgcm at the worst zero cell, so this is not 1DMIX-058's mechanism.

**`lab_sea_6mo` -- confirmed but refined.** `hbl` drives the residual by two distinct routes: five genuinely large misses of 17-22 m, and a 42-cell cluster of roughly 0.25 m offsets at column `(i=13,j=1)`, all at `z=1`, timesteps 58-99. Correlation is 0.22 overall and 0.48 excluding the zero cells. Two of the 54 zero-signature cells fit neither route and are recorded as unclassified. The measurement is bounded by the test's own first-100-of-4368-timestep subsample, and the cluster runs to the last timestep of that window, so it may continue unseen.

**`seaice_obcs` -- attribution disproved.** The cells carrying the largest disagreement have `hbl` differences of only 0.01-0.02 m while MITgcm's `ghat` is near 99, and correlation is 0.18 overall. At the single worst cell neither the `hbl` tail nor a `bfsfc` branch flip is present, and the deeper trigger inside `compute_bl_mixing`'s shape-function evaluation that returns exactly 0.0 despite `kbl` placing the cell inside the boundary layer is **not** root-caused. The docstring states that ignorance plainly rather than substituting a third guess.

No tolerance was widened and no bound was touched: Richard diffed the file against the exact pre-issue candidate blob rather than against HEAD and confirmed the only changes are the four docstrings.

### Independent review
Richard (agent `12933cb0-81b4-40cc-90b8-0ceebaea4a1c`) across 3 rounds. He deliberately **varied the oracle** rather than recomputing the same statistic -- Spearman rank correlation, leave-out-top-k Pearson sensitivity, rank overlap and per-column zero-signature grouping -- after reading and then executing Bob's probe script under his own identity rather than trusting its reported output. He reproduced every headline figure exactly. His round-0 review returned **APPROVE_WITH_FIXES** with two must-fixes, both the same defect this issue exists to eliminate, one level down: a claim measured at one cell and stated over a group. First, `seaice_obcs`'s ruling-out had been measured only at the worst cell but written as covering the whole 12-cell zero-signature group; he found cell `(t=4,i=2,j=4,z=1)` in that same group with a 13.14 m `hbl` disagreement on the identical `bfsfc` branch, so that cell **is** `hbl`-driven and the group is heterogeneous. Second, the `lab_sea_6mo` cluster count was 42 rather than 49, its timestep range ran to the subsample's last step, and two zero-signature cells fit neither stated route. Both were corrected and his round-2 review returned **APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`**.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Full pytest: `120 passed, 3 skipped`, unchanged -- this iteration changed only docstrings. Structural verification: `EXECUTED PASS`. Final scientific verification (`final_verification.py`): `EXECUTED PASS`. Remaining bounds: `lab_sea_6mo`'s conclusion is bounded by the test's own 100-timestep subsample and its cluster may extend beyond that window; `seaice_obcs`'s worst-cell trigger is explicitly unestablished; and only the worst cell of each capture was characterized in depth, with group-level heterogeneity now stated rather than assumed away.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them -- all changes remain in the working tree). Bob (agent `e14b8abf-cc21-4367-88db-521b0af97c41`) measured and corrected across 2 rounds. Richard reviewed across 3 rounds in his own session; his rounds 0 and 1 are recorded incomplete, round 1 solely because its footer cited a navigate receipt instead of the sealed documentation report, which round 2 corrected with no change to the verdict. `kind: scientific_change`, `--budget-kind scientific_change` sized from measured work. Related issues: 1DMIX-056, 1DMIX-057 and 1DMIX-058, the three prior disproofs of this attribution that motivated the issue.

## RESOLVED: `VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` split into two standalone per-scheme reports, cleaned of issue-tracking archaeology in the same pass

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-27T21:20:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-28T15:20:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-055
**Anchors**: `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/KPP_port_validation/KPP_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` (new); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/GGL90_port_validation/GGL90_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` (new); `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/VALIDATION_RESULTS.md`; `MITgcm_to_Python_port_verification/README.md`.

### Issue
KPP and GGL90 are separate, independently-implemented ports of two unrelated MITgcm packages, yet one 781-line document covered both, so a reader interested in one port had to filter out the other. Worse, genuinely shared content was attached to whichever scheme's section happened to need it first: the EOS pressure-conversion material sat under `## GGL90` while discussing a KPP result and pointing at "the KPP section above", a structure that would break or duplicate awkwardly under a naive split. Separately, the document carried 56 citation-bearing lines of internal issue IDs and before/after narrative, which 1DMIX-052 required removed.

### Resolution and justification
Split into two standalone reports, cleaned in the same pass because 1DMIX-055's own text permitted it and doing the cleanup separately would have rewritten the same prose twice: `KPP_port_validation/KPP_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` (614 lines) and `GGL90_port_validation/GGL90_VALIDATION_RESULTS.md` (452 lines). Each is self-contained with per-experiment results, a causal mechanism for every real discrepancy, a physical and methodological limitations section, and **its own** shared-infrastructure section written in that scheme's framing — the stated convention, so no cross-file "see the X section" reference survives in either direction. The original file was reduced to a 16-line pointer rather than deleted, because six non-archive documents link to it and deleting it would break instruction-link checking. `README.md` went from 260 to 96 lines, keeping the directory guide, the runnable pipeline commands and a compact status table. All four files return `0` for `grep -c '1DMIX-'`. Newer measurements from this session superseded the stale prose throughout rather than being copied forward.

The work was delivered in three short dispatches (one per output document) at $4.39, $5.00 and $7.99 — deliberately short, because a prior single-turn attempt on this same rewrite lost its whole allocation to a terminal-write shape, and because a $12 per-turn expectation had already stranded another issue.

### Independent review
Richard (agent `b8dee8e9-5d32-4da0-9d7c-4de27d4493b4`) across 2 rounds, reviewing cold with no sight of the implementer's turn. He confirmed the structural acceptance in full — line counts, zero citations in all four files, zero literal cross-file references, each report's shared-infrastructure section self-framed, and all six documents citing the old path resolving with none stranded. He then found **three substantive content defects** that no self-check would have caught, because they concern whether the content is *true* rather than whether the split was performed:

1. **A dangling, unsourceable claim.** The KPP report stated a result was "confirmed on the `vermix` capture" — but `vermix` has **zero KPP files anywhere in the repository**; every `vermix` artifact is GGL90-only. Git history showed the sentence pre-existed under `## GGL90` and had been *relocated* into the KPP shared-infrastructure section without acquiring a KPP source. That is exactly the cross-scheme entanglement this issue existed to resolve, carried forward rather than fixed. Replaced with a properly sourced `1D_ocean_ice_column` confirmation (`hbl` median_abs 1.18e-4 → 8.65e-5 m, `visc_az` 9.46e-6 → 8.36e-6), which Richard verified matches `closed_issues.md:949` verbatim and which is a real, on-disk KPP capture.
2. **A wrong sample-size range.** "2,400–5,750 per scenario per field" against actual `n_total` values of `{24, 48, 50, 1150, 1200, 2400}`; corrected to 24–2,400, which Richard re-derived independently by script in both rounds.
3. **Figures from a superseded build presented as current.** The `lab_sea` 6-month full-655,200-sample `hbl` figures trace to an instrumentation build predating this experiment's tau-averaging and truncation fixes, so they do not describe the capture on disk. Now captioned explicitly as historical and not a current measurement.

Round 1 returned **APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`** after checking each correction against its claimed source rather than against the implementer's report.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Structural verification: `EXECUTED PASS`. Final scientific verification (`final_verification.py`): `EXECUTED PASS`. The full pytest suite was deliberately not re-run for this issue and is not claimed: no Python source changed, only Markdown. Remaining bounds: the `lab_sea` 6-month historical figures are retained rather than re-measured against the current capture, which would need a fresh replay; and the two reports cite internal issue IDs nowhere, so a reader wanting provenance for a specific number is directed to the root ledgers rather than to an inline citation.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree). Delivered across three iterations. Iteration 1 produced the three documents; iteration 2 was spent discovering that closing the issue required dispositioning 18 targets of which all 18 were an unrelated ESX framework upgrade, which led to ESX 1.3.0's `upgrade_supplied` attribution and reduced this issue's judgment burden to a single target; iteration 3 obtained the review. One implementer turn was killed by an operator keystroke after its edits reached disk but before its report, and was recovered by verifying the tree and resealing rather than redoing the work. Related issues: 1DMIX-052 (whose acceptance this pass also satisfies on disk), 1DMIX-045 (the combined report this replaces), TEAM-UPGRADE-DIFF-001 (the framework defect this closure exposed).

## RESOLVED: `use_doublediff` now raises instead of being silently ignored, and the omission is recorded in the model contract

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-28T09:10:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-28T16:05:00Z
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-059
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_parameters.py::KPPParameters.__post_init__`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/KPP/kpp_routines.py::ri_iwmix`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_doublediff_guard.py` (new); `pkg/kpp/kpp_calc.F:533-545`.

### Issue
`KPP_DOUBLEDIFF` is a real MITgcm option, on by default in `pkg/kpp/KPP_OPTIONS.h`, adding salt-fingering and diffusive-convection mixing to the interior scheme. The Python port declared `exclude_doublediff` and `use_doublediff` but implemented none of that physics, and unlike `allow_shelfice` and `use_sw_frac_3d` these two carried no `__post_init__` guard. A user requesting double diffusion was therefore silently ignored, in a project whose stated constraint is exact MITgcm correspondence.

### Resolution and justification
`__post_init__` now appends `use_doublediff` to the existing `unimplemented` list that raises `NotImplementedError`, following the `allow_shelfice` idiom. `exclude_doublediff` is deliberately left unguarded: it is the compile-time exclusion, which the port trivially satisfies. `docs/model_contract.md` records double diffusion as a known unported option with its physical consequence, `kpp_default_parameters.yaml`'s comment matches the guarded meaning, and the capture-parsing and replay paths record `KPPuseDoubleDiff` so a future capture enabling it is detected rather than replayed blind against a port that omits the physics.

`Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_doublediff_guard.py` is the registered witness. It was shown to be a real guard rather than a tautology by temporarily disabling the guard, whereupon 2 of its 4 tests failed with DID NOT RAISE while the `exclude_doublediff` and default cases correctly kept passing.

### Independent review
Richard (agent `bf2a89f9-0df4-4e0f-b1a2-bacfdc766dc5`): **APPROVE, `must_fix=[]`**, reviewing cold across two distinct concerns.

He tested the flag-choice judgment against real MITgcm source rather than accepting it: `kpp_mods/kpp_calc.F:533-545` shows `KPP_DOUBLEDIFF` is invoked only inside `#ifndef EXCLUDE_KPP_DOUBLEDIFF` **and** `IF(KPPuseDoubleDiff)`, so `exclude_doublediff` can never independently change behaviour; and all 8 captured NetCDF inputs across 4 experiments carry `exclude_doublediff=0`/`KPPuseDoubleDiff=0`, matching stock MITgcm's own default. Guarding the other flag would have been moot at best and would have rejected every existing configuration at worst. He confirmed the guard's premise by reading `ri_iwmix` (`kpp_routines.py:215-391`) end to end plus a repo-wide grep for `doublediff|Rrho0|dsfmax`, finding zero code-path hits outside declarations, tests and docs. He broke the guard in a scratch copy and confirmed the witness fails. He independently grepped all four consumer scripts and confirmed non-breakage exactly.

He also performed the **first-ever review of 1DMIX-054's 18 Fortran build-input files** across the two new `*_code_validation/` directories, which had sat in the tree unreviewed because 1DMIX-054 closed partial without a sealed report. He confirmed `kpp_calc.F`, `kpp_routines.F` and `ggl90_calc.F` are real symlinks byte-identical to the canonical `kpp_mods/` and `ggl90_mods/` copies rather than stale forks; `SIZE.h` byte-identical to each host with grid literals matching the experiment names exactly (90x40x15 Nr=15; 20x16 Nr=23); every other shared header diffing clean against its host; and each `packages.conf` change being exactly the one documented line.

### Verification and remaining bounds
Structural verification: `EXECUTED PASS`. Final scientific verification (`final_verification.py`, required for `scientific_change`): `EXECUTED PASS`. The witness passes and fails when the guard is removed. Remaining bounds: the physics itself is still unported, which is the point — this issue makes the omission loud rather than removing it; no capture in this project enables `KPPuseDoubleDiff`, so the gap remains dormant and the guard is untested against a real double-diffusive capture; and 1DMIX-054's build directories are now reviewed but still uncompiled and uncaptured, which is 1DMIX-054's own remaining scope.

### Traceability
Not committed this turn (no commit authorization; per project policy, commits only happen when the owner explicitly requests them — all changes remain in the working tree). Delivered across two iterations: iteration 1 implemented and self-verified the guard but was under-allocated as `harness_change` so the reviewer was never dispatchable; iteration 2 obtained the review after ESX 1.3.0 and 1.4.0 removed the artificial disposition burden (31 of 64 targets auto-attributed to a recorded upgrade or carried forward from another accepted closeout). Related issues: 1DMIX-058 (whose sibling-flag audit found this gap), 1DMIX-054 (whose 18 build-input files this closeout reviewed for the first time), TEAM-UPGRADE-DIFF-001 and TEAM-BASELINE-PIN-001 (the framework defects this closure exposed and which ESX 1.3.0/1.4.0 address).

The unreferenced, unreproducible legacy mitgcm_kpp_inputs_1D_10.nc was removed from external_inputs. ## 🟢 RESOLVED: neither momentum-boundary witness exercises the real adapter-to-driver pipeline, so a leak injected in `KPPAdapter.compute_mixing` escapes the entire suite

**Date Identified**: 2026-09-29T01:55:00Z
**Date Resolved**: 2026-09-30T07:17:58.456653+00:00
**Status**: Resolved
**UUID**: 1DMIX-064
**Anchors**: `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/mixing_adapter.py::KPPAdapter.compute_mixing`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_ghat_gate.py::test_momentum_invariant_to_ghat_value`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/tests/test_kpp_ghat_gate.py::test_momentum_solve_never_receives_ghat`; `Vertical_Mixing_Models/main/unified_driver.py::UnifiedColumnDriver._apply_vertical_diffusion`

### Issue or research question
Third generation of one thread, each layer found by the review of the previous fix. 1DMIX-058's review found the momentum boundary of KPP's `ghat` unguarded; 1DMIX-061 added a call-level witness; its review found that witness guarded only the `ghat=`-keyword route; 1DMIX-063 added a quantity-level guard asserting momentum is bit-identical when only `ghat` varies, which does catch every leak implemented **inside** `_apply_vertical_diffusion`. This issue is the next layer: both witnesses hand-build a `MixingOutput` and call `_apply_vertical_diffusion` directly, so neither exercises the real path from `KPPAdapter.compute_mixing` into the driver. A leak injected upstream of the driver body is therefore invisible to both, and to everything else.

### Evidence
Unblocked 2026-09-30: 1DMIX-065 restored every external_inputs capture, so verification evidence can be produced again. The implementation candidate (Bob, 2026-09-29) is still uncommitted in the working tree and awaits independent review.

The 1DMIX-063 reviewer built a fourth route in a scratch copy (since deleted): contaminating `MixingOutput.visc_az` with a `ghat`-dependent term inside `KPPAdapter.compute_mixing`, upstream of `_apply_vertical_diffusion`'s body. It genuinely contaminates the viscosity — `visc_az` moves from `0.0` to `5.837e-3` under nonzero `ghat` — yet it escapes `test_momentum_solve_never_receives_ghat`, escapes `test_momentum_invariant_to_ghat_value`, and escapes the **entire 69-test focused suite**, which reports `69 passed, 3 skipped`, unchanged from clean bytes. The mechanism is structural rather than a tolerance problem: both witnesses construct `MixingOutput` themselves, so no test ever observes what the real adapter hands the driver.

### Scientific or engineering impact
Latent, and narrower than it first appears: the invariant holds on current bytes, and the adapter has no reason to touch `ghat` at all, so this is a coverage boundary rather than a live defect. But it is the boundary that matters most for the next edit, because it is the one place a `ghat`-to-momentum leak could be introduced while every existing guard reports success — and this project now has three consecutive demonstrations that a guard believed sufficient was narrower than it looked. The general lesson is worth recording alongside the specific gap: a hand-built witness guards the function it calls, not the pipeline that calls it.

### Proposed action and acceptance
Decide first whether the right answer is another witness or an end-to-end one. A guard that drives a real scenario through `KPPAdapter.compute_mixing` into `_apply_vertical_diffusion` and asserts the same momentum-invariance property would close this layer and would be the first test in this thread to exercise the real pipeline; that is preferable to a fourth hand-built witness, which would only move the boundary again. Consider also whether the property belongs in the adapter's own contract — that `compute_mixing` never returns a `visc_az` depending on `ghat` — which is checkable without a driver at all. Acceptance: whichever guard is chosen fails under the reviewer's adapter-contamination mutant, rebuilt and demonstrated rather than asserted; the three previously demonstrated routes remain caught; no tolerance is widened; and `docs/model_contract.md`'s coverage statement is updated to say which witness covers which boundary, including any boundary still uncovered, so the record does not overstate the guarantee again.

### Gate acceptance

Accepted by `loop_gate.py --check-done` at 2026-09-30T07:17:58.456653+00:00 for iteration 2026-09-30T06:55:55.612667+00:00. Added and strengthened test_real_pipeline_momentum_invariant_to_ghat, the first ghat-momentum witness that drives the real KPPAdapter.compute_mixing -> UnifiedColumnDriver._apply_vertical_diffusion path (ghat varied at the KPPDriver->adapter boundary; exact visc_az and u_vel/v_vel invariance across x4, x0, interior, all-faces and signed variants, with non-vacuity asserts). Richard's round-0 adversarial REJECT showed face-location, sign and threshold leaks on the documented routes escaping the original three variants; round 1 added the all-faces and signed variants, which catch all eight, and rewrote docs/model_contract.md's coverage map and still-uncovered list to claim exactly what the tests catch. Richard round 1: APPROVE_WITH_FIXES, empty must_fix (optional face-0 sign/magnitude variant filed as a follow-up). No model source changed. Final verification EXECUTED PASS.
